## I / 第一章

===Original===
If it had been possible for Theodore Savage to place on record for those who came after him the story of his life and experiences, he would have been the first to admit that the interest of the record lay in circumstance and not in himself. From beginning to end he was much what surroundings made of him; in his youth the product of a public school, Wadham and the Civil Service; in maturity and age a toiler with his hands in the company of men who lived brutishly. In his twenties, no doubt, he was frequently bored by his clerking duties and the routine of the Distribution Office; later on there were seasons when all that was best in him cried out against confinement in a life that had no aspiration; but neither boredom nor resentment ever drove him to revolt or set him to the moulding of circumstance. If he was destined to live as a local tradition and superman of legend, the honour was not gained by his talents or personal achievements; he had to thank for it an excellent constitution, bequeathed him by his parents, certain traces of refinement in manner and speech and the fears of very ignorant men.

===Chinese===
若有可能将生平经历记录下来传诸后世，西奥多·萨维奇（Theodore Savage）恐怕会第一个承认，这记录的趣味在于境遇而非他本人。自始至终，他多半都是环境塑造的产物：年轻时是公学、瓦德汉学院（Wadham）和文官体系的造物；中年以后则沦为用双手劳作、与粗野之人为伍的苦力。二十多岁时，他无疑时常厌倦配给局（Distribution Office）的文书差事和例行公事；后来某些年月里，他身上所有最优秀的部分都呐喊着抗议这种毫无志向的生活囚笼；但无论厌倦还是怨恨，都从未驱使他去反抗，或着手改造周遭的境况。倘若他注定要作为地方传说和超级英雄载入史册，这份荣耀并非来自他的才华或个人成就；他得感谢的是父母遗传的强健体魄、举止言谈中残留的几分文雅，以及愚昧之辈的恐惧。

===Original===
When the Distribution Office—like his Hepplewhite furniture, his colour-prints and his English glass—was with yesterday's seven thousand years, it is more than possible that Theodore Savage, looking back on his youth, saw existence, till he neared the age of thirty, as a stream of scarcely ruffled content. Sitting crouched to the fire in the sweat-laden air of his cabin or humped idly on a hillside in the dusk of summer evening, it may well have seemed, when his thoughts strayed backwards, that the young man who once was impossibly himself was a being whom care did not touch. What he saw with the eye of his mind and memory was a neat young Mr. Savage who was valeted in comfortable chambers and who worked, without urgence, for limited hours, in a room that looked on Whitehall. Who in his plentiful leisure gained a minor reputation on the golf-links! Who frequented studios, bought—now and then—a picture and collected English glass and bits of furniture. Who was passably good-looking, in an ordinary way, had a thoughtful taste in socks and ties and was careful of his hands as a woman. ... So—through the vista of years and the veil of contrast—Theodore may have seen his young manhood; and in time, perhaps, it was difficult for a coarse-fingered labourer, dependent for his bread on the moods of nature, to sympathize greatly with the troubles of neat Mr. Savage or think of him as subject to the major afflictions of humanity.

===Chinese===
当配给局——如同他的赫普怀特家具、彩印版画和英国玻璃器皿——与昨日的七千年一同湮没之后，西奥多·萨维奇回望青春时，极有可能觉得三十岁之前的人生不过是几乎无波无澜的安逸之流。蜷缩在小屋汗气弥漫的炉火旁，或在夏日黄昏的山坡上慵懒地弓背而坐，思绪飘回往事时，那个曾经不可思议的年轻时的自己，或许显得像个无忧无虑的存在。他以心灵之眼回忆起的是一个整洁利落的年轻萨维奇先生，住在舒适的公寓里有人伺候，在俯瞰白厅的房间里不紧不慢地工作几小时。闲暇充裕时还在高尔夫球场上博得些许名声！常去画室，偶尔买一幅画，收集英国玻璃器皿和家具碎片。相貌尚可，中人之姿，对袜子和领带的品味颇为考究，双手保养得像女人一样细致。……于是——穿越岁月的纵深和对比的帷幕——西奥多或许就这样审视着自己的青年时代；久而久之，一个指尖粗糙、靠天吃饭的粗人，大概很难对整洁的萨维奇先生的烦恼感同身受，也难以想象他竟会遭受人间的重大苦难。

===Original===
All the same, he would spend long hours in communion with his vanished self; striving at times to trace resemblances between the bearded, roughened features that a fishing-pool reflected and the smooth-chinned civil servant with brushed hair and white collar whom he followed in thought through his work, his amusements, his lovemaking and the trivial details of existence. ... And imagining, sometimes, the years and the happenings that might have been if his age, like his youth, had been soaped and collared, routined by his breeding and his office; if gods and men had not run amuck in frenzy and his sons had been born of a woman who lived delicately—playing Chopin of an evening to young Mr. Savage and giving him cream in his tea? ...

===Chinese===
尽管如此，他仍会花上长长时间与消逝的自我神交；时常试图辨认钓鱼池中映出的那张胡子拉碴、粗糙沧桑的面孔，与他跟随思绪穿越其工作、娱乐、恋爱和生活琐碎的那个下巴光洁、头发梳得一丝不苟、系着白领子的文官之间，究竟有几分相似。……偶尔，他还会想象——倘若他的壮年也如青年时代那般被香皂和硬领打理得体、被教养和公职规范得井然有序；倘若神明与世人未曾癫狂失控，倘若他的儿子们由一位精致生活的女子所生——夜晚为年轻的萨维奇先生弹奏肖邦，茶里给他加奶油——那些本可能发生的故事和年岁？

---

===Original===
Even if life in his Civil Service days was not all that it shone through the years of contrast, Theodore Savage could have had very little of hardship to complain of in the days when he added to a certain amount of private income a salary earned by the duties of the unexacting billet which a family interest had secured for him. If he had no particular vocation for the bureaucratic life—if good painting delighted, and official documents bored him—he had sufficient common sense to understand that it is given to most of us, with sufficient application, to master the intricacies of official documents, while only to few is it given to master an art. After a phase of abortive experiment in his college days he had realized—fortunately—that his swift and instinctive pleasure in beauty had in it no creative element; whereupon he settled down, early and easily, into the life and habits of the amateur. ... There remained with him to the end of his days an impression of a young man living pleasurably, somewhat fastidiously; pursuing his hobbies, indulging his tastes, on the whole without much damage to himself or to others affected; acting decently according to his code and, when he fell in love and out of it, falling not too grossly or disastrously. If he had a grievance against his work at the Distribution Office, it was no more serious than this: it took much time, certain hours every days, from the interests that counted in his life. And against that grievance, no doubt, he set the ameliorating fact that his private means unaided would hardly have supported his way of existence, his many pleasant interests and himself; it was his civil servant's salary that had furnished his rooms in accordance with his taste and made possible the purchase of his treasured Fragonard and his bell-toned Georgian wineglasses. ... The bearded toiler, through a mist of years, watched a young man dawdling, without fear of the future, through a world of daily comforts that to his sons would seem fantastic, the creation of legend or of dream.

===Chinese===
即便他在文官生涯中的生活并不像在对比岁月里所显现的那般光鲜，西奥多·萨维奇在那段日子——以家族关系谋得的闲职薪俸加上一定的私人收入——也几乎没有什么困苦可抱怨。如果说他对官僚生涯并无特别志趣——如果说精美的画作令他愉悦而公文使他厌烦——他至少有足够的常识明白：多数人只要肯下功夫，便能掌握公文的繁复，而只有少数人才能精通艺术。在大学阶段一番无果的尝试之后，他——幸好——意识到自己对美的那种迅疾而本能的愉悦并不包含创造的成分；于是他早早地、轻松地安顿下来，过上了业余爱好者的典型生活。……直到生命尽头，他给人的印象始终是一个生活愉快、略带挑剔的年轻人；追求自己的爱好，满足自己的品味，总体上对自己和他人并无太大损害；依照自己的准则行事得体，恋爱与失恋时也不至于太粗俗或太惨烈。如果他对配给局的工作有什么不满，也不过是如此：那份工作占用了他生活中许多重要的兴趣所在的时间和精力。不过毫无疑问，他也会想到一个宽慰的事实：单靠私人收入，他根本无法维持这种生活方式、这些众多的愉悦爱好以及他本人；正是文官的薪水让他得以按自己的品味布置房间，购买珍藏的弗拉戈纳尔（Fragonard）画作和音色清亮的乔治王朝酒杯。……透过岁月的迷雾，那个胡子拉碴的劳力者注视着一个年轻人无所畏惧地穿行于日常安逸的世界——对他的儿子们而言，这个世界将是神话般的奇想或梦境的创造。

---

===Original===
It was that blind and happy lack of all fear of the future that lent interest to the toiler's watching; knowing what he knew of the years that lay ahead, there was something of grim and dramatic humour in the sight of himself—yea, Theodore Savage, the broken-nailed, unshorn—arrayed of a morning in a flowered silk dressing-gown or shirt-fronted for an evening at the opera. ... As it was in the beginning, is now and ever shall be—that, so it seemed to him in later years, had been the real, if unspoken, motto of the world wherein he had his being in the days of his unruffled content. ...

===Chinese===
正是那对未来全然无所畏惧的盲目而幸福的无知，才使劳力者的注视有了趣味；深知未来岁月的真相，眼见自己——对，西奥多·萨维奇，指甲断裂、蓬头垢面的西奥多·萨维奇——晨起穿着花纹丝绸晨衣，或晚间衬衫前襟挺括地出席歌剧，其中自有一种冷峻而戏剧性的幽默。……太初如是，今亦如是，且永将如是——在他后来的岁月里，这似乎就是那个曾经安享无波无澜的太平岁月的世界所奉行的、虽未宣之于口却真实存在的箴言。

---

===Original===
Of the last few weeks in the world that was and ever should be he recalled, on the whole, very little of great hurrying and public events; it was the personal, intimate scenes that stood out and remained to a line and a detail. His first meeting with Phillida Rathbone, for instance, and the chance interview with her father that led to it: he could see himself standing by Rathbone's desk in the Distribution Office, see the bowl between his fingers, held to the light—see its very shape and conventional pattern of raised flowers.

===Chinese===
关于那个本应永存的世界的最后几周，他回忆中总体上鲜有惊天动地的公共事件；凸显出来并铭刻在心的是那些私密的个人场景。比如，他与菲莉达·拉思伯恩（Phillida Rathbone）的初次相遇，以及由此引发的与她父亲的偶然会面：他可以看见自己站在配给局拉思伯恩的办公桌旁，看见那个碗举在灯光下——看见它的形状和凸起花卉的程式化图案。

===Original===
Rathbone—John Rathbone—was his chief in his Distribution days; a square-jawed, formidable, permanent official who was held in awe by underlings and Ministers, and himself was subject, most contentedly subject, to a daughter, the ruler of his household. Her taste in art and decoration was not her father's, but, for all the bewilderment it caused him, he strove to gratify it loyally; and for Phillida's twenty-third birthday he had chosen expensively, on his way to the office, at the shop of a dealer in antiquities. Swept on the spate of the dealer's eloquence he had been pleased for the moment with his find—a flowered bowl, reputed Chelsea; it was not until half an hour later that he remembered uneasily his daughter's firm warnings against unaided traffic with the miscreants who deal in curios. With the memory uncomfortable doubts assailed him, while previous experiments came thronging unpleasantly to mind—the fiasco of the so-called Bartolozzi print and the equally lamentable business of the so-called Chippendale settee. ... He drew his purchase from its paper wrapping, set it down on the table and stared at it. The process brought no enlightenment and he was still wrestling with uncomfortable doubts when Theodore Savage knocked and came in with a draft report for approval.

===Chinese===
拉思伯恩（John Rathbone）——在配给局的岁月里，是他的顶头上司；方下巴、令人生畏的常任官员，下属和大臣们对他敬畏有加，而他自己则心甘情愿地、极为顺从地受制于一个女儿——他家中的主宰。她对艺术和装饰的品味与其父迥异，但尽管这让他困惑，他仍竭力忠实地满足女儿的喜好；为了给菲莉达庆贺二十三岁生日，他上班途中在一家古董店花了大价钱挑选礼物。在店主滔滔不绝的推销攻势下，他一时对所选之物颇为满意——一只花卉纹碗，据说是切尔西（Chelsea）瓷器；直到半小时后，他才不安地想起女儿一再告诫他不要单独和那些古董贩子做买卖。记忆带来了令人不安的疑虑，先前的教训也一股脑涌上心头——那幅所谓的巴托洛齐（Bartolozzi）版画的惨败，以及同样令人惋惜的那张所谓的奇彭代尔（Chippendale）长椅事件。……他从纸包装中取出买下的东西，放在桌上盯着看。这个过程并未带来任何启示，他仍在与不安的疑虑搏斗，这时西奥多·萨维奇敲门进来，拿着一份报告草案请他审阅。

===Original===
The worry born of ignorance faded out of Rathbone's face as he conned the document and amended its clauses with swift pencilled notes in the margin; he was back with the solidities he knew and could make sense of, and superfluous gimcracks for the moment had ceased to exist. It was Savage who unwittingly recalled their existence and importance; when his chief, at the end of his corrections, looked up, the younger man was eyeing the troublesome gimcrack with a meditative interest that reminded Rathbone of his daughter's manner when she contemplated similar rubbish.

===Chinese===
拉思伯恩审读文件、用铅笔在页边迅速批注修改条款时，因无知而生的忧虑逐渐从他脸上消退；他又回到了那些他熟悉且能理解的实在事物中，那些多余的廉价小玩意儿此刻暂时不复存在。是萨维奇无意中唤醒了它们的存在与重要性；当上司批阅完毕抬起头时，年轻人正以一种沉思的目光端详着那只惹麻烦的廉价小玩意儿，这让拉思伯恩想起了女儿审视类似垃圾时的神情。

===Original===
"Know anything about old china?" he inquired—an outward and somewhat excessive indifference concealing an inward anxiety.
"Not much," said Theodore modestly; but, taking the query as request for an opinion, his hand went out to the bowl.
"What do you make of it?" asked Rathbone, still blatantly indifferent. "I picked it up this morning—for my daughter. Supposed to be Chelsea—should you say it was?"

===Chinese===
"你懂古瓷器吗？"他问道——表面上的、甚至略显做作的冷漠掩盖着内心的焦虑。
"不太懂，"西奥多谦虚地说；但他把这询问当作征求意见，伸手去拿那只碗。
"你觉得怎么样？"拉思伯恩问道，仍然一副明目张胆的无所谓样子。"我今天早上买的——送我女儿的。据说出自切尔西——你觉得呢？"

===Original===
If the answer had been in the negative the private acquaintance between chief and subordinate would probably have made no further progress; no man, even when he makes use of it, is grateful for the superior knowledge in a junior that convicts him to his face of gullibility. As it was, the verdict was favourable and Rathbone, in the relief of finding that he had not blundered, grew suddenly friendly—to the point of a dinner invitation; which was given, in part, as instinctive thanks for restored self-esteem, in part because it might interest Phillida to meet a young man who took gimcracks as gravely as herself. The invitation, as a matter of course, was accepted; and three days later Savage met Phillida Rathbone.

===Chinese===
如果回答是否定的，上司与下属之间的私人交往大概就到此为止了；没有人——即便他自己也利用这种知识——会感激下属比自己更在行而当场揭穿自己的易受骗。好在结论是正面的，拉思伯恩因发现自己并未出丑而如释重负，顿时变得友善起来——甚至发出了共进晚餐的邀请；这邀请部分是出于恢复自尊后的本能感激，部分是因为让女儿见见一个和她一样认真对待廉价小玩意儿的年轻人或许会让她感兴趣。邀请自然被接受了；三天后，萨维奇见到了菲莉达·拉思伯恩。

===Original===
"I've asked a young fellow you're sure to get on with"—so Rathbone had informed his daughter; who, thereupon, as later she confessed to Theodore, had made up her mind to be bored. She threw away her prejudice swiftly when she found the new acquaintance talked music with intelligence—she herself had music in her brain as well as in her fingertips—while he from the beginning was attracted by a daintiness of manner and movement that puzzled him in Rathbone's daughter. ... From that first night he must have been drawn to her, since the evening remained to him clear in every detail; always in the hollow of a glowing fire he could summon up Phillida, himself and Rathbone, sitting, the three of them, round the table with its silver and tall roses. ... In the centre a branching cluster of roses—all yellow, like Phillida's dress. ... Rathbone, for the most part, good-naturedly silent, Phillida and himself talking swiftly. ... In shaded light and a solid, pleasant comfort; ordinary comfort, which he took for granted as an element of daily life, but which yet was the heritage of many generations, the product of long centuries of striving and cunning invention. ... Later, in the drawing-room, the girl made music—and he saw himself listening from his corner of the sofa with a cigarette, unlit, between his fingers. Above all it was her quality of daintiness that pleased him; she was a porcelain girl, with something of the grace that he associated with the eighteenth century. ...

===Chinese===
"我请了一个你肯定会喜欢的年轻人"——拉思伯恩这样通知女儿；而她——后来向西奥多承认——当时已打定主意要感到无聊。当她发现这位新相识谈论音乐时见解独到——她自己脑中和指尖都流淌着音乐——便迅速抛开了成见；而他则从一开始就被她举止和动作中的那种精致所吸引，这在拉思伯恩的女儿身上让他颇为费解。……从那个夜晚起，他一定被她吸引了，因为那晚的每个细节都清晰地留在他的记忆里；他总能在炉火的光晕中唤起菲莉达、他自己和拉思伯恩三人围坐在摆着银器和高挑玫瑰的餐桌旁的画面。……桌子中央是一簇分枝的玫瑰——全是黄色的，和菲莉达的裙子一样。……拉思伯恩大半时间好脾气地沉默着，菲莉达和他自己则热烈地交谈。……在柔和的灯光和实在而舒适的安逸中；这种安逸他视作日常生活的寻常元素而理所当然地享用，但它其实是数代人的遗产，是漫长世纪的奋斗和精巧发明的结晶。……后来在客厅里，女孩弹起了琴——他看见自己坐在沙发一角，指间夹着一支未点燃的烟，聆听她的演奏。最令他愉悦的是她的那种精致；她是个瓷娃娃般的姑娘，带着某种让他联想到十八世纪的优雅。

===Original===
After half an hour that was sheer content to Theodore she broke off from her playing to sit on the arm of her father's chair and ruffle his grey hair caressingly.
"Old man, does my noise on the piano prevent you from reading your paper?"
Whereat Rathbone laughed and returned the caress; and Phillida explained, for the visitor's benefit, that the poor dear didn't know one tune from another and must have been bored beyond measure—by piano noises since they came upstairs and nothing but music-talk at dinner.
"I believe we've driven him to the Montagu divorce case," she announced, looking over his shoulder. " 'Housemaid cross-examined—the Colonel's visits.' Daddy, have you fallen to that?"

===Chinese===
半小时纯粹的满足之后，她中断了演奏，坐在父亲椅子的扶手上，爱抚地揉乱他灰白的头发。
"老爹，我弹钢琴吵到你看报纸了吗？"
拉思伯恩笑着回以爱抚；菲莉达则向客人解释道，这可怜的老头子分不清调子，肯定无聊透了——上楼后一直听她弹琴，晚饭时又尽是音乐话题。
"我相信我们把他逼到蒙塔古离婚案上了，"她看着父亲肩头的报纸说。"'女仆被盘问——上校的来访。'爹，你读到那儿了？"

===Original===
"No, minx," he rebuked her, "I haven't. I'm not troubling to wade through the housemaid's evidence for the very good reason that it's quite unnecessary. I shall hear all about it from you."
"That's a nasty one," Phillida commented, rubbing her cheek against her father's. She turned the paper idly, reading out the headlines. " 'American elections—Surprises at Newmarket—Bank Rate'—There doesn't seem much news except the housemaid and the colonel, does there?"

===Chinese===
"没有，小淘气，"他嗔怪道，"我没读。我才懒得去啃女仆的证词，理由很充分——根本没必要。反正你会全告诉我的。"
"那可真是个烂摊子，"菲莉达一边说，一边把脸颊贴着父亲的脸蹭了蹭。她随意翻着报纸，念出标题。"'美国大选——纽马克特赛马场爆冷——银行利率'——好像除了女仆和上校就没啥新闻了，是吧？"

===Original===
Rathbone laughed as he pinched her cheek and pointed—to a headline here and a headline there, to a cloud that was not yet the size of a man's hand.
"It depends on what you call news. It seems to have escaped you that we've just had a Budget. That matters to those of us who keep expensive daughters. And, little as the subject may interest you, I gather from the size of his type, that the editor attaches some importance to the fact that the Court of Arbitration has decided against the Karthanian claim. That, of course, compared to a housemaid in the witness-box is—"

===Chinese===
拉思伯恩笑着捏了捏她的脸，指着这里一个标题、那里一个标题，指着一朵尚未大如人掌的云。
"那得看你管什么叫新闻。看来你没注意到我们刚发布了财政预算。这对养着奢侈女儿的人可事关重大。而且，尽管这话题引不起你什么兴趣，我从字体大小判断，编辑对仲裁法院（Court of Arbitration）驳回卡尔塔尼亚（Karthania）诉求这一事实还是颇为重视的。当然了，跟女仆出庭作证比起来——"

===Original===
"Ponderous," she finished and laughed across at Theodore. "Important, no doubt, but ponderous—the Court of Arbitration always is. That's why I skipped it." ... Then, carelessly interested, and running her eye down the columns of the newspaper, she supposed the decision was final and those noisy little Karthanians would have to be quiet at last. Rathbone shrugged his shoulders and hoped so.
"But they'll have to, won't they?" said Phillida. "Give me a match, Daddy—There's no higher authority than the Court of Arbitration, is there?"

===Chinese===
"沉重，"她替他说完，朝西奥多笑起来。"重要，当然重要，但是沉重——仲裁法院总是这样。所以我跳过去了。"……接着，她漫不经心地扫视报纸各栏，想当然地认为裁决是最终的，那些吵闹的卡尔塔尼亚小人终于该安静了。拉思伯恩耸耸肩，希望如此。
"但他们必须接受，对吧？"菲莉达说。"给我根火柴，爹——没有比仲裁法院更高的权威了，不是吗？"

===Original===
"If," Rathbone suggested as he held a light to her cigarette, "if your newspaper reading were not limited to scandals and chiffons, you might have noticed that your noisy little friends in the East have declared with their customary vehemence that in no circumstances whatever will they accept an adverse verdict—not even from the Court of Arbitration."
"But they'll have to, won't they?" Phillida repeated placidly. "I mean—they can't go against everybody else. Against the League."
She tried to blow a smoke-ring with conspicuous ill-success, and Theodore, watching her from his corner of the sofa—intent on her profile against the light—heard Rathbone explaining that "against everybody else" was hardly the way to put it, since the Federal Council was not a happy family at present. There was very little doubt that Karthania was being encouraged to make trouble—and none at all that there would be difference of opinion on the subject of punitive action. ... Phillida, with an arm round her father's neck, was divided between international politics and an endeavour to make the perfect ring—now throwing in a question anent the constitution and dissensions of the League, now rounding her mouth for a failure—while Theodore, on the sofa, leaned his head upon his hand that he might shade his eyes and watch her without seeming to watch. ... He listened to Rathbone—and did not listen; and that, as he realized later, had been so far his attitude to interests in the mass. The realities of his life were immediate and personal—with, in the background, dim interests in the mass that were vaguely distasteful as politics. A collective game played with noisy idealism and flaring abuse, which served as copy to the makers of newspapers and gave rise at intervals to excited conversation and argument. ...

===Chinese===
"假如，"拉思伯恩一边给她点烟，一边说，"假如你读报不只是看丑闻和时装，你或许会注意到，你东方那些吵闹的小朋友已经一如既往地激烈宣称，无论在何种情况下都不会接受不利裁决——即便是仲裁法院的裁决也不行。"
"但他们必须接受，不是吗？"菲莉达平静地重复道。"我是说——他们不能跟所有人为敌。跟联盟（the League）作对。"
她试图吹一个烟圈，明显不成功，西奥多从沙发角落注视着她——凝神于她逆光中的侧脸——听见拉思伯恩解释说"跟所有人为敌"的说法并不确切，因为联邦委员会（Federal Council）眼下并非一团和气。毫无疑问，卡尔塔尼亚正被怂恿制造麻烦——而关于惩罚行动的分歧也毫无悬念。……菲莉达一只手臂搂着父亲的脖子，在国际政治和努力吹出完美烟圈之间左右开弓——时而抛出一个关于联盟章程和内部分歧的问题，时而撅起嘴准备吹——又一次失败；与此同时，西奥多在沙发上用手支着头，以便遮住目光、不露痕迹地注视着她。……他听拉思伯恩说话——又没有听进去；他后来意识到，迄今为止，这就是他对公共利益的态度。他生活中的真实是直接的、个人的——背景中隐约有公共利益的影子，却像政治一样令他隐约生厌。一场以喧嚣的理想主义和激烈的谩骂进行的集体游戏，为报纸提供素材，时不时引发激烈的讨论和争辩。……

===Original===
What was real, and only real while Rathbone talked, was the delicate poise of Phillida's head, the decorative line of Phillida's body, his pleasure in the sight of her, his comfort in a well-ordered room; these things were realities, tangible or aesthetic, in whose company a man, if he were so inclined, might discuss academically an Eastern imbroglio and the growing tendency to revolt against the centralized authority of the League. Between life, as he grasped it, and public affairs there was no visible, essential connection. The Karthanian imbroglio, as he strolled to his chambers, was an item in the makeup of a newspaper, the subject of a recent conversation; it was the rhythm of Phillida's music that danced in his brain as a living and insistent reality. That, and not the stirrings of uneasy nations, kept him wakeful till long after midnight.

===Chinese===
拉思伯恩说话之际，唯一真实的——唯一只有真实可言的——是菲莉达头部优雅的姿态、菲莉达身体优美的曲线、他对她身影的愉悦、他对一间布置妥当的房间的舒适感；这些是实在的、可触可感或审美性的事物，一个男人若有兴致，尽可以在它们的陪伴下高谈阔论东方乱局和反抗联盟集权日益增长的倾向。在他看来，他所把握的生活与公共事务之间并无可见的、本质的联系。当他漫步回公寓时，卡尔塔尼亚乱局不过是报纸版面的一个条目、刚才谈话的一个话题；而菲莉达琴声的节奏却在他脑海中跃动，作为一种鲜活而萦绕不去的真实。是它，而非那些不安的民族的骚动，让他彻夜无眠直到午夜过后很久。

## II / 第二章

===Original===
While Theodore Savage paid his court to Phillida Rathbone, the Karthanian decision was the subject of more than conversation; diplomatists and statesmen were busy while he drifted into love and dreamed through the sudden rumours that excited his fellows at the office. In London, for the most part, journalism was guarded and reticent, the threat of secession at first hardly mentioned; but in nations and languages that favoured secession the press was voicing the popular cry with enthusiasm that grew daily more heated. Through conflicting rumour this at least was clear: at the next meeting of the Council of the League its authority would be tested to the uttermost, since the measure of independent action demanded by the malcontent members would amount to a denial of the federal principle, to secession in fact if not in name. ... Reaction against central and unified authority was not a phenomenon of yesterday; it had been gathering its strength through years of racial friction, finding an adherent in every community that considered itself aggrieved by a decision of the Council or award of the Court of Arbitration, and for years it had taxed the ingenuity of the majority of the Council to avoid open breach and defiance.

===Chinese===
当西奥多·萨维奇向菲莉达·拉思伯恩（Phillida Rathbone）献殷勤时，卡尔塔尼亚裁决引发的远不止闲谈；外交官和政治家们忙着应对，而他却在坠入爱河的过程中，对办公室同僚们兴奋议论的那些突如其来的传闻充耳不闻。在伦敦，新闻界大体上谨慎而缄默，起初几乎不提分裂的威胁；但在那些拥护分裂的国家和语言中，报纸正以日益高涨的热情发出大众的呼声。尽管传言相互矛盾，有一点至少是清楚的：在联盟（the League）理事会下次会议上，其权威将受到最严峻的考验，因为不满成员要求的独立行动措施，将等于对联邦原则的否定，即便不叫分裂，实质也是如此。……对中央集权和统一权威的反叛并非昨日现象；它在多年的种族摩擦中积蓄力量，每一个认为自己被理事会决议或仲裁法院（Court of Arbitration）裁决所伤害的群体都成了它的拥护者；多年来，理事会多数成员绞尽脑汁以避免公开的破裂和对抗。

===Original===
Before open breach and its consequences, both sides had so far manoeuvred, hesitated, compromised; it had been left to a minor, a very minor, state, to rush in where others feared to tread. The flat refusal of a heady, half-civilized little democracy to accept the unfavourable verdict of the Court of Arbitration was the spark that might fire a powder-barrel; its frothy demonstrations, ridiculous in themselves, appealed to the combative instinct in others, to race-hatreds, old herding feuds and jealousies. These found vent in answering demonstrations, outbursts of popular sympathy in states not immediately affected; the noisy rebel was hailed as a martyr and pioneer of freedom, and became the pretext for resistance to the Council's oppression. There was no doubt of the extent of the regrouping movement of the nations, of the stirrings of a widespread combativeness which denounced Federation as a system whereby dominant interests and races exploited their weaker rivals. With the meeting of the Council would come the inevitable clash of interests; the summons to the offending member of the League to retreat from its impossible position, and—in case of continued defiance—the proposal to take punitive action. That proposal, to all seeming, must bring about a crisis; those members of the League who had encouraged the rebel in defiance would hardly consent to cooperate in punitive measures; and refusal—withdrawal of their military contingents—would mean virtual secession and denial of majority rule. If collective excitement and anger ran high, it might mean even more than secession; there were possibilities—first hinted at, later discussed without subterfuge—of actual and armed opposition should the Council attempt to enforce its decree and authority. ... Humanity, once more, was gathering into herds and growing sharply conscious alike of division and comradeship.

===Chinese===
在公开破裂及其后果面前，双方迄今都在迂回、犹豫、妥协；让一个微不足道的小国去闯别人不敢涉足的险境。一个头脑发热、半开化的小民主国家断然拒绝接受仲裁法院不利裁决的行为，如同点燃火药桶的火星；它那些泡沫般的示威活动本身虽属荒唐，却激起了其他人心中的战斗本能、种族仇恨、古老的群体宿怨和嫉妒。这些情绪在同样性质的示威活动中得到发泄，在未直接受影响的国家里爆发出民众的同情；那个吵闹的反叛者被奉为殉道者和自由先驱，成为抵抗理事会压迫的借口。各国重新组合的规模毋庸置疑，一股广泛的战斗性正在觉醒，它谴责联邦制度是主导利益和种族剥削弱小对手的体系。理事会召开时，利益冲突将不可避免地爆发；联盟将召令那个犯事的成员从其不可接受的立场上退缩——倘若继续抗拒——则将提议采取惩罚行动。这一提议看似必然引发危机；那些曾鼓励叛逆者抗拒的联盟成员几乎不可能同意配合惩罚措施；而拒绝——撤回军事分遣队——将意味着事实上的分裂和对多数规则的否定。如果集体兴奋和愤怒高涨，其后果甚至可能超越分裂；存在这样的可能性——起初只是暗示，后来则无所掩饰地讨论——即若理事会试图强制执行其法令和权威，将可能爆发实际的武装对抗。……人类，再次聚集成群，对分裂和同伴情谊都变得同样敏锐地自觉。

===Original===
It was some time before Theodore was even touched by the herding instinct and spirit; apart, in a delicate world of his own, he concerned himself even less than usual with the wider interests of politics. By his fellows in the Distribution Office he was known as an incurable optimist; even when the cloud had spread rapidly and darkened he saw "strained relations" through the eyes of a lover, and his mind, busied elsewhere, refused to dwell anxiously on "incidents" and "disquieting possibilities." They intruded clumsily on his delicate world and, so soon as might be, he thrust them behind him and slipped back to the seclusion that belonged to himself and a woman. All his life, thought and impulse, for the time being, was a negation, a refusal of the idea of strife and destruction; in his happy egoism he planned to make and build—a home and a lifetime of content.

===Chinese===
西奥多过了相当一段时间才受到群体本能和精神的影响；沉浸在自己精致的小世界里，他比平时更少关心政治的广泛利益。配给局的同事们都知道他是个无可救药的乐观主义者；即便乌云迅速蔓延变暗，他也透过情人的眼睛看待"紧张关系"，心思忙于他处，拒绝焦虑地思考那些"事件"和"令人不安的可能性"。它们笨拙地侵入他精致的世界，他一有机会就把它们抛在脑后，溜回属于他和一个女人的幽静之所。此刻，他全部的生命、思想和冲动都是一种否定，是对冲突和毁灭的拒绝；在幸福的自我中心主义中，他计划着创造和建造——一个家，一段安逸的人生。

===Original===
Now and again, and in spite of his reluctance, his veil of happy egoism was brushed aside—some chance word or incident forcing him to look upon the menace. There was the evening in Vallance's rooms, for instance—where the talk settled down to the political crisis, and Holt, the long journalist, turned sharply on Vallance, who supposed we were drifting into war.
"That's nonsense, Vallance! Nonsense! It's impossible—unthinkable!"
"Unpleasant, if you like," said Vallance; "but not impossible. At least—it never has been."

===Chinese===
尽管不情愿，他那层幸福自我主义的面纱还是时不时被掀开——某个偶然的词语或事件迫使他正视威胁。比如在瓦兰斯（Vallance）房间里的那个夜晚——话题落在了政治危机上，那个瘦高的记者霍尔特（Holt）突然转向瓦兰斯，后者认为我们正在滑向战争。
"那是胡说，瓦兰斯！胡说！不可能——不可想象！"
"令人不快，如果你愿意这么说，"瓦兰斯说；"但并非不可能。至少——从未有过不可能的事。"

===Original===
"That's no reason," Holt retorted; "we're not living yesterday. There'll be no war, and I'll tell you why: because the men who will have to start it—daren't!" He had a penetrating voice which he raised when excited, so that other talk died down and the room was filled with his argument. Politicians, he insisted, might bluff and use threats—menace with a bogey, shake a weapon they dared not use—but they would stop short at threats, manoeuvre for position and retreat. Let loose modern science, mechanics and chemistry, they could not—there was a limit to human insanity, if only because there was a limit to the endurance of the soldier. Unless you supposed that all politicians were congenital idiots or criminal lunatics out to make holocausts. What was happening at present was manoeuvring pure and simple; neither side caring to prejudice its case by open admission that appeal to force was unthinkable, each side hoping that the other would be the first to make the admission, each side trotting out the dummy soldiers that were only for show, and would soon be put back in their boxes. ... War, he repeated, was unthinkable. ...

===Chinese===
"那不成理由，"霍尔特反驳道；"我们活的不是昨天。不会有战争，我告诉你们为什么：因为那些必须发动战争的人——不敢！"他声音洪亮，激动时更加高亢，因此其他谈话都平息了，房间里充满了他的论证。他坚持认为，政客们或许会虚张声势、使用威胁——用稻草人吓人、摇晃他们不敢使用的武器——但他们会止步于威胁，为争取有利位置而周旋，然后退缩。一旦放开现代科学、机械和化学，他们就无法得逞——人类的疯狂毕竟有限度，至少士兵的承受力是有极限的。除非你认为所有政客都是天生的白痴或一心制造浩劫的疯子。目前发生的事不过是纯粹的周旋；双方都不愿因公开承认诉诸武力不可想象而损害自己的立场，双方都希望对方先做出让步，双方都搬出仅供展览的稻草兵，很快就会放回盒子。……他重申，战争是不可想象的。

===Original===
"Man," said a voice behind Theodore, "does much that is unthinkable!"
Theodore turned that he might look at the speaker—Markham, something in the scientific line, who had sat in silence, with a pipe between his lips, till he dropped out his slow remark.
"Your mistake," he went on, "lies in taking these people—statesmen, politicians—for free agents, and in thinking they have only one fear. Look at Meyer's speech this morning—that's significant. He has been moderate so far, a restraining influence; now he breathes fire and throws in his lot with the extremists. What do you make of that?"
"Merely," said Holt, "that Meyer has lost his head."
"In which happy state," suggested Vallance, "the impossible and unthinkable mayn't frighten him."
"That's one explanation," said Markham. "The other is that he is divided between his two fears—the fear of war and the fear of his democracy, which, being in a quarrelsome and restless mood, would break him if he flinched and applauds him to the echo when he blusters. And, maybe, at the moment, his fear of being broken is greater than his fear of the impossible—at any rate the threat is closer. ... The man himself may be reasonable—even now—but he is the instrument of instinctive emotion. Almost any man, taken by himself, is reasonable—and, being reasonable, cautious. Meyer can think, just as well as you and I, so long as he stands outside a crowd; but neither you nor I, nor Meyer, can think when we are one with thousands and our minds are absorbed into a jelly of impulse and emotion."

===Chinese===
"人啊，"西奥多身后一个声音说，"做了许多不可想象的事！"
西奥多转身看向说话者——马卡姆（Markham），搞科学研究的，一直叼着烟斗默默坐在那里，直到他慢慢吐出这句话。
"你的错误，"他接着说，"在于把这些人物——政治家、政客——当作自由的行动者，并且以为他们只有一种恐惧。看看迈耶（Meyer）今天的演讲——那很有意义。他迄今为止一直温和节制，是个约束力量；现在他却怒火中烧，与极端派站到了一起。你怎么看？"
"不过是，"霍尔特说，"迈耶头脑发热了。"
"在这种愉快的状态下，"瓦兰斯暗示道，"不可能和不可想象的东西或许吓不住他。"
"这是一种解释，"马卡姆说。"另一种是他被两种恐惧撕裂——对战争的恐惧和对其民众的恐惧；他的民众此刻正处于好斗而躁动的状态，若他退缩会毁掉他，若他虚张声势则会对他报以满堂喝彩。而且，也许此刻他对被毁灭的恐惧超过了对不可能之事的恐惧——至少威胁离他更近。……他本人或许现在还是理性的——但他已是本能情感的工具。几乎任何人，单看个体，都是理性的——而理性的人都谨慎。只要置身人群之外，迈耶和你我一样能思考；但你我迈耶任何人，一旦与千万人融为一体，心智被吸收进冲动和情感的果冻中，就无法思考了。

===Original===
"I like your phrase about jelly," said Vallance. "It has an odd picturesqueness. Your argument itself—or, rather, your assertion—strikes me as a bit sweeping."
"All the same," Markham nodded, "it's worth thinking over. ... Man in the mass, as a crowd, can only feel; there is no such thing as a mass-mind or intellect—only mass desires and emotions. That is what I mean by saying that Meyer—whatever his intelligence or sanity—is the instrument of instinctive emotion. ... And instinctive emotion, Holt—until it has been hurt—is damnably and owlishly courageous. It isn't clever enough to be afraid; not even of red murder—or starvation by millions—or the latest thing in gas or high explosive. Stir it up enough and it'll run on 'em—as the lemmings run to the sea."
Holt snorted something that sounded like "Rot!" and Vallance, sprawling an arm along the mantelpiece, asked, "Another of your numerous theories?"
"If you like," Markham assented, "but it's a theory deduced from hard facts. ... It's a fact, isn't it, that no politician takes a crowd into his confidence until he wants to make a fight of it? It's a fact, isn't it, that no movements in mass are creative or constructive—that simultaneous action, simultaneous thought, always is and must be destructive? Set what we call the People in motion and something has got to be broken. The crowd-life is still at the elementary, the animal stage; it has not yet acquired the human power of construction ... and the crowd, the people, democracy—whatever you like to call it—has been stirring in the last few years; getting conscious again, getting active, looking round for something to break ... which means that the politician is faced once more with the necessity of giving it something to break. Naturally he prefers that the breakage should take place in the distance—and, League or no League, the eternal and obvious resource is War ... which was not too risky when fought with swords and muskets, but now—as Holt says—is impossible. Being a bit of a chemist, I'm sure Holt is right; but I'm also sure that man, as a herd, does not think. Further, I am doubtful if man, as a herd, ever finds out what is impossible except through the painful process of breaking his head against it."
"I'm a child in politics," said Vallance, "and I may be dense—but I'm afraid it isn't entirely clear to me whether your views are advanced or grossly and shamelessly reactionary?"
"Neither," said Markham, "or both—you can take your choice. I have every sympathy with the people, the multitude; it's hard lines that it can only achieve destruction—just because there is so much of it, because it isn't smaller. But I also sympathize with the politician in his efforts to control the destructive impulse of the multitude. And, finally—in view of that progress of science of which Holt has reminded us, and of which I know a little myself—I'm exceedingly sorry for us all."

===Chinese===
"我喜欢你那个关于果冻的说法，"瓦兰斯说。"有种奇特的画面感。不过你的论证本身——或者说你的断言——在我看来有些过于绝对。"
"尽管如此，"马卡姆点头道，"值得深思。……作为群体的人只能感受；不存在什么群体心智或群体智力——只有群体欲望和情感。这就是我说迈耶——无论他多有才智或多理智——是本能情感的工具的意思。……而本能情感，霍尔特——在受伤之前——是极其勇敢而愚蠢的。它不够聪明，不懂恐惧；甚至不怕大规模的杀戮——数百万人的饥荒——或者最新式的毒气和烈性炸药。把它煽动得足够厉害，它就会朝那些东西冲去——就像旅鼠奔向大海。"
霍尔特哼了一声，像是说"胡扯！"，瓦兰斯一只手臂懒洋洋地搭在壁炉架上，问道："又是你众多理论中的一个？"
"随你怎么说，"马卡姆同意，"但这是从事实中推导出来的理论。……事实是，没有哪个政客会在不想打一场的时候把群众纳入他的信心圈子，不是吗？事实是，没有哪个群众运动是创造性的或建设性的——同步的行动、同步的思想，过去如此，将来也必然如此是破坏性的。让我们所说的人民运动起来，就必须有东西被打破。群体生活仍然处于初级的、动物的阶段；它尚未获得人类的建设能力……而群众、人民、民主——随便你怎么称呼——在过去几年里一直在骚动；重新觉醒，变得活跃，四处寻找可以打破的东西……这意味着政治家再次面临给它东西可打的必然性。他自然更希望破坏发生在远方——无论有没有联盟，永恒而显而易见的手段就是战争……用刀剑和火枪打还不算太冒险，但现在——正如霍尔特所说——已经不可能了。作为半个化学家，我确信霍尔特说得对；但我同样确信，作为群体的人不会思考。此外，我怀疑作为群体的人，除了经历碰得头破血流的痛苦过程外，是否曾认清什么是不可能的。"
"我在政治上是个门外汉，"瓦兰斯说，"也许我迟钝——但恐怕我不太明白你的观点究竟是进步的还是极端而无耻的反动？"
"都不是，"马卡姆说，"或者说都是——你自便。我对人民、对大众深表同情；它只能带来破坏——仅仅因为它人数太多、不够少——这确实残酷。但我也同情那些试图控制大众破坏冲动的政治家。最后——鉴于霍尔特提醒我们的科学进步，我自己也略知一二——我为我们所有人感到无比痛惜。"

===Original===
Someone from across the room asked: "You make it war, then?"
"I make it war. We have had peace for more than a generation, so our periodic bloodletting is already a long time overdue. The League has staved it off for a bit, but it hasn't changed the human constitution; and the real factor in the Karthanian quarrel—or any other—is the periodic need of the human herd for something to break and for something to break itself against. ... Resistance and self-sacrifice—the need of them—the call of the lemming to the sea. ... And, perhaps, it's all the stronger in this generation because this generation has never known war, and does not fear it."
"Education," said Holt, addressing the air, "is general and compulsory—has been so for a good many years. The inference being that the records of previous wars—and incidentally of the devastation involved—are not inaccessible to that large proportion of our population which is known as the average man."
"As printed pages, yes," Markham agreed. "But what proportion even of a literate population is able to accept the statement of a printed page as if it were a personal experience?"
"As we're not all fools," Holt retorted, "I don't make it war."
"I hope you're right, for my own sake," said Markham good-temperedly. He knocked out his pipe as he spoke and made ready to go—while Theodore looked after him, interested, for the moment, disturbingly. ... Markham's unemotional and matter-of-fact acceptance of "periodic bloodletting" made rumour suddenly real, and for the first time Theodore saw the Karthanian imbroglio as more than the substance of telegrams and articles, something human, actual, and alive. ... Saw himself, even Phillida, concerned in it—through a medley of confused and threatening shadows. ... For the moment he was roused from his self-absorption and thrust into the world that he shared with the common herd of men. He and Phillida were no longer as the gods apart, with their lives to make in Eden; they were little human beings, the sport of a common human destiny. ... He remembered how eagerly he caught at Holt's condemnation of Markham as a crank and Vallance's next comment on the crisis.

===Chinese===
房间那边有人问："那你认为会是战争？"
"我认为是战争。我们已经和平了一代人之久，所以我们的定期放血早该到来了。联盟把它拖延了一阵子，但并未改变人类的构造；卡尔塔尼亚争端——或任何其他争端——的真正因素，是人类群体对需要打破什么东西、以及拿什么去撞击的定期需求。……抵抗和自我牺牲——对它们的需要——旅鼠奔向大海的召唤。……而且，也许在这一代人身上这种需求更加强烈，因为这一代人从未经历过战争，也不惧怕战争。"
"教育，"霍尔特对着空气说，"已经普及且强制——已经好多年了。推论是，过往战争的记录——以及附带的破坏记录——对于我们所谓普通大众中的大多数人来说并非不可得。"
"作为印刷的文字，是的，"马卡姆同意。"但即便是识字人口，有多大比例能接受印刷文字的陈述如同亲身经历？"
"我们又不全是傻瓜，"霍尔特反驳道，"我认为不会是战争。"
"我希望你是对的，为了我自己，"马卡姆好脾气地说。他边说边磕掉烟斗，准备离开——西奥多目送他离去，此刻带着兴趣，甚至有些不安。……马卡姆对"定期放血"那种冷静而实事求是的接受，让传言突然变得真实起来，西奥多第一次将卡尔塔尼亚乱局视为超越电报和文章内容的东西，一种真实、鲜活的人间事。……他看见自己，甚至菲莉达，都被卷入其中——穿过一片混乱而威胁的阴影。……此刻他从自我沉溺中被唤醒，被推入与芸芸众生共享的世界。他和菲莉达不再是高高在上的神明，在伊甸园中构筑自己的生活；他们成了渺小的人类，共同人类命运的玩物。……他记得自己多么急切地抓住霍尔特对马卡姆怪人论的谴责，以及瓦兰斯接着对危机的评论。

===Original===
"We had exactly the same scare three—or was it four?—years ago. This is the trouble about Transylvania all over again—just the same alarums and excursions. That fizzled out quietly in a month or six weeks and the chances are that Karthania will fizzle out, too."
"Of course it will," Holt declared with emphasis—and proceeded to demolish Markham's theories. Theodore left before he had finished his argument; as explained dogmatically in Holt's penetrating voice, the intrigues and dissensions of the Federal Council were once more unreal and frankly boring. The argument satisfied, but no longer interested—and ten minutes after Markham's departure his thoughts had drifted away from politics to the private world he shared with Phillida Rathbone.

===Chinese===
"我们三年前——还是四年前？——有过完全相同的恐慌。这不过是特兰西瓦尼亚问题的翻版——同样的警报和骚动。那次在一个月或六周内悄无声息地平息了，卡尔塔尼亚大概也会如此。"
"当然会，"霍尔特加重语气宣称——接着便开始瓦解马卡姆的理论。西奥多在他论证结束前就离开了；在霍尔特洪亮的声音中教条式的解释下，联邦委员会的阴谋和分歧再次变得不真实且令人厌倦。论证让人满意，但不再引人入胜——马卡姆离开十分钟后，他的思绪已从政治漂回到与菲莉达·拉思伯恩共享的私人世界。

---

===Original===
For very delight of it he lingered over his courtship, finding charm in the pretence of uncertainty long after it had ceased to exist. To Phillida also there was pleasure not only in the winning, but in the exquisite game itself; once or twice when Theodore was hovering near avowal, she deferred the inevitable, eluded him with laughter, asked tacitly to play a little longer. ... In the end the avowal came suddenly, on the flash and impulse of a moment—when Phillida hesitated over one of his gifts, a print she had admired on the wall of his sitting-room, duly brought the next day for her acceptance.
"No, I oughtn't to take it—it's one of your treasures," she remonstrated.
"If you'd take all I have—and me with it," he stammered. ... That was the crisis of the exquisite game—and pretence of uncertainty was over.

===Chinese===
出于纯粹的愉悦，他故意拖延求爱，在早已不存在的不确定性伪装中寻找魅力。对菲莉达来说，乐趣也不仅在于赢得，更在于这场精致的游戏本身；有那么一两次，当西奥多接近表白时，她推迟了必然的结局，笑着躲避，含蓄地请求再玩一会儿。……最终，表白突然到来，出于刹那的灵光和冲动——当时菲莉达对他的礼物犹豫不决，那是一幅版画，他曾挂在起居室的墙上，她很欣赏，第二天他便带来了给她。
"不，我不该收下——这是你的珍藏之一，"她抗议道。
"如果你愿意收下我所有的一切——连我一起，"他结结巴巴地说。……那是精致游戏的高潮——不确定性的伪装就此终结。"

## III

===Original===
One impression of those first golden hours that stayed with him always was the certainty with which they had dwelt on the details of their common future; he could see Phillida with her hands on his shoulders explaining earnestly that they must live very near to the Dad﻿—the dear old boy had no one but herself and they mustn’t let him miss her too much. And when Theodore asked, “You don’t think he’ll object to me?” Rathbone’s disapproval was the only possible cloud﻿—which lifted at Phillida’s amused assurance that the old dear wasn’t as blind as all that and, having objections, would have voiced them before it was too late.

===Chinese===
关于那些最初黄金时刻的印象，始终萦绕在他心头的，是他们谈论彼此共同未来时的那种笃定；他仿佛看见菲莉达双手搭在他肩上，认真解释道，他们必须住得离老爸近一些——这亲爱的老头子除了她没有别人，他们不能让他太想她。当西奥多问“你觉得他会反对我吗？”时，拉思伯恩的反对是唯一可能的阴云——但菲莉达略带戏谑地保证说，这老宝贝没那么迟钝，要是有反对意见，早就在太迟之前说出来了。

===Original===
“You don’t suppose he hasn’t noticed﻿—just because he hasn’t said anything!”﻿ ﻿… Whereupon Theodore caught at her hands and demanded how long she had noticed?﻿—and they fell to a happy retracing of this step and that in their courtship.

===Chinese===
“你该不会以为他没注意到——就因为他什么都没说！”……西奥多于是抓住她的手，问她注意多久了？——两人便愉快地回溯起恋爱历程中的点点滴滴。

===Original===
When they heard Rathbone enter she ran down alone, telling Theodore to stay where he was till she called him; returning in five minutes or so, half-tearful and half-smiling, to say the dear old thing was waiting in the library. Then Theodore, in his turn, went down to the library where, red to the ears and stammering platitudes, he shook hands with his future father-in-law﻿—proceeding eventually to details of his financial position and the hope that Rathbone would not insist upon too lengthy an engagement?﻿ ﻿… The answer was so slow in coming that he repeated his question nervously.

===Chinese===
听到拉思伯恩进门的声音，她独自跑下楼，让西奥多原地等着，等她叫他；大约五分钟后，她半是泪痕半是微笑地回来了，说那亲爱的老头子在书房等着呢。于是西奥多也下楼去了书房，在那里，他耳朵通红，结结巴巴地说着客套话，与未来的岳父握手——最终谈及自己的经济状况，以及希望拉思伯恩不要坚持太长的订婚期？……回答迟迟不来，他紧张地重复了一遍问题。

===Original===
“No,” said Rathbone at last, “I don’t know that I”﻿—(he laid stress on the pronoun)﻿—“I don’t know that I should insist upon a very lengthy engagement. Only.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“不，”拉思伯恩终于说道，“我不知道我”——（他重读了代词）——“我不知道我是否该坚持很长的订婚期。只是……”

===Original===
Again he paused so long that Theodore repeated “Only?”

===Chinese===
他又停顿了许久，西奥多重复道：“只是？”

===Original===
“Only﻿—there may be obstacles﻿—not of my making or Phillida’s. Connected with the office﻿—your work﻿ ﻿… I dare say you’ve been too busy with your own affairs to give very much attention to the affairs of the world in general; still I conclude the papers haven’t allowed you to forget that the Federal Council was to vote today on the resolution to take punitive action? Result is just through﻿—half an hour ago. Resolution carried, by a majority of one only.”

===Chinese===
“只是——可能会有障碍——不是我或菲莉达造成的。与办公室有关——你的工作……我猜你一直忙于自己的事，没太关注世界的总体事态；不过我猜报纸没让你忘记联邦委员会今天要投票表决采取惩罚性行动的决议？结果刚出来——半小时前。决议通过了，仅以一票多数。”

===Original===
“Was it?” said Theodore﻿—and remembered a vague impulse of resentment, a difficulty in bringing down his thoughts from Phillida to the earthiness of politics. It took him an effort and a moment to add: “Close thing﻿—but they’ve pulled it off.”

===Chinese===
“是吗？”西奥多说——他想起一种模糊的反感，思绪难以从菲莉达拉回到政治的世俗层面。他费了点劲，过了一会儿才补充道：“险胜——但他们成功了。”

===Original===
“They have,” said Rathbone. “Just pulled it off﻿—but it remains to be seen if that’s matter for congratulation.﻿ ﻿… The vote commits us to action﻿—definitely﻿—and the minority have entered a protest against punitive action.﻿ ﻿… It seems unlikely that the protest is only formal.”

===Chinese===
“他们确实，”拉思伯恩说，“勉强成功了——但这是否值得庆贺还有待观察。……这项投票将我们明确地推上了行动之路——而少数派已经提交了抗议，反对惩罚性行动。……这项抗议看来不太可能只是走过场。”

===Original===
He was dry and curiously deliberate﻿—leaning back in his chair, speaking quietly, with fingers pressed together.﻿ ﻿… To the end Theodore remembered him like that; a square-jawed man, leaning back in his chair, speaking slowly, unemotionally﻿—the harbinger of infinite misfortune.﻿ ﻿… And himself, the listener, a young man engrossed by his own new happiness; irritated, at first, by the intrusion of that which did not concern it; then (as once before in Vallance’s rooms) uneasy and conscious of a threat.

===Chinese===
他语气平淡，出奇地从容——靠在椅背上，轻声说话，手指交叉。……西奥多至死都记得他这副模样；一个方下巴的男人，靠在椅背上，说话缓慢，毫无感情——他是无尽不幸的先兆。……而他自己，这个倾听者，是个沉浸在新婚幸福中的年轻人；起初，他因这无关之事的侵入而恼怒；随后（就像以前在瓦兰斯房间里一样）感到不安，意识到了威胁。

===Original===
He heard himself asking, “You think it’s﻿—serious?” and saw Rathbone’s mouth twist into the odd semblance of a smile.

===Chinese===
他听见自己问道：“你觉得这事——严重吗？”然后看见拉思伯恩的嘴扭曲成一种奇怪的似笑非笑的表情。

===Original===
“I think so. One way or other we shall know within a week.”

===Chinese===
“我认为是的。无论怎样，一周内我们就会知道了。”

===Original===
“You can’t mean﻿—war?” Theodore asked again﻿—remembering Holt and his “Impossible!”

===Chinese===
“你该不会是说——战争？”西奥多又问——想起了霍尔特和他的“不可能！”

===Original===
“It doesn’t seem unlikely,” said Rathbone.

===Chinese===
“似乎并非不可能，”拉思伯恩说。

===Original===
He had risen, with his hands thrust deep into his pockets, and begun to pace backwards and forwards. “Something may happen at the last minute﻿—but it’s difficult to see how they can draw back. They have gone too far. They’re committed, just as we are﻿—committed to a principle.﻿ ﻿… If we yield the Council abdicates its authority once for all; it’s an end of the League﻿—a plain break, and the Lord knows what next. And the other side daren’t stop at verbal protest. They will have to push their challenge; there’s too much clamour behind them.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
他站起身，双手深深插进口袋，开始来回踱步。“最后一分钟可能会有变故——但很难看出他们如何能退缩。他们走得太远了。他们和我们一样——被绑在了一个原则上。……如果我们让步，委员会将永远丧失权威；联盟将终结——彻底破裂，天知道接下来会发生什么。而对方不敢止步于口头抗议。他们必须推进挑战；他们背后的呼声太高了。……”

===Original===
“There was Transylvania,” Theodore reminded him.

===Chinese===
“还有特兰西瓦尼亚事件，”西奥多提醒他。

===Original===
“I know﻿—and nothing came of it. But that wasn’t pushed quite so far.﻿ ﻿… They threatened, but never definitely﻿—they left themselves a possibility of retreat. Now﻿ ﻿… as I said, something may happen﻿ ﻿… and, meanwhile, to go back to what I meant about you, personally, how this might affect you.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“我知道——但那次没成气候。不过那次没推得那么绝。……他们威胁过，但从不确定——给自己留了退路。现在……就像我说的，可能会有变故……同时，回到我刚才想说的关于你个人的事，这可能会如何影响你。……”

===Original===
He dropped into swift explanation. “Considerable rearrangement in the work of the Department﻿—if it should be necessary to place it on a war-footing.” Theodore’s duties﻿—if the worst should happen﻿—would certainly take him out of London and therefore part him from Phillida. “I can tell you that definitely﻿—now.”

===Chinese===
他迅速解释道：“部门工作将大幅调整——如果需要转入战时状态的话。”西奥多的职责——如果最坏的情况发生——肯定会让他离开伦敦，因此与菲莉达分离。“我现在可以明确告诉你这一点。”

===Original===
Perhaps he realized that the announcement, on a day of betrothal, was brutal; for he checked himself suddenly in his walk to and fro, clapped the young man good-naturedly on the shoulder, repeated that “Something might happen” and supposed he would not be sorry to hear that a member of the Government required his presence﻿—“So you and Phillida can dine without superfluous parents.”﻿ ﻿… And he said no word of war or parting to Phillida﻿—who came down with Theodore to watch her父亲 off, standing arm-in-arm upon the doorstep in the pride of her new relationship.

===Chinese===
也许他意识到，在订婚这天宣布这个消息太残忍了；因为他突然停下踱步，善意地拍拍年轻人的肩膀，重复说“可能会有变故”，并说他不会介意一位政府成员需要他的到场——“这样你和菲莉达就能不受多余父母打扰地共进晚餐了。”……他没有对菲莉达提起战争或分离——她和西奥多一起下楼送她父亲离开，挽着手臂站在门阶上，为新的关系而自豪。

===Original===
The threat lightened as they dined alone deliciously, as a foretaste of housekeeping in common; Phillida left him no thoughts to stray and only once, while the evening lasted, did they look from their private Paradise upon the world of common humanity. Phillida, as the clock neared ten, wondered vaguely what Henderson had wanted with her father? Was there anything particular, did Theodore know, any news about the Federal Council?﻿ ﻿… He hesitated for a moment, then told her the bare facts only﻿—the vote and the minority protest.

===Chinese===
当他们单独享用美味晚餐时，这威胁似乎减轻了，仿佛在预演共同持家的生活；菲莉达没让他思绪飘散，整个晚上他们只从他们私密的天堂望向凡人世界一次。快十点时，菲莉达隐约想知道亨德森找她父亲有什么事？西奥多知道有什么特别的事吗，关于联邦委员会的消息？……他犹豫了一下，然后只告诉她基本事实——投票和少数派抗议。

===Original===
“A protest,” she repeated. “That’s what they’ve all been afraid of.﻿ ﻿… It looks bad, doesn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“抗议，”她重复道。“那是他们所有人都害怕的。……情况不妙，是吧？”

===Original===
He agreed it looked bad; thinking less, it may be, of the threat of red ruin and disaster than of Rathbone’s warning that his duties would part him from Phillida.

===Chinese===
他同意情况不妙；他想到的，与其说是血色毁灭与灾难的威胁，不如说是拉思伯恩的警告——他的职责将使他与菲莉达分离。

===Original===
“I hope it doesn’t mean war,” she said.

===Chinese===
“我希望这不是战争的意思，”她说。

===Original===
At the time her voice struck him as serious, even anxious; later it amazed her that she had spoken so quietly, that there was no trembling of the slim white fingers that played with her chain of heavy beads.

===Chinese===
当时她的声音在他听来是严肃的，甚至焦虑的；后来他惊讶于她说话如此平静，那纤细的白皙手指把玩着沉重的珠链，竟没有一丝颤抖。

===Original===
“Do you think it does?” she asked him.

===Chinese===
“你认为这是战争吗？”她问他。

===Original===
Because he remembered the threat of parting and had need of her daily presence, he was stubborn in declaring that it did not, and could not, mean war; quoting Holt that modern war was impossible, that statesmen and soldiers knew it, and insisting that this was the Transylvanian business over again and would be settled as that was settled. She shook her head thoughtfully, having heard other views from her父亲; but her voice (he knew later) was thoughtful only﻿—not a quiver, not a hint of real fear in it.

===Chinese===
因为他记得分离的威胁，需要她的日常陪伴，他固执地宣称这不意味着，也不可能是战争；他引用霍尔特的话说现代战争不可能发生，政治家和士兵都知道，并坚持说这只是特兰西瓦尼亚事件的重演，会像那次一样得到解决。她若有所思地摇摇头，从她父亲那里听到了不同的观点；但她的声音（他后来知道）只是沉思——没有颤抖，没有一丝真正的恐惧。

===Original===
“It’ll have to come sometime﻿—now or in a year or two. At least, that’s what everybody says. I wonder if it’s true.”

===Chinese===
“总有一天会来的——现在或一两年后。至少，所有人都这么说。我不知道这是不是真的。”

===Original===
“No,” he said, “it isn’t﻿—unless we make it true. This sort of thing﻿—it’s a kind of common nightmare we have now and then. Every few years﻿—and when it’s over we turn round and wake up and wonder what the devil we were frightened about.”

===Chinese===
“不，”他说，“不是真的——除非我们让它成真。这种事——它是我们时不时会做的一种常见噩梦。每隔几年——等它过去，我们转身醒来，纳闷我们到底在怕什么。”

===Original===
“Yes,” she agreed, “when you come to think of it, it is rather like that. I don’t remember in the least what the fuss was all about last time﻿—but I know the papers were full of Transylvania and the poor old Dad was worked off his head for a week or two.﻿ ﻿… And then it was over and we forgot all about it.”

===Chinese===
“是的，”她同意道，“仔细想想，确实有点像那样。我完全不记得上次的纷争是为了什么——但我知道报纸上全是特兰西瓦尼亚的事，可怜的老爸有一两个星期忙得晕头转向。……然后事情过去了，我们就全忘了。”

===Original===
And at that they turned and went back to their golden solitude, shutting out, for the rest of the evening, a world that made protests and sent ominous telegrams. Before Theodore left her, to walk home restless with delight, they had decided on the fashion of Phillida’s ring and planned the acquisition of a Georgian house﻿—with powder-closet.

===Chinese===
于是他们转身回到他们金色的孤寂中，在整个晚上屏蔽了一个抗议不断、发送不祥电报的世界。在西奥多离开她、满怀喜悦地走回家之前，他们已经决定了菲莉达戒指的样式，并计划购置一栋乔治亚风格的房子——带化妆间。

===Original===
It was his restless delight that made sleep impossible﻿—and he sat at his window and smoked till the east was red.﻿ ﻿… While Henderson and Rathbone, a mile or two away, planned Distribution on a war-footing.

===Chinese===
正是他那份无法平静的喜悦让他无法入睡——他坐在窗前抽烟，直到东方泛红。……而亨德森和拉思伯恩，一两英里外，正在筹划战时状态的配给工作。

===Original===
---
Events in the next few days moved rapidly in an atmosphere of tense and rising life; races and peoples were suddenly and acutely意识地 the awareness of their collective life; and the neighbourly quarrel and bitterness of yesterday was forgotten in the new comradeship born of common hatred and common passion for self-sacrifice. There was talk at first, with diplomatists and leader-writers, of a possibility of localizing the conflict; but within forty-eight hours of the issue of the minority protest it was clear that the League would be rent. On one side, as on the other, statesmen were popular only when known to be unyielding in the face of impossible demands; crowds gathered when ministers met to take counsel and greeted them with cries to stand fast. Behind vulgar effervescence and music-hall thunder was faith in a righteous cause; and, as ever, man believed in himself and his cause with a hand on the hilt of his sword. Freedom and justice were suddenly real and attainable swiftly﻿—through violence wrought on their enemies.﻿ ﻿… Humanity, once more, was inspired by ideals that justified the shedding of blood and looked death in the face without fear.

===Chinese===
接下来的几天里，事件在紧张而高涨的生活气氛中迅速发展；种族与民族突然强烈地意识到他们的集体生活；昨天的邻里争吵与苦涩在共同仇恨和共同自我牺牲热情中诞生的新同志情谊中被遗忘。起初，外交官和社论作者们还在谈论局部化冲突的可能性；但在少数派抗议发出后的四十八小时内，很明显联盟将被撕裂。双方的政治家只有在表现出面对不可能要求时毫不屈服才受民众欢迎；当部长们会商时，人群聚集起来，高呼要坚守立场。在庸俗的亢奋和音乐厅的雷鸣背后，是对正义事业的信念；一如既往，人们相信自己和自己的事业，手按剑柄。自由与正义突然变得真实且可迅速实现——通过对敌人施加暴力。……人类再次被那些为流血辩护、无惧死亡的理想所鼓舞。

===Original===
As always, there were currents and crosscurrents, and those who were not seized by the common, splendid passion denounced it. Some meanly, by distortion of motive﻿—crying down faith as cupidity and the impulse to self-sacrifice as arrogance; and others, more worthy of hearing, who realized that the impulse to self-sacrifice is passing and the idealism of today the bestial cunning of tomorrow.﻿ ﻿… On one side and the other there was an attempt on the part of those who foresaw something, at least, of the inevitable, to pit fear against the impulse to self-sacrifice and make clear to a people to whom war was a legend only the extent of disaster ahead. The attempt was defeated, almost as begun, by the sudden launching of an ultimatum with twenty-four hours for reply.

===Chinese===
一如既往，潮流与逆流并存，那些未被共同、辉煌热情攫住的人谴责它。有些人卑劣地扭曲动机——将信仰贬为贪婪，将自我牺牲的冲动贬为傲慢；另一些人更值得倾听，他们意识到自我牺牲的冲动正在消逝，今天的理想主义将成为明天的野蛮诡计。……在双方，那些至少预见到必然性一部分的人试图用恐惧对抗自我牺牲的冲动，向那些视战争为传说的民族揭示前方灾难的程度。这项尝试几乎刚启动就被挫败了，因为突然发布了一份要求二十四小时内答复的最后通牒。

===Original===
At the news young men surged to the recruiting-stations, awaiting their turn for admission in long shouting, jesting lines; the best blood and honour of a generation that had not yet sated its inborn lust of combat. Women stood to watch them as their ranks moved slowly to the goal﻿—some proud to tears, others giggling a foolish approval. Great shifting crowds﻿—men and women who could not rest﻿—gathered in public places and awaited the inevitable news. In the last few hours﻿—all protest being useless﻿—even the loudest of the voices that clamoured against war had died down; and in the life collective was the strange, sudden peace which comes with the cessation of internal feud and the focusing of hatred on those who dwell beyond a nation’s borders.

===Chinese===
听到这个消息，年轻人涌向征兵站，在又长又吵闹、玩笑不断的队伍中等待入场；这是一个尚未满足其天生战斗欲望的世代中最优秀的血液与荣誉。妇女们站在那里看着他们的队伍缓缓走向目标——有些人骄傲得流泪，另一些人则傻笑着表示赞同。巨大的流动人群——坐立不安的男女——聚集在公共场所，等待不可避免的消息。在最后几小时——所有抗议都无济于事——即使是那些最响亮的反战声音也平息了；在集体生活中出现了奇怪的、突然的和平，这种和平随着内部纷争的停止和仇恨聚焦于国境之外的人而到来。

===Original===
---
Theodore Savage, in the days that followed his betrothal, was kept with his nose to the Distributive grindstone, working long hours of overtime in an atmosphere transformed out of recognition. The languid and formal routine of departments was succeeded by a fever of hurried innovation; gone were the lazy, semi-occupied hours when he had been wont to play with his thoughts of Phillida and the long free evenings that were hers as a matter of course. In the beginning he felt himself curiously removed from the strong, heady atmosphere that affected others like wine. Absorption in Phillida counted for something in his aloofness, but even without it his temperament was essentially averse from the crowd-life; he was stirred by the common desire to be of service, but was conscious of no mounting of energy restless and unsatisfied.﻿ ﻿… Having little conviction or bias in politics, he accepted without question the general version of the origins of conflict and resented, in orthodox fashion, the gross breach of faith and agreement which betrayed long established design. “It had got to be” and “They’ve been getting ready for years” were phrases on the general lip which he saw no reason to discredit; and, with acceptance of the inevitability of conflict, he ceased to find conflict “unthinkable.” In daily intercourse with those to whom it was thinkable, practical, a certainty﻿—to some, in the end, a desirable certainty﻿—Holt’s phrase lost its meaning and became a symbolic extravagance.﻿ ﻿… So far he was caught in the swirl of the crowd-life; but he was never one with it and remained conscious of it always as something that flowed by him, something apart from himself.

===Chinese===
在订婚后的日子里，西奥多·萨维奇埋头于配给局的工作，在完全改变的气氛中长时间加班。部门慵懒而正式的日常被匆忙创新的狂热所取代；那些他惯于思念菲莉达的慵懒、半工作状态的时光消失了，那些原本理所当然属于她的漫长自由夜晚也消失了。起初，他感到自己奇怪地远离了那如酒般影响他人的浓烈气氛。对菲莉达的专注在一定程度上造成了他的疏离，但即使没有它，他的性情本质上也厌恶群体生活；他被服务大众的共同愿望所触动，但并未感到那种不安而未满足的能量在增长。……由于对政治缺乏信念或偏见，他毫无异议地接受了冲突起源的普遍说法，并以正统方式憎恶背信弃义和对长期既定计划的严重背叛。“必须如此”和“他们已经准备了好几年”是人们嘴边的话，他没有理由不相信；随着接受冲突的必然性，他不再觉得冲突“不可想象”。在与那些认为冲突可想象、实际、确定——对某些人来说最终是可喜的确定——的人日常交往中，霍尔特的话失去了意义，成为一种象征性的夸张。……至此，他被卷入了群体生活的漩涡；但他从未与之合一，并始终意识到它是流经他的事物，是与他分离的事物。

===Original===
Above all he knew it as something apart when he saw how it had seized and mastered Phillida. She was curiously alive to its sweep and emotion, and beneath her outward daintiness lay the power of fervid partisanship. “If it weren’t for you,” she told him once, “I should break my heart because I’m only a woman”; and he saw that she pitied him, that she was even resentful for his sake, when she learned from her father that there was no question of allowing the clerks of the Distribution Office to volunteer for military service.

===Chinese===
尤其是当他看到它如何攫住并主宰了菲莉达时，他更知道它是某种分离的东西。她对其席卷与情感有着惊人的敏感，在她外表的优雅之下隐藏着狂热党派性的力量。“要不是为了你，”她曾告诉他，“我会心碎，因为我只是个女人”；当她从父亲那里得知配给局的职员不允许自愿参军时，他看出她同情他，甚至为他感到不平。

===Original===
“He says the Department will need all its trained men and that modern war is won by organization even more than by fighting. I’m glad you won’t have to go, my dear﻿—I’m glad﻿—” and, saying it, she clung to him as to one who stood in need of consolation.

===Chinese===
“他说部门需要所有训练有素的人，现代战争靠组织赢得，甚至比靠战斗更重要。我很高兴你不用去，亲爱的——我很高兴——”说着，她依偎着他，仿佛他需要安慰。

===Original===
He felt the implied consolation and sympathy﻿—with a twinge of conscience, not entirely sure of deserving it. But for the rigid departmental order, he knew he should have thought it his duty to volunteer and take his share of the danger that others were clamouring to face; but he had not cursed vehemently, like his junior, Cassidy, when Holles, equally blasphemous, burst into the room with the news that enlistment was barred. He thought of Cassidy’s angry blue eyes as he swore that, by hook or by crook, he would find his way into the air-service.﻿ ﻿… Phillida would have sympathized with Cassidy and the flash of her eyes answered his; she too, for the moment, was one with the crowd-life, and there were moments when he felt it was sweeping her away from his hold.

===Chinese===
他感受到那隐含的安慰与同情——带着一丝良心不安，并不完全确定自己值得。但若非严格的部门规定，他本应认为志愿参军、承担他人争相面对的危险是他的责任；但他没有像他的下级卡西迪那样激烈咒骂，当同样亵渎的霍勒斯冲进房间宣布征募被禁止时。他想起卡西迪愤怒的蓝眼睛，他发誓无论如何都要进入航空队。……菲莉达会同情卡西迪，她眼神的闪光回应了他的眼神；那一刻，她也与群体生活合一，有时他觉得它正将她从他的掌控中卷走。

===Original===
He felt it most on their last evening, on the night the ultimatum expired; when he came from the office, after hours of overtime, uncertain whether he should find her, wondering whether her excited restlessness had driven her out into the crowds that surged round Whitehall. As he ran up the stairs the sound of a piano drifted from the room above; no definite melody but a vague, irregular striking of chords that came to an end as he entered the room and Phillida looked up, expectant.

===Chinese===
他在他们最后一个夜晚感受最深，就在最后通牒到期的那个晚上；当他从办公室出来，经过数小时加班后，不确定是否能找到她，不知道她激动不安是否已将她推向白厅周围涌动的人群。当他跑上楼时，楼上传来钢琴声；没有明确的旋律，只有模糊、不规则的和弦敲击，当他走进房间，菲莉达抬起头，期待着。

===Original===
“At last,” she said as she ran to him. “You don’t know how I have wanted you. I can’t be alone﻿—if you hadn’t turned up I should have had to find someone to talk to.”

===Chinese===
“终于，”她跑向他说。“你不知道我有多想你。我不能一个人待着——如果你没来，我就得找个人说话。”

===Original===
“Anyone﻿—didn’t matter who?” he suggested.

===Chinese===
“任何人——不管是谁？”他暗示道。

===Original===
She laughed, caught his hand and rubbed her cheek against it. “Yes, anyone﻿—you know what I mean. It’s just﻿—when you think of what’s happening, how can you keep still?﻿ ﻿… As for father, I never see him nowadays. I suppose there isn’t any news?”

===Chinese===
她笑了，抓住他的手，用脸颊蹭着它。“是的，任何人——你知道我的意思。只是——当你想到正在发生的事，你怎么能静得下心？……至于父亲，我现在都见不到他了。我猜没有什么新消息吧？”

===Original===
“There can’t be,” he answered. “Not till twelve.”

===Chinese===
“不可能有，”他回答。“要到十二点。”

===Original===
“No﻿—and even at twelve it won’t really be news. Just no answer﻿—and the time will be up.﻿ ﻿… We’re at peace now﻿—till midnight.﻿ ﻿… What’s the time?”

===Chinese===
“不——即使到十二点也不会真有新闻。只是没有答复——而时间就到了。……我们现在是和平的——直到午夜。……现在几点了？”

===Original===
He longed to be alone with her﻿—alone with her in thought as well as in outward seeming﻿—but her talk slipped restlessly away from his leading and she moved uncertainly about the room, returning at last to her vague striking of the piano﻿—sharp, isolated notes, and then suddenly a masterful chord.

===Chinese===
他渴望与她独处——不仅是外表上的独处，更是思想上的独处——但她不安的谈话偏离了他的引导，她在房间里不确定地走动，最后又回到她模糊的钢琴弹奏——尖锐、孤立的音符，然后突然一个有力的和弦。

===Original===
“Play to me,” he asked, “play properly.”

===Chinese===
“为我弹，”他请求道，“好好弹。”

===Original===
She shook her head and declared it was impossible.

===Chinese===
她摇摇头，说这是不可能的。

===Original===
“Anything connected is beyond me; I can only strum and make noises.” She crashed in the bass, rushed a swift arpeggio to the treble, then turned to him, her eyes wide and glowing. “If you hold your breath, can’t you feel them all waiting?﻿—thousands on thousands﻿—all through the world?﻿ ﻿… Waiting till midnight﻿ ﻿… can’t you feel it?”

===Chinese===
“任何连贯的东西我都弹不了；我只能乱弹乱敲。”她在低音区猛击，快速弹奏一串琶音到高音区，然后转向他，眼睛睁得大大的，闪闪发光。“如果你屏住呼吸，你感觉不到他们都在等待吗？——成千上万——全世界？……等待到午夜……你感觉不到吗？”

===Original===
“You make me feel it,” he answered. “Tell me﻿—you want war?”

===Chinese===
“你让我感觉到了，”他回答。“告诉我——你想要战争吗？”

===Original===
The last words came out involuntarily, and it was only the startled, sudden change in her face that brought home to him what he had said.

===Chinese===
最后几个字不由自主地说出口，只有她脸上惊讶、突然的变化才让他意识到自己说了什么。

===Original===
“I want war,” she echoed.﻿ ﻿… “I want men to be killed.﻿ ﻿… Theodore, what makes you say that?”

===Chinese===
“我想要战争，”她重复道。……“我想要男人被杀。……西奥多，你怎么会这么说？”

===Original===
He fumbled for words, not sure of his own meaning﻿—sure only that her eyes would change and lose their fervour if, at the last moment and by God-sent miracle, the sword were returned to its sheath.

===Chinese===
他笨拙地寻找词语，不确定自己的意思——只确信如果在最后一刻，通过上帝赐予的奇迹，剑被收回鞘中，她的眼睛会改变并失去热情。

===Original===
“Not that, of course﻿—not the actual fighting. I didn’t mean that.﻿ ﻿… But isn’t there something in you﻿—in you and in everyone﻿—that’s too strong to be arrested? Too swift?﻿ ﻿… If nothing happened﻿—if we drew back﻿—you couldn’t be still now; you couldn’t endure it.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“当然不是那个——不是真正的战斗。我不是那个意思。……但你内心——你和每个人内心——不是有些东西强大到无法停止吗？太迅速了？……如果什么都没发生——如果我们退缩——你现在无法静止；你无法忍受。……”

===Original===
She looked at him thoughtfully, puzzled, half-assenting; then protested again: “I don’t want it﻿—but we can’t be still and endure evil.”

===Chinese===
她若有所思地看着他，困惑，半是赞同；然后再次抗议道：“我不想要它——但我们不能静坐忍受邪恶。”

===Original===
“No,” he said, “we can’t﻿—but isn’t there a gladness in the thought that we can’t?”

===Chinese===
“不，”他说，“我们不能——但想到我们不能，不是有一种喜悦吗？”

===Original===
“Because we’re right,” she flashed. “It’s not selfish﻿—you know it isn’t selfish. We see what is right and, whatever it costs us, we stand for it. The greatest gladness of all is the gladness of giving﻿—everything, even life.﻿ ﻿… That’s what makes me wish I were a man!”

===Chinese===
“因为我们是对的，”她脱口而出。“这不是自私——你知道这不是自私。我们看到什么是正确的，无论代价如何，我们都为之奋斗。最大的喜悦是给予的喜悦——一切，甚至生命。……这就是为什么我希望自己是个男人！”

===Original===
“The passion for self-sacrifice,” he said, quoting Markham. “I was told the other day it was one of the causes of war.﻿ ﻿… Don’t look at me so reproachfully﻿—I’m not a pacifist. Give me a kiss and believe me.”

===Chinese===
“自我牺牲的激情，”他说，引用马卡姆的话。“前几天我被告知这是战争的原因之一。……别那么责备地看着我——我不是和平主义者。给我一个吻，相信我。”

===Original===
She laughed and gave him the kiss he asked for, and for a minute or two he drew her out of the crowd-life and they were alone together as they had been on the night of their betrothal. Then the spirit of restlessness took hold of her again and she rose suddenly, declaring they must find out what was happening﻿—they must go out and see for themselves.

===Chinese===
她笑了，给了他要求的吻，有一两分钟，他将她从群体生活中拉出来，他们独处在一起，就像订婚那晚一样。然后不安的精神再次抓住了她，她突然站起，宣称他们必须弄清楚发生了什么——他们必须出去亲眼看看。

===Original===
“It’s only just past ten,” he argued. “What can be happening for another two hours? There’ll only be a crowd﻿—walking up and down and waiting.”

===Chinese===
“才刚过十点，”他争辩道。“接下来两个小时能发生什么？只会有一群人——走来走去等待着。”

===Original===
It was just the crowd and its going to and fro that she needed, and she set to work to coax him out of his reluctance. There would never be another night like this one﻿—they must see it together and remember it as long as they lived.﻿ ﻿… Perhaps, her point gained, she was remorseful, for she rewarded his assent with a caress and a coaxing apology.

===Chinese===
她需要的正是这群人及其来来往往，她开始哄劝他克服不情愿。再也不会有像今晚这样的夜晚了——他们必须一起见证，终生铭记。……也许，达到目的后，她有些懊悔，因为她用爱抚和哄劝的道歉回报了他的同意。

===Original===
“We shall have so many evenings to ourselves,” she told him﻿—“and tonight﻿—tonight we don’t only belong to ourselves.”

===Chinese===
“我们将有很多个夜晚属于我们自己，”她告诉他——“而今晚——今晚我们不仅仅属于彼此。”

===Original===
He could feel her arm tremble and thrill on his own as they came in sight of the Clock Tower and the swarm of expectant humanity that moved and murmured round Westminster. On him the first impression was of seething insignificance that the Clock Tower dwarfed and the dignity of night reproved; on her, as he knew by the trembling of her fingers, a quickening of life and sensation.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
当他看到大本钟和威斯敏斯特周围涌动、低语的期待人群时，他能感觉到她的手臂在他手臂上颤抖和悸动。对他来说，第一印象是沸腾的微不足道，大本钟将其矮化，夜晚的庄严加以责备；对她，正如他从她手指的颤抖所知，是生命与感觉的加速。……

===Original===
They were still at the shifting edges of the crowd when a man’s voice called “Phillida!” and one of her undergraduate cousins linked himself on to their company. For nearly an hour the three moved backwards and forwards﻿—through the hum and mutter of voices, the ceaseless turning of eyes to Big Ben and the shuffling of innumerable feet.﻿ ﻿… When the quarters chimed, there was always a hush; when eleven throbbed solemnly, no man stirred till the last beat died.﻿ ﻿… With silence and arrested movement the massed humanity at the base of the Clock Tower was no longer a seething insignificance; without speech, without motion, it was suddenly dignified﻿—life faced with its destiny and intent upon a Moving Finger.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
他们仍在人群流动的边缘，这时一个男人的声音喊道“菲莉达！”她的一位本科生表弟加入了他们的行列。将近一个小时里，三人来回走动——穿过嗡嗡低语的人群、眼睛不停转向大本钟的注视、以及无数双脚的拖沓。……当刻钟敲响时，总是一片寂静；当十一点庄严地颤动时，直到最后一声消逝，无人移动。……随着寂静和静止的动作，聚集在大本钟底部的人群不再是沸腾的微不足道；没有言语，没有动作，它突然变得庄严——生命面对它的命运，专注于一只移动的手指。……

===Original===
“Only one more hour,” whispered Phillida as the silence broke; and the Rathbone boy, to show he was not moved, wondered if it was worth their while to stay pottering about for an hour?﻿ ﻿… No one answered his question, since it needed no answer; and, the dignity of silence over, they drifted again with the crowd.

===Chinese===
“只剩一个小时了，”沉默打破时，菲莉达低语道；拉思伯恩家的男孩，为了表示自己没被触动，想知道他们是否值得再晃荡一个小时？……没人回答他的问题，因为无需回答；随着寂静的庄严结束，他们再次随人群流动。

## IV / 第四章

===Original===
The Moving Finger had written off another five minutes or so when police were suddenly active and sections of the crowd lunged uncomfortably; way was being made for the passing of an official car—and in the backward swirl of packed humanity Theodore was thrust one way, Phillida and the Rathbone boy another. For a moment he saw them as they looked round and beckoned him; the next, the swirl had carried him yet further—and when it receded they were lost amongst the drifting, shifting thousands. After ten minutes more of pushing to and fro in search of them, Theodore gave up the chase as fruitless and made his way disconsolately to the Westminster edge of the crowd.… Phillida, if he knew her, would stay till the stroke of midnight, later if the spirit moved her; and she had an escort in the Rathbone boy, who, in due time, would see her home.… There was no need to worry—but he cursed the luck of what might be their last evening.

For a time he lingered uncertainly on the edge of the pushing, shuffling mass; perhaps would have lingered till the hour struck, if there had not drifted to his memory the evening at Vallance's when Holt had declared this night to be impossible—and when Markham had "made it war." And, with that, he remembered also that Markham had rooms nearby—in one of the turnings off Great Smith Street.

===Chinese===
移动的指尖（Moving Finger）又划去了大约五分钟光景，警察突然行动起来，人群的一部分令人难受地猛挤过来；人们正为一辆公务车的通过让路——在拥挤人群的后退旋涡中，西奥多被推向一边，菲莉达和拉思伯恩（Rathbone）家的男孩则被推向另一边。有那么一瞬间，他看见他们回头张望、向他招手；紧接着，旋涡又将他推得更远——待旋涡退去时，他们已消融在漂移变幻的万千人潮中。他又推搡着寻找了十分钟，终于放弃这徒劳的追逐，闷闷不乐地挤到人群的威斯敏斯特一侧。……菲莉达，他了解她，会一直待到午夜钟响，如果兴致来了还会更晚；何况有拉思伯恩家的男孩做伴，到时候会送她回家。……用不着担心——但他诅咒那该死的运气，这也许是他们最后一个夜晚了。

他在推推搡搡的人群边缘踟蹰了一阵；若不是瓦兰斯（Vallance）家那个夜晚的记忆飘进脑海——霍尔特（Holt）曾断言今夜不可能，马卡姆（Markham）则说"就当是战争吧"——他或许会一直待到钟声敲响。与此同时，他也想起马卡姆就住在附近——大史密斯街（Great Smith Street）某个拐角处。

---

===Original===
There was a light in the room that he knew for Markham's and it was only after he had rung that he wondered what had urged him to come. He was still wondering when the door opened and could think of no better explanation than "I saw you were up—by your light."

"If you'd passed five minutes ago," said Markham, as he led the way upstairs, "you wouldn't have seen any light. I'm only just back from the lab—and dining off biscuits and whisky."

"Is this making any difference to you, then?" Theodore asked. "I mean, in the way of work?"

Markham nodded as he poured out his visitor's whisky. "Yes, I'm serving the country—the military people have taken me over, lock and stock: with everyone else, apparently, who has ever done chemical research. I've been pretty hard at it the last few days, ever since the scare was serious.… And you—are you soldiering?"

"No," said Theodore and told him of the departmental prohibition.

"It mayn't make much difference in the end," said Markham.… "You see, I was right—the other evening."

"Yes," Theodore answered, "I believe that was why I came in. The crowd tonight reminded me of what you said at Vallance's—though I don't think I believed you then.… How long is it going to last?"

"God knows," said Markham, with his mouth full of biscuit. "We shall have had enough of it—both sides—before very long; but it's one thing to march into hell with your head up and another to find a way out.… There's only one thing I'm fairly certain about—I ought to have been strangled at birth."

Theodore stared at him, not sure he had caught the last words.

"You ought to—?"

"Yes—you heard me right. If the human animal must fight—and nothing seems to stop it—it should kill off its scientific men. Stamp out the race of 'em, forbid it to exist.… Holt was also right that evening, fundamentally. You can't combine the practice of science and the art of war; in the end, it's one or the other. We, I think, are going to prove that—very definitely."

"And when you've proved it—we stop fighting?"

Markham shrugged his shoulders, thrust aside his plate and filled his pipe.

"Curious, the failure to understand the influence on ourselves of what we make and use. We just make and use and damn the consequence.… When Lavoisier invented the chemical balance, did he stop to consider the possibilities of chemical action in combination with outbursts of human emotion? If he had…!"

In the silence that followed they heard the chiming of three-quarters—and there flashed inconsequently into Theodore's memory, a vision of himself, a small boy with his hand in his mother's, staring up, round-eyed, at Big Ben of London—while his mother taught him the words that were fitted to the chime.

> Lord—through—this—hour
 
 Be—Thou—our—guide,
 
 So—by—Thy—power
 
 No—foot—shall—slide.

… That, or something like that.… Odd, that he should remember them now—when for years he had not remembered.… "Lord—through—this—hour—"

===Chinese===
那个房间亮着灯，他知道那是马卡姆的住处；按了门铃之后，他才纳闷起是什么驱使自己来到这里。门开了，他仍在纳闷，只能想出一句解释："我看见你亮着灯——就上来了。"

"你要是早来五分钟，"马卡姆一边领他上楼一边说，"就看不到灯了。我刚从实验室回来——拿饼干和威士忌当晚餐。"

"那这件事对你有什么影响吗？"西奥多问。"我是说，对你的工作？"

马卡姆给客人倒上威士忌，点了点头。"有，我在为国效力——军方把我整个人连锅端了：显然，所有搞过化学研究的人都一样。过去这几天我一直拼命干，自从局势真正严峻起来以后。……你呢——你在当兵吗？"

"没有，"西奥多说，并告诉他部门的禁令。

"最终可能也差不多，"马卡姆说。……"你看，那天晚上我是对的。"

"对，"西奥多答道，"我想这就是我今晚过来的原因。今晚的人群让我想起了你在瓦兰斯家说的那些话——虽然我觉得当时我并不相信你。……这场仗会打多久？"

"天知道，"马卡姆嘴里塞满了饼干说。"双方很快都会受够的——但昂首阔步走进地狱是一回事，找到出路又是另一回事。……我只相当确定一件事——我本该在出生时就被扼死的。"

西奥多盯着他，不确定自己是否听清了最后几个字。

"你本该——？"

"对——你没听错。如果人类这种动物非要打仗——似乎没什么能阻止它——那就应该把搞科学的人全部杀光。把这一族人彻底铲除，禁止其存在。……霍尔特那天晚上从根本上说也是对的。科学实践与战争艺术不可兼得；归根结底，只能取其一。我觉得，我们将要非常明确地证明这一点。"

"等你证明了——我们就不打了？"

马卡姆耸了耸肩，把盘子推到一边，往烟斗里装着烟丝。

"奇怪的是，人们竟然不明白我们所制造和使用的东西反过来对我们自身的影响。我们只管制造和使用，管它什么后果。……拉瓦锡（Lavoisier）发明化学天平时，他有没有停下来想过化学反应与人类情感爆发结合在一起的可能性？如果他想过……！"

沉默中，他们听到了三点三刻的钟声——一个毫不相干的画面突然闪入西奥多的记忆：他自己，一个小男孩，牵着母亲的手，圆睁着眼睛仰望伦敦的大本钟（Big Ben）——母亲正在教他配合钟声的那几句词。

> 主啊——引领——此时此刻
 
 作——我们——的引导，
 
 凭——你——大能
 
 脚步——不致——滑跌。

……就是那首，或者差不多的。……奇怪，他竟然在此刻记起了那些——已经多年不曾记起。……"主啊——引领——此时此刻——"

---

===Original===
He realized suddenly that Markham was speaking—in jerks, between pulls at his pipe. "… And the same with mechanics—not the engine but the engine plus humanity. Take young James Watt and his interest in the lid of a teakettle! In France, by the way, they tell the same story of Papin; but, so far as the rest of us are concerned it doesn't much matter who first watched the lid of a kettle with intelligence—the point is that somebody watched it and saw certain of its latent possibilities. Only its more immediate possibilities—and we may take it for granted that amongst those which he did not foresee were the most important. The industrial system—the drawing of men into crowds where they might feed the machine and be fed by it—the shrinkage of the world through the use of mechanical transport. That—the shrinkage—when we first saw it coming, we took to mean union of peoples and the clasping of distant hands—forgetting that it also meant the cutting of distant throats.… Yet it might have struck us that we are all potential combatants—and the only known method of preventing a fight is to keep the combatants apart! These odd, simple facts that we all of us know—and lose sight of… the drawing together of peoples has always meant the clashing of their interests… and so new hatreds. Inevitably new hatreds."

Theodore quoted: "'All men hate each other naturally.'… You believe that?"

"Of individuals, no—but of all communities, yes. Is there any form of the life collective that is capable of love for its fellow—for another community? Is there any church that will stand aside that another church may be advantaged?… You and I are civilized, as man and man; but collectively we are part of a life whose only standard and motive is self-interest, its own advantage… a beast-life, morally. If you understand that, you understand tonight.… Which demands from us sacrifices, makes none itself.… That's as far as we have got in the mass."

Through the half-open window came the hum and murmur of the crowd that waited for the hour.… Theodore stirred restlessly, conscious of the unseen turning of countless faces to the clock—and aware, through the murmur, of the frenzied little beating of his watch.… He hesitated to look at it—and when he drew it out and said "Five minutes more," his voice sounded oddly in his ears.

"Five minutes," said Markham.… He laughed suddenly and pushed the bottle across the table. "Do you know where we are now—you and I and all of us? On the crest of the centuries. They've carried us a long roll upwards and now here we are—on top! In five more minutes—three hundred little seconds—we shall hear the crest curl over.… Meanwhile, have a drink!"

He checked himself and held up a finger. "Your watch is slow!"

The hum and murmur of the crowd had ceased and through silence unbroken came the prayer of the Westminster chime.

> Lord—through—this—hour
 
 Be—Thou—our—guide,
 
 So—by—Thy—power
 
 No—foot—shall—slide.

There was no other sound for the twelve booming strokes of the hour: it was only as the last beat quivered into silence that there broke the moving thunder of a multitude.

"Over!" said Markham. "Hear it crash?… Well, here's to the centuries—after all, they did the best they knew for us!"

===Chinese===
他突然意识到马卡姆正在说话——断断续续的，一边从烟斗里吸着烟。"……机械也是一样——不是引擎本身的问题，而是引擎加上人性。想想年轻的詹姆斯·瓦特（James Watt）对茶壶盖子的兴趣！顺便说一句，在法国，同样的故事讲的是帕平（Papin）；但对我们其他人来说，到底是谁第一个带着智慧注视茶壶的盖子并不那么重要——关键在于有人注视了，并且看到了它某些潜在的可能性。只是那些比较直接的可能性——我们可以想当然地认为，在他没有预见到的可能性中，最重要的恰恰被遗漏了。工业体系——把人们聚集到一起，让他们去喂养机器、也被机器喂养——机械运输的使用带来的世界缩小。那个——缩小——当我们第一次看到它来临时，我们以为它意味着民族的联合和远方之手的相握——却忘了它也意味着远方之喉的割断。……然而我们本该想到，我们所有人都是潜在的斗士——而已知的唯一防止打架的方法，就是把斗士们隔开！这些奇怪的、简单的事实，我们都知道——却又视而不见……人们聚到一起，总是意味着利益的冲突……于是新的仇恨。必然的新的仇恨。"

西奥多引述道："'所有人天然互相仇恨。'……你相信这个？"

"对个体，不——但对所有群体，信。集体生活有任何一种形式能够对其同类——对另一个群体——怀有爱意吗？有任何教会会为了另一个教会的利益而退让吗？……你和我，作为人与人之间，是文明的；但作为一个整体，我们是一种生活的一部分，其唯一的标准和动机是自利，自身的利益……道德上的兽性生活。如果你理解了这个，你就理解了今晚。……它向我们索取牺牲，自己却从不付出。……我们大众就到了这一步。"

半掩的窗外传来等待钟声的人群的嗡嗡低语。……西奥多不安地动了动，感觉到无数张面孔虽看不见却在转向时钟——而透过那嗡嗡声，他意识到了自己腕表那急促而微弱的跳动。……他犹豫着要不要看它——当他掏出表说出"还有五分钟"时，自己的声音在他耳中听来颇为陌生。

"五分钟，"马卡姆说。……他突然笑起来，把酒瓶推过桌子。"你知道我们现在在哪里吗——你和我，我们所有人？在世纪的巅峰之上。它们把我们一路推了上来，现在我们到了——在顶上！再过五分钟——三百个小秒——我们将听到巅峰翻卷过去的声音。……来，喝一杯！"

他猛然打住，竖起一根手指。"你的表慢了！"

人群的嗡嗡低语已经停止，一片无声的寂静中传来威斯敏斯特钟楼的祈祷钟声。

> 主啊——引领——此时此刻
 
 作——我们——的引导，
 
 凭——你——大能
 
 脚步——不致——滑跌。

十二下洪亮的报时声中再无其他声响：直到最后一下余音颤动着归于寂静，万千人群才爆发出震撼的雷鸣般的欢呼。

"结束了！"马卡姆说。"听到了吗，那崩塌之声？……好吧，敬世纪们一杯——不管怎么说，它们已尽其所能了！"

## IX

===Original===
It was somewhere towards the end of autumn that Theodore Savage realized that the war had come to an end so far, at least, as his immediate England was concerned. What was happening elsewhere he and his immediate England had no means of knowing and were long past caring to know. There was no definite ending but a leaving-off, a slackening; the attacks the burnings and panics by degrees were fewer and not only fewer but less devastating, because carried out with smaller forces; there were days and nights without alarm, without smoke cloud or glow on the horizon. Then yet longer intervals and so on to complete cessation. By the time the nights had grown long and frosty the war that was organized and alien had ended; there remained only the daily, personal and barbaric form of war wherein every man's hand was raised against his neighbour and enemy. That warfare ceased not and could not cease until the human herd had reduced itself to the point at which the bare earth could support it.

It seemed to him later a wonder almost a miracle that he had come alive through the months of war and after; at times he stood amazed that any had lived in the waste of hunger and violence, of pestilence and rotting bodies which for months was the world as he knew it. He was near death not once nor a score of times, but daily; death from exhaustion or the envy of men who were starved and reckless as himself. The mockery of peace brought no plenty or hope of it, no sign of reconstruction or dawn of new order; reconstruction and order were rank impossibilities so long as human creatures preyed on each other in a land swept bare, and prowled after the manner of wolves. No revival of common life, no system was possible until earth once more brought forth her fruits.

He judged, by the length of the nights, that it was somewhere about the middle of November when the first snow came suddenly and thickly; the harbinger and onslaught of a fiercely hard winter that killed in their thousands the gaunt human beasts who tore at each other for the refuse and vermin that was food. In the all-pervading dearth and starvation there was only one form of animal life that increased and flourished mightily; the rat overran empty buildings, found dreadful sustenance in street and field and, in turn, was hunted, trapped and fed on.
