In the beginning there was relief that they had come through alive; but, with the passing of the first instinct of relief, came understanding of the meaning of being alive. The breath in their bodies, the knowledge that they still walked the earth: and for the rest, vagrancy and beast-right—the right of the strongest to live!

They took counsel together as the night crept over them and—because there was nothing else to do—planned to search the charred ruin as the fire died out, in the hope of salvage from the camp. They counted such few, odd possessions as remained to them: cartridge belts, rifles thrown away in flight and then picked up in the road, the contents of their pockets—no more. In the end, for the most part, they slept the dead sleep of exhaustion till morning—to wake with cold rain on their faces.

===Chinese===
路上他遇到了其他人，那些和他一样摸黑往回走的身影；渐渐地，二十来个人聚到路中央，分成几个小群站着，有的喃喃自语，有的沉默不语，看着火焰渐渐熄灭。路上和路边横陈着尸体——都是逃跑时从上方被射杀的人；活着的人翻动他们，查看那些扭曲的面孔。没有人指挥；他们的指挥官在第一颗炸弹爆炸时当场阵亡，一个中尉蜷缩在路上，只剩下呼吸。他们知道的就这么多。起初他们为自己活下来而庆幸；但最初的庆幸过去后，他们开始明白活着意味着什么。胸腔里尚有气息，双脚仍踏在大地上：除此之外，便是流浪和兽的权利——最强者生存的权利！

夜色悄悄笼罩下来，他们聚在一起商议——因为别无他法——计划等火势减弱后搜寻烧焦的废墟，希望能从营地里抢救出些什么。他们清点了所剩无几的零碎家当：子弹带、逃跑时扔掉又在路上捡回的步枪、口袋里的东西——仅此而已。最后大多数人沉入了精疲力竭的死睡直到清晨——醒来时脸上淋着冰冷的雨水。

===Original===
The rain, for all its wretchedness to men without shelter, was so far their friend that it beat down the flames on the smouldering timbers which were all that remained of their fortress and rock of defence. They burrowed feverishly among the black wreckage of their store-sheds, blistering and burning their fingers by too eager handling of logs that still flickered, unearthing, now and then, some scrap of charred meat but, for the most part, nothing but lumps of molten metal that had once been the tins containing food. In their pressing anxiety to avert the peril of hunger they were heedless of a peril yet greater; their search had attracted the attention of others—scarecrow vagrants, the rabble of the roads, who saw them from a distance and came hurrying in the hope of treasure-trove. The first single spies retreated at the order of superior and disciplined numbers; but with time their own numbers were swollen by those who halted at the rumour of food, and there hovered round the searchers a shifting, snarling, envious crowd that drew gradually nearer till faced with the threat of pointed rifles. Even that only stayed it for a little—and, spurred on by hunger, imagining riches where none existed, it rushed suddenly forward in a mob that might not be held.

Those who had rifles fired at it and men in the foremost ranks went down, unheeded in the rush of their fellows; those who might have hesitated were thrust forward by the frantic need behind, and the torrent of misery broke against the little group of soldiers in a tumult of grappling and screeching. Women, like men, asserted their beast-right to food—when sticks and knives failed them, asserted it with claws and teeth; unhuman creatures, with eyes distended and wide, yelling mouths, went down with their fingers at each other's throats, their nails in each other's flesh. Theodore clubbed a length of burnt wood and struck out saw a man drop with a broken, bloody face and a woman back from him shrieking then was gripped from behind, with an arm round his neck, and went down. The famished creatures fought above his body and beat out his senses with their feet.

===Chinese===
雨水虽让无处遮蔽的人倍感凄苦，却在某种程度上成了他们的朋友——它浇灭了仍在闷烧的木梁上的火焰，那些木梁曾是他们的堡垒和防御之基。他们发疯似的在储藏棚的焦黑废墟中翻找，因为太急切地搬弄还在冒火星的木头而烫伤了手指，偶尔挖出一点烧焦的肉，但大多数时候只找到曾经装食物的金属罐头熔化后的团块。他们急于避免饥饿的威胁，却忽视了更大的危险；他们的搜寻吸引了其他人的注意——那些衣衫褴褛的流浪者，道路上的乌合之众，他们从远处看到这边有人，便匆匆赶来，希望能找到宝藏。最初的几个散兵游勇在对方人数占优、纪律严明的威势下退却了；但随着时间推移，那些因食物传闻而停下脚步的人不断加入，于是一群躁动不安、咆哮嫉妒的人群在搜寻者周围徘徊，渐渐逼近，直到面对步枪的威胁才暂时停下。即便这也只挡住了他们片刻——在饥饿的驱使下，幻想着根本不存在的财富，他们突然成群结队地冲了上来，势不可挡。

有步枪的人开了火，最前面的人倒下了，在同伴的冲击中无人理会；那些本可能犹豫的人被身后疯狂的需求推了上去，苦难的洪流撞击着那一小群士兵，在抓扯和尖叫声中乱成一团。女人和男人一样，以兽的权利索取食物——当棍棒和刀子不管用时，就用爪子和牙齿来争抢；那些非人的生灵，瞪大了眼睛，张着嘴尖叫，彼此掐着喉咙、抠着对方的肉倒在地上。西奥多抡起一段烧焦的木棍猛打——看见一个男人脸被打烂、满脸是血地倒下，一个女人尖叫着从他身边退开——接着被人从后面一把搂住脖子，摔倒在地。饥饿的生灵在他身体上方厮打，用脚踢得他失去知觉。

===Original===
When life came back to him the sun was very low in the west. In his head little hammers beat intolerably and all his strained body ached with bruises as he raised himself, slowly and groaning, and leaned on an arm to look round. He lay much where he had fallen, but the soldiers, the crowd of human beasts, had vanished; the bare stretch of camp, still smoking in places, was silent and almost deserted. Two or three bending and intent figures were hovering round the charred masses of wreckage—moving slowly, stopping often, peering as they walked and thrusting their hands into the ashes, in the hope of some fragment that those who searched before them had missed. A woman lay face downwards with her dead arm flung across his feet; further off were other bodies—which the searchers passed without notice. Three or four were in uniform, the bodies of men who had once been his comrades; others, for the benefit of the living, had been stripped, or half-stripped, of their clothing.

He lifted himself painfully and crawled on hands and knees, with many groans and halts, to the stream that had formed one border of the camp—where he drank, bathed his head and washed the dried blood from his scratches. With a measure of physical relief—the blessing of cool water to a burning head and throat—came a clearer understanding and, with clearer understanding, fear. He knew himself alone in chaos.

As soon as he might he limped back to the smouldering wood-heaps and accosted a woman who was grubbing in a mess of black refuse. Did she know what had become of the soldiers? Which way they had gone when they left? The woman eyed him sullenly, mistrustful and resenting his neighbourhood—knew nothing, had not seen any soldiers—and turned again to grub in her refuse. A skeleton of a man was no wiser; had only just turned off the road to search, did not know what had happened except that there must have been a fight—but it was all over when he came up. He also had seen no soldiers—only the dead ones over there. Theodore saw in their eyes that they feared him, were dreading lest he should compete with them for their possible treasure of refuse.

===Chinese===
他恢复知觉时，太阳已经西沉得很低了。小锤子在他脑袋里令人无法忍受地敲击，他抬起身子时全身淤伤都在疼痛，他呻吟着，用一只胳膊撑着环顾四周。他基本上还躺在跌倒的地方，但那些士兵——那群人形野兽——已经消失无踪；空旷的营地有些地方还在冒烟，一片死寂，几乎空无一人。两三个弯着腰、神情专注的身影在烧焦的废墟堆周围徘徊——慢慢移动，时常停下，边走边仔细查看，把手伸进灰烬里，希望能找到前人遗漏的碎片。一个女人面朝下趴着，一只僵硬的胳膊横搭在他脚上；更远处还有其他尸体——搜寻者们径直走过，毫不在意。有三四个人穿着军服，曾经是他的战友；其他人为了活人的便利，已经被剥去或半剥去衣服。

他艰难地撑起身子，手膝并用地匍匐前进，呻吟着、时走时停，来到曾构成营地边界的小溪边——在那里喝水、冲洗头部、洗掉抓伤处干涸的血迹。随着身体得到些许缓解——清凉的水对灼热的头颅和喉咙的恩赐——他有了更清醒的认识，而随着清醒认识而来的是恐惧。他知道自己在这混沌中孤身一人。

他一能走路就蹒跚地回到闷烧的木堆旁，搭讪一个正在一堆黑色垃圾中翻找的女人。她知道那些士兵怎么样了吗？他们离开时往哪个方向去了？那女人阴沉地打量他，充满戒心，厌恶他的靠近——什么也不知道，没见过什么士兵——然后又埋头翻她的垃圾。一个骷髅般的人同样一无所知；他也是刚从路上拐过来搜寻，除了知道这里肯定打过架之外，什么也不清楚——但等他来时一切都已结束了。他也没见过士兵——只看到那边的死人。西奥多从他们眼中看出他们在怕他，唯恐他会和他们争夺那可能的垃圾宝藏。

===Original===
For the time being a sickly faintness deprived him of all wish for food; he left the sullen creatures to their clawing and grubbing, went back to the water, drank and soused once more, then crept farther off in search of a softer ground to lie on. After a few score yards of painful dragging and halting, he stretched himself exhausted on a strip of dank grass at the roadside—and dozed where he fell until the morning.

With sunrise and awakening came the pangs of sharp hunger, and he dragged himself limping through mile after mile in search of the wherewithal to stay them. He was giddy with weakness and near to falling when he found his first meal in a stretch of newly-burned field—the body of a rabbit that the fire had blackened as it passed. He fell upon it, hacked it with his clasp-knife and ate half of it savagely, looking over his shoulder to see that no one watched him; the other half he thrust into his pocket to serve him for another meal. He had learned already to live furtively and hide what he possessed from the neighbours who were also his enemies. Next day he fished furtively—with a hook improvised out of twisted wire and worm-bait dug up by his clasp-knife; lurking in bushes on the riverbank, lest others, passing by, should note him and take toll by force of his catch.

===Chinese===
此时一阵令人作呕的虚弱夺去了他对食物的所有渴望；他丢下那些阴沉的家伙继续翻找，回到水边又喝了一通、冲洗了一遍，然后匍匐到更远处寻找一块较软的地面躺下。拖着疼痛的身子蹒跚了几十码后，他在路边一条潮湿的草带上筋疲力尽地躺倒——倒下便睡到天亮。

日出和苏醒带来了剧烈的饥饿感，他一瘸一拐地拖着自己走过一英里又一英里，寻找果腹之物。他虚弱得头晕目眩，几乎要倒下时，终于在一片新烧过的田野里找到了他的第一餐——一只被火经过时烧焦的兔子尸体。他扑上去，用折叠刀把它砍开，狼吞虎咽地吃掉了一半，不时回头张望，确认没人看着他；另一半塞进口袋，留着下顿吃。他已经学会了偷偷摸摸地生活，把拥有的东西藏起来，不让那些既是邻居也是敌人的人看到。第二天他又偷偷摸摸地钓鱼——用弯曲的铁丝做成鱼钩，用折叠刀挖出蚯蚓作鱼饵；他潜伏在河岸的灌木丛中，唯恐路过的人注意到他，强行从他的渔获中分一杯羹。

===Original===
He lived thenceforth as men have always lived when terror drives them this way and that, and the earth, untended, has ceased to yield her bounties; warring with his fellows and striving to outwit them for the remnant of bounty that was left. He hunted and scraped for his food like a homeless dog; when found, he carried it apart in stealth and bolted it secretly, after the fashion of a dog with his offal. In time all his mental values changed and were distorted: he saw enemies in all men, existed only to exist—that he might fill his stomach—and death affected him only when he feared it for himself. He had grown to be self-centred, confined to his body and its daily wants and that side of his nature which concerned itself with the future and the needs of others was atrophied. He had lost the power of interest in all that was not personal, material and immediate; and, as the uncounted days dragged out into weeks, even the thought of Phillida, once an ever-present agony, ceased to enter much into his daily struggle to survive. He starved and was afraid: that was all. His life was summed up in the two words, starvation and fear.

===Chinese===
从那以后，他像所有被恐惧驱赶的人一样生活——当大地无人照料、不再产出她的丰饶时；他与同伴交战，为争夺所剩无几的残羹而竭力智胜他们。他像一条丧家犬一样猎取和搜刮食物；找到后偷偷带走，像狗吃腐肉一样悄悄吞下。渐渐地，他所有的精神价值都改变了、扭曲了：他在所有人身上都看到敌人，只为活着而活着——只为填饱肚子——只有当他为自己恐惧死亡时，死亡才会影响他。他变得自我中心，局限于自己的身体和日常需求，天性中关注未来和他人需求的那一面已经萎缩。他丧失了对一切非个人、非物质、非眼前事物的兴趣；随着数不清的日子拖成数周，甚至对菲莉达（Phillida）的思念——曾经是无时不在的痛苦——也不再大量进入他每日的生存挣扎。他挨饿、恐惧：仅此而已。他的一生可以归结为两个词：饥饿与恐惧。

===Original===
At night, as a rule, he sheltered in a house or deserted farm-building that stood free for anyone to enter—sometimes alone, but as often as not in company. Starved rabble, as long as it hunted for food, avoided its rivals in the chase; but when night, perforce, brought cessation of the hunt, the herding instinct reasserted itself and lasted through the hours of darkness. As autumn sharpened, guarded fires were lit in cellars where they could not be seen from above and fed with broken furniture, with fragments of doors and palings; and one by one, human beasts would slink in and huddle down to the warmth—some uncertainly, seeking a new and untried refuge, and others returning to their shelter of the night before. The little gangs who shared fire and roof for the space of a night never ate in each other's company; food was invariably devoured apart, and those who had possessed themselves of more than an immediate supply would hide and even bury it in a secret place before they came in contact with their fellows. Hence no gang, no little herd, was permanent or contained within itself the beginnings of a social system; its members shared nothing but the hours of a night and performed no common social duties. A face became familiar because seen for a night or two in the glow of a common fire; when it vanished none knew—and none troubled to ask—whether a man had died between sunrise and sunset or whether he had drifted further off in his daily search for the means to keep life in his body. When a man died in the night, with others round him, the manner of his ending was known; otherwise he passed out of life without notice from those who yet crawled on the earth. With morning the herd of starvelings that had sheltered together broke up and foraged, each man for himself and his own cravings; rooted in fields and trampled gardens, crouched on riverbanks fishing, laid traps for vermin, ransacked shops and houses where scores had preceded them. And some, it was muttered—as time went on and the need grew yet starker—fed horribly and therefore plentifully.

===Chinese===
一般来说，晚上他会在无人看管、任何人可自由进入的房屋或废弃的农舍里栖身——有时独自一人，但往往和别人在一起。饥饿的乌合之众只要还在搜寻食物，就会在追逐中回避对手；但当夜晚迫不得已地终止了搜寻，群居本能便重新抬头，持续整个黑暗时段。随着秋意渐浓，人们在地下室——从上方看不到的地方——点燃篝火，用破门破栅栏的碎片当柴火；然后一个接一个，那些人形野兽溜进来蜷缩在火堆旁取暖——有的犹豫不决，寻找新的、未曾尝试过的栖身之所，有的则回到前夜的庇护所。那些共度一夜、分享火堆和屋顶的小团伙从不一起吃饭；食物总是在分开后才被吞下，那些手头有多余存粮的人在接触同伴之前就会把食物藏起来甚至埋到秘密的地方。因此没有任何团伙、任何小群体是持久的，也不具备社会体系的雏形；其成员除了共享一夜的时间之外什么也不共享，不履行任何共同的社会义务。一张面孔变得熟悉，只因为曾在篝火旁见过一两夜；当它消失时，没人知道——也没人费心去问——一个人是在日出和日落之间死了，还是在每日寻找维生之物的途中漂到了更远的地方。当一个人在夜里死去，身边有人时，他的死法是被知道的；否则他便悄然离世，不会引起尚在世上匍匐之人的注意。清晨一到，那些共度一夜的饥民群体便散去觅食，每人只顾自己和自己的饥肠；在田野和被践踏的花园里翻找，在河岸边蹲着钓鱼，设陷阱捕捉小动物，洗劫已有数十人捷足先登的店铺和房屋。而且有人低声议论——随着时间推移，需求变得更加赤裸——有些人吃得很可怕……因此也吃得很饱。

===Original===
There were nights—many nights—when a herd broke in panic from its shelter and scattered to the winds of heaven at an alarm of the terror overhead; and always, as starvation pressed, it dwindled—by death and the tendency to dissolve into single nomads, who (such as survived) regrouped themselves elsewhere, to scatter and regroup again. With repeated wandering—now this way, now that, as hope and hunger prompted—went all sense of direction and environment; the nomads, hunting always, drifted into broken streets or dead villages and through them to the waste of open country—not knowing where they were, in the end not caring, and turned back by a river or the sea.

The sight or suspicion of food and plunder would always draw vagrancy together in crowds; district after district untouched by an enemy had been swept out of civilized existence by the hordes which fell on the remnants of prosperity and tore them; which ransacked shops and dwellings, slaughtered sheep, horses, cattle and devoured them and, often enough, in a fury of destruction and vehement envy, set light to houses and barns lest others might fare better than themselves. But when flocks, herds and storehouses had vanished, when agriculture, like the industry of cities, had ceased to exist and nothing remained to devour and plunder, the motive for common action passed. With equality of wretchedness union was impossible, and every man's hand against his neighbour; if groups formed, here and there, of the stronger and more brutal, who joined forces for common action, they held together only for so long as their neighbours had possessions that could be wrested from them—stores of food or desirable women; once the neighbours were stripped of their all and there was nothing more to prey on, the group fell apart or its members turned on each other. In the life predatory man had ceased to be creative; in a world where no one could count on a morrow, construction and forethought had nothing meaning.

===Chinese===
有许多个夜晚——许多许多个夜晚——一个群体会因空中恐怖之物的警报而惊慌失措地逃离庇护所，四散在天风中；而且随着饥饿的压力，群体总是不断缩小——有人死去，有人倾向于脱离群体成为独行的流浪者，幸存者则在别处重新结群，然后再次分散。随着一次又一次的流浪——时而这边、时而那边，由希望和饥饿驱使——方向感和环境感完全丧失；这些永远在猎食的流浪者漂进残破的街道或死寂的村庄，穿过它们进入空旷原野的荒芜之中——不知道自己身在何处，最终也不在乎，被河流或大海挡住了去路。

食物和掠夺物的迹象或猜测总能把流浪者聚集成群；一个又一个未遭敌人蹂躏的地区被成群结队的劫掠者扫荡出文明的存在——他们扑向残存的繁荣，将其撕碎；他们洗劫店铺和住所，宰杀羊、马、牛并吞食它们，而且常常在破坏的狂怒和强烈的嫉妒中，放火烧毁房屋和谷仓，唯恐别人过得比自己好。但当畜群和仓库都消失殆尽，当农业和城市工业都不复存在，再也没有什么可吞噬和掠夺时，共同行动的动机便消失了。在同等的悲惨中联合变得不可能，人人与邻人为敌；如果各地形成了由更强壮、更凶残者组成的团伙，为共同行动而联合起来，他们也只能在邻居还有可以抢夺的财物——食物储备或可欲的女人——时维持团结；一旦邻居被洗劫一空、再无东西可掠夺，团伙便分崩离析，或者成员之间反目成仇。在掠食的生活中，人已经不再有创造力；在一个没人能指望明天的世界里，建设和远见毫无意义。

## VIII

===Original===
In a world where all were vagabond and brutal, where each met each with suspicion and all men were immersed in the intensity of their bodily needs, very few had thoughts to exchange. Mentally, as well as actually, they lived to themselves and where they did not distrust they were indifferent; the starvelings who slunk into shelter that they might huddle for the night round a common fire found little to say to one another. As human desire concentrated itself on the satisfaction of animal cravings, so human speech degenerated into mere expression of those cravings and the emotions aroused by them. Only once or twice while he starved and drifted did Theodore talk with men who sought to give expression to more than their present terrors and the immediate needs of their bodies, who used speech that was the vehicle of thought.

===Chinese===
在一个所有人都流浪野蛮的世界里，人人相互猜疑，所有人都沉浸在肉体需求的强烈渴求中，几乎没有人有思想可交流。无论精神上还是实际上，他们都各自为生，不信任的地方便是漠然；那些溜进庇护所、为了围坐在篝火旁过夜而蜷缩在一起的饥饿者，彼此几乎无话可说。当人类的欲望集中于满足兽性的渴望时，人类的言语便退化为仅仅表达那些渴望及其激起的情绪。西奥多（Theodore）在饥饿漂泊的日子里，只有一两次与那些试图表达超越当前恐惧和肉体急需之物的人交谈过——那些使用语言作为思想载体的人。

===Original===
One such he remembered—met haphazard, as all men met each other—when he sheltered for an autumn night on the outskirts of a town left derelict. With falling dusk came a sudden sharp patter of rain and he took refuge hurriedly in the nearest house—a redbrick villa, standing silent with gaping windows. What was left of the door swung loosely on its hinges—half the lower panels had been hacked away to serve as firewood; the hall was befouled with the feet of many searchers and of the furniture remained but a litter of rags and fragments that could not be burned.
He thought the place empty till he scented smoke from the basement; whereupon he crept down the stairs, soft-footed and alert, to discover that precaution was needless. There was only one occupant of the house, a man plainly dying; a livid hollow-eyed skeleton who coughed and trembled as he knelt by the grate and tried to blow damp sticks into a flame. Theodore, in his own interests, took charge of the fire, ransacked the house for inflammable material and tore up strips of broken boarding that the other was too feeble to wrestle with. When the blaze flared up, the sick man cowered to it, stretched out his hands—filthy skin-covered bones—and thanked him; whereat Theodore turned suddenly and stared. It was long—how long?—since any man had troubled to thank him; and this man, for all his verminous misery, had a voice that was educated, cultured. … Something in the tone of it—the manner—took Theodore back to the world where men ate courteously together, were companions, considered each other; and instinctively, almost without effort, he offered a share of his foraging. The offer was refused, whereat Theodore wondered still more; but the man, near death, was past desire for food and shook his head almost with repulsion. Perhaps it was the fever that had turned him against food that loosened his tongue and set him talking—or perhaps he, also, by another’s voice and manner, was reminded of his past humanity.

===Chinese===
他记得有这样一个人——像所有人相遇一样偶然碰到——当时他在一座被遗弃的城镇郊区避难过秋夜。暮色降临之际，雨点突然急促地拍打下来，他匆匆躲进最近的一座房子——一栋红砖别墅，窗户洞开，寂静无声。残存的门扇在铰链上松垮地摆动——下半部分的门板已被劈去当柴烧；门厅被许多搜寻者的脚弄脏了，家具只剩下一堆烧不掉的碎布和碎片。
他以为这地方空无一人，直到闻到地下室飘来的烟味；于是他蹑手蹑脚、警觉地走下楼梯，却发现这种谨慎是多余的。房子里只有一个人，一个显然濒死的人；一个面色铁青、眼窝深陷的骷髅，跪在壁炉前试图把潮湿的树枝吹出火焰，咳嗽着、颤抖着。西奥多出于自身利益接管了生火的活计，在房子里搜寻可燃物，撕下那个人虚弱得无力对付的破碎木板条。当火焰熊熊燃起时，病人畏缩地凑近，伸出双手——肮脏的皮包骨头——向他道谢；西奥多突然转身凝视。已经很久了——多久了？——没有人费心向他道谢；而这个人，尽管浑身虱蚤、悲惨不堪，声音却是受过教育的、有教养的。……那声音中的某种东西——那种姿态——把西奥多带回到那个人们一起彬彬有礼地用餐、彼此为伴、互相尊重的世界；他本能地、几乎不假思索地提出分给他一些搜寻来的食物。提议被拒绝了，西奥多更加惊讶；但这个人已接近死亡，对食物毫无欲望，几乎是厌恶地摇头。也许是发烧使他对食物生厌，也松开了他的舌头让他说话——也许他同样被另一个人的声音和举止唤起了对自己昔日人性的记忆。

===Original===
"'My mind to me a kingdom is,'" he quoted suddenly. "Who wrote that—do you remember?"
"No," Theodore said, "I've forgotten." He stared at the cowering, hunched figure with its shaking hands stretched to the blaze. The man, it might be, was mad as well as dying—he had met many such in his wanderings; babbling of verse as someone—who was it?—had babbled in dying of green fields.
"'My mind to me a kingdom is,'" the sick man repeated. "Well, even if we've forgotten who wrote it, there's one thing about him that's certain; he didn't know what we know—hadn't lived in our kind of hell. The place where you haven't a mind—only fear and a stomach. … The flesh and the devil—hunger and fear; they haven't left us a world! … But if there's ever a world again, I believe I shall have learned how to write. Now I know what we are—the fundamentals and the nakedness. …"

===Chinese===
"'My mind to me a kingdom is,'"他突然引用道。"谁写的——你还记得吗？"
"不，"西奥多说，"我忘了。"他凝视着那个畏缩蜷伏、颤抖的双手伸向火焰的身影。这个人也许不仅濒死而且疯了——他在流浪中遇到过许多这样的人；在临终时喃喃念着诗句，就像某个人——是谁来着？——在临终时喃喃念着绿色的田野。
"'My mind to me a kingdom is,'"病人重复道。"好吧，即使我们忘了谁写的，有件事是确定的；他不知道我们所知道的——没有活在我们这样的地狱里。那个你没有思想——只有恐惧和肚子的地方。……肉体与魔鬼——饥饿与恐惧；它们没有给我们留下一个世界！……但如果还有世界重来的一天，我相信我会学会如何写作。现在我知道我们是什么——本质与赤裸。……"

===Original===
"Were you a writer?" Theodore asked him—and at the question his old humanity stirred curiously within him.
"Yes," said the other, "I was a writer. … When I think of what I wrote—the little, little things that seemed important! … I spent a year once—a whole good year—on a book about a woman who was finding out she didn't love her husband. She was well fed and housed, lived comfortably—and I wrote of her as if she were a tragedy. The work I put into it—the work and the thought! I tried to get what I called atmosphere. … And all the time there was this in us—this raw, red thing—and I never even touched it, never guessed what we were without our habits. … Do you know where we made the mistake?"—he turned suddenly to Theodore, thrusting out a finger—"We were not civilized—it was only our habits that were civilized; but we thought they were flesh of our flesh and bone of our bone. Underneath, the beast in us was always there—lying in wait till his time came. The beast that is ourselves, that is flesh of our flesh—clothed in habits, in rags that have been torn from us."

===Chinese===
"你以前是作家吗？"西奥多问他——这个问题在他心中激起了旧日人性的奇妙悸动。
"是的，"对方说，"我是作家。……想想我写过的东西——那些看似重要的琐碎小事！……我曾花了一年——整整一年好时光——写一本关于一个女人发现她不爱丈夫的书。她吃穿不愁，住得舒适——我却把她写得像一出悲剧。我投入的工作——那些工作和思考！我试图营造我所谓的氛围。……而我们内心一直有这种东西——这种原始的、血腥的东西——我却从未触及过它，从未猜想过脱离习惯后我们究竟是什么。……你知道我们错在哪里吗？"——他突然转向西奥多，伸出一根手指——"我们并非文明——只是我们的习惯文明；但我们以为习惯就是我们的血肉。在底层，我们内心的野兽一直潜伏着——等待它的时机到来。那野兽就是我们自己，就是我们的血肉——裹在习惯里，裹在从我们身上剥下的破布里。

===Original===
He broke off to cough horribly and lay breathless and exhausted for a time; then, when breath came back to him, talked on while Theodore listened—not so much to his words as to a voice from the world that had passed.
"The religions were right," he said. "They were right through and through; the only sane thing and the only safe thing is humility—to realize your sin, to confess it and repent. … We—we were bestial and we did not know it; and when you don't even suspect you sin how can you repent and save your soul alive? … We dressed ourselves and taught ourselves the little politenesses and ceremonies which made it easy to forget that we were brutes in our hearts; we never faced our own possibilities of evil and beastliness, never confessed and repented them, took no precautions against them. Our limitless possibilities. … We thought our habits—we called them virtues—were as real and natural and ingrained as our instincts; and now what is left of our habits? When we should have been crying, 'Lord have mercy on us,' we believed in ourselves, our enlightenment and progress. Enlightenment that ended as science applied to destruction and progress that has led us—to this. … And today it has gone, every shred of it, and we're back at what we started with—hunger and lust! Brute instincts … and the primitive passion, hatred—against those who thwart hunger and lust. Nothing else—how can there be anything else? When we lost all we loved, we lost the habit and power of loving. … 'My mind to me a kingdom is'—of hatred and hunger and lust."

===Chinese===
他停下来剧烈地咳嗽，一时喘不过气、精疲力竭；然后，当他恢复呼吸时，又继续说下去，西奥多聆听着——与其说听他的话，不如说在听那个已逝世界传来的回声。
"宗教是对的，"他说。"它们彻头彻尾是对的；唯一理智和唯一安全的东西是谦卑——认识到你的罪，忏悔它，悔改。……我们——我们是兽性的，而我们不知道；当你甚至不怀疑自己有罪时，你怎么能悔改并拯救你的灵魂？……我们给自己穿上衣服，教自己那些小小的礼貌和仪式，使我们容易忘记内心是野兽；我们从不直面自己作恶和兽性的可能性，从不忏悔和悔改，也从不加以防范。我们无限的可能性。……我们以为我们的习惯——我们称之为美德——像我们的本能一样真实、自然和根深蒂固；而现在我们的习惯还剩下什么？当我们本该呼喊'主啊，怜悯我们'时，我们却相信自己，相信我们的启蒙和进步。启蒙以应用于毁灭的科学告终，进步则把我们引向——这个地步。……而今天，一切都荡然无存，我们回到了起点——饥饿与情欲！兽性的本能……以及原始的激情、仇恨——针对那些阻碍饥饿和情欲的东西。没有别的——怎么可能有别的？当我们失去所有所爱之物时，我们也失去了爱的习惯和能力。……'我的思想就是我的王国'——仇恨、饥饿和情欲的王国。

===Original===
"Yes," said Theodore—and he, too, stared at the fire. … What the other had said was truth and truth only. Even Phillida had left him; the power of loving her was gone. "I hadn't thought of it like that—but it's right. … We can only hate."
"It's that," said the dying man, "that's beyond all torment. … God pity us!"
He covered his eyes and sat silent until Theodore asked him, "Does that mean you still believe in God?"
"There's Law," said the other. "Is that God? … We have got to see into our own souls and to pay for everything we take. That's all I know, so far—except that what we think we own—owns us. That's what the wise men meant by renunciation. … It's what we made and thought we owned that has turned on us—the creatures that were born for our pleasure and power, to increase our comfort and our riches. As we made them they fastened on us—set their claws in us—and they have taken our minds from us as well as our bodies. As we made them, they followed the law of their life. We created life without a soul; but it was life and it went its own way."

===Chinese===
"是的，"西奥多说——他也凝视着火焰。……对方所说的是真理，仅仅是真理。连菲莉达（Phillida）也离开了他；爱她的力量已经消失。"我以前没这样想过——但你说得对。……我们只能恨。"
"正是这个，"垂死的人说，"超越一切折磨。……上帝怜悯我们！"
他捂住眼睛沉默地坐着，直到西奥多问他："这是说你仍然相信上帝？"
"有法则，"对方说。"那就是上帝吗？……我们必须看清自己的灵魂，为我们所拿走的一切付出代价。这就是我目前所知的一切——除了我们以为自己拥有的东西——拥有我们。这就是智者所说的禁欲的含义。……是我们制造并以为拥有的东西反噬了我们——那些为我们的愉悦和权力而生、为增进我们的舒适和财富而创造的造物。我们制造它们时，它们就附着于我们——把爪子刺入我们——夺走了我们的心智和身体。我们制造它们时，它们遵循着自己的生命法则。我们创造了没有灵魂的生命；但那确实是生命，它走上了自己的道路。

===Original===
Crouched to the fire, and between his bouts of coughing, he played with the idea and insisted on it. Everything that we made, that we thought dead and dumb, had a life that we could not control. In the case of books and art we admitted the fact, had a name for the life, called it influence: influence a form of independent existence. … In the same way we took metals and welded them, made machines; which were beasts, potent beasts, whose destiny was the same as our own. To live and develop and, developing, to turn on the power that enslaved them. … That was what had happened; they had made themselves necessary, fastened on us and, grown strong enough, had turned on their masters and killed—even though they died in the killing. The revolt against servitude had always been accounted a virtue in men and the law of all life was the same. The beasts we had made could not live without us, but they would have their revenge before they died.

===Chinese===
他蜷缩在火旁，在一阵阵咳嗽的间隙，反复思索并坚持这一观点。我们制造的一切，我们以为死寂无声的一切，都有一种我们无法控制的生命。对于书籍和艺术，我们承认了这一事实，给那种生命取了名字，称之为影响：影响是一种独立存在的形式。……同样，我们取来金属将它们焊接，制造机器；那些机器是野兽，强大的野兽，它们的命运与我们相同。活着并发展，在发展中反抗奴役它们的力量。……这就是发生的事；它们使自己变得不可或缺，附着于我们，长得足够强大后，便反叛它们的主人并杀戮——即使它们自己也在杀戮中死去。反抗奴役在人类中一直被视为一种美德，所有生命的法则都是一样的。我们制造的野兽没有我们便无法生存，但它们在死前会进行报复。

===Original===
"Think of us," he said, "how we run and squeal and hide from them! … The patient servants, our goods and chattels, who were brought into life for our pleasure—they chase us while we run and squeal and hide!"
"Yes," Theodore answered, "I've felt that, too—the humiliation."
"The humiliation," the sick man nodded. "Always in the end the slave rules his master—it's the price paid for servitude, possession. I tell you, they were wise men who preached renunciation—before what we own takes hold of us and possession turns to servitude. For there's a law of average in all things—have you ever felt it as I have? A law of balance which we never strike aright. … When the mighty tread hard enough on the humble and meek, the humble and meek are exalted and begin to tread hard in their turn. That's obvious and we've generally known it; but it's the same in what we call material things. We rise into the air—make machines that can fly—and grovel underground to protect ourselves from the flying-man. As we struck the balance to the one side, so it has to swing back on the other; a few men rise high into the air and many creep down into trenches and cellars, crouch flat. … If we could work out the numbers and heights mathematically, be sure that we should strike the perfect balance—represented by the surface of the earth. Balance—in all things balance."

===Chinese===
"想想我们，"他说，"我们如何奔跑、尖叫、躲藏！……那些耐心的仆从，我们的财物和动产，为我们的愉悦而被带入生命——它们追逐我们，而我们奔跑、尖叫、躲藏！"
"是的，"西奥多回答，"我也感受到了——那种屈辱。"
"屈辱，"病人点头道。"奴隶最终总会统治主人——这是为奴役和占有付出的代价。我告诉你，那些宣扬禁欲的人是智者——在我们拥有的东西控制我们之前，在占有变成奴役之前。因为万物皆有平均律——你曾像我一样感受到它吗？一种平衡法则，我们从未正确把握。……当强者足够用力地践踏卑微温顺之人时，卑微温顺之人便被抬高，转而开始用力践踏。这是显而易见的，我们通常都知道；但在所谓的物质方面也是一样。我们升上天空——制造能飞的机器——又匍匐到地下以保护自己免受飞行者的伤害。当我们把平衡偏向一侧时，它必须摆回另一侧；少数人高高飞上天空，许多人却匍匐到壕沟和地窖里，趴在地上。……如果我们能用数学计算出数量和高度，我们肯定能找到完美的平衡——以地表为代表。平衡——万物皆在平衡中。

===Original===
He rambled on, perhaps half-delirious, coughing out his thoughts and theories concerning a world he was leaving. … In all things balance, inevitably; the purpose of life which, so far, we sought blindly—by passion and recoil from it, by excess and consequent exhaustion. … It was in the cities where men herded, where life swarmed, that death had come most thickly, that desolation was swiftest and most complete. The ground underneath them needed rest from men; there was an average of life it could support and bear with. Now, the average exceeded, the cities lay ruined, were silent, knew the peace they had craved for—while those who once swarmed in them avoided them in fear or scattered themselves in the open country, finding no sustenance in brickwork, stone or paved street. … With the machine and its consequence, the industrial system, population had increased beyond the average allotted to the race; now the balance was righting itself by a very massacre of famine—induced by the selfsame process of invention which had fostered reproduction unhindered. Because millions too many had crawled upon earth, long stretches of earth must lie waste and desolate till the average had worked itself out. … The art of life was adjustment of the balance in all things—was action and reaction rightly applied, was provision of counterweight, discovery of the destined mean. Was control of Truth, lest it turn into a lie; was check upon the power and velocity of Good ere it swung to immeasurable Evil. …

===Chinese===
他继续漫谈，或许已半昏迷，边咳嗽边吐露着他关于正在离去的世界的思想和理论。……万物皆有平衡，不可避免；生命的目的，迄今我们仍在盲目寻求——通过激情及其反弹，通过过度及其随之而来的衰竭。……正是在人们聚集、生命蜂拥的城市里，死亡最为密集，荒凉最为迅速和彻底。他们脚下的土地需要从人类那里获得休养生息；土地所能支撑和承受的生命有一个平均值。如今，平均值被超越，城市沦为废墟，寂静无声，获得了它们曾渴求的和平——而那些曾经蜂拥其中的人们要么因恐惧而避开它们，要么散落在空旷的乡野，在砖石或铺路石中找不到维生之物。……随着机器及其后果——工业体系——人口增长已超出分配给这个种族的平均值；如今平衡正通过一场饥荒的大屠杀来自我矫正——而这场饥荒正是由曾无阻碍地促进繁殖的同一发明过程所引发。因为有数百万人多余地爬行在地球上，大片大片的土地必须荒废荒凉，直到平均值自行恢复。……生命的艺术是调整万物的平衡——是正确应用的作用与反作用，是提供平衡力，是发现注定的中庸。是控制真理，以免它变成谎言；是在善的力量和速度摆向不可估量的恶之前加以遏制。……

===Original===
The fire, for want of more wood to pile on it, had died low, to a flicker in the ashes, and the two men sat almost in darkness; the one, between the bouts that shook him, whispering out the tenets of his Law; the other, now listening, now staring back into the world that once was—and ever should be. … He was with Markham, listening to the Westminster chimes—(on the crest of the centuries, Markham had said)—when there were sudden yelping screams outside and a patter of feet on the road. The human rats who had crept into the town for shelter from the night were bolting in panic from their holes.
"They're running," said the dying man and felt towards the stairs. "It's gas—it must be gas! Oh God, where's the door—where's the door?"

===Chinese===
火堆因缺乏更多木材添柴而渐渐熄灭，只剩灰烬中的微光，两人几乎坐在黑暗中；一个在阵阵颤抖的间隙，低声诉说着他的法则信条；另一个时而聆听，时而回望那个曾经存在——并永远应该存在的世界。……他和马卡姆（Markham）在一起，聆听威斯敏斯特的钟声——（在世纪之巅，马卡姆曾说）——这时外面突然响起尖锐的叫声和路上急促的脚步声。那些为躲避夜晚而爬进城中的人类老鼠，正惊恐万状地从洞穴中逃窜。
"他们跑了，"垂死的人说着，摸索着走向楼梯。"是毒气——一定是毒气！天哪，门在哪里——门在哪里？"

===Original===
As they groped and stumbled through the door and up the stairway, he was clutching at Theodore's arm and gasping in an ecstasy of terror; as fearful of losing his few poor hours of life as if they had been years of health and usefulness. In the open air was darkness with figures flying dimly by; a thin stream of panic that raced against death by suffocation.
The man with death on him held to Theodore's arm and besought him, for Christ's sake, not to leave him—he could run if he were only helped! Theodore let him cling for a dragging pace or two; then, looking behind him, saw a woman reel, clawing the air.
He wrenched himself free and ran on till he could run no further.

===Chinese===
当他们摸索着跌跌撞撞穿过门走上楼梯时，他紧抓着西奥多的手臂，在极度恐惧中气喘吁吁；他害怕失去自己仅剩的可怜几个小时的生命，仿佛那是多年的健康和效用。在户外的黑暗中，人影模糊地飞奔而过；一股细细的恐慌洪流正与窒息死亡赛跑。
那个濒死的人抓住西奥多的手臂，恳求他看在基督的份上不要抛下他——只要有人帮一把他就能跑！西奥多让他拖着走了几步；然后回头一看，只见一个女人摇摇晃晃，双手在空中乱抓。
他挣脱开来，继续奔跑，直到再也跑不动了。

## X / X

===Original===
That night for Theodore Savage was the beginning of an odd partnership, a new phase of his life uncivilized. The girl who had clutched at him as the drowning clutch at straws was destined to bear him company for more than a winter's night and a journey to comparative safety; being by nature and training of the type that clings, as a matter of right, to whomsoever will fend for it, she drifted after him instinctively. When she woke in the morning in the shelter he had found for her she looked round for him to guide and, if possible, feed her﻿—and awaited his instructions passively.

===Chinese===
那个夜晚对西奥多·萨维奇（Theodore Savage）而言，是一段奇异伙伴关系的开端，是他未经文明化的生活的新阶段。那个像溺水者抓住稻草般紧抓住他的女孩，注定要陪伴他不止一个冬夜和一段通往相对安全之地的旅程；她天性与教养都属于那种认为依附他人是理所当然、只要有谁愿意庇护便紧随其后的人，因此她本能地跟着他。清晨在她为她找到的庇护所里醒来时，她环顾四周寻找他的指引和，如果可能的话，食物——然后被动地等待他的吩咐。

===Original===
One human being﻿—so it did not threaten him with violence﻿—was no more to him than another, and perhaps he hardly noticed that when he rose and moved on she followed. From that hour forth she was always at his heels﻿—complaining or too wretched to complain. He would let her hang on his arm as they trudged and shared his findings of food with her﻿—because she had followed, was there; and it was some time before he realized that he had shouldered a responsibility which had no intention of shifting itself from his back.﻿ ﻿… When he realized the fact he had already tacitly accepted it; and for the first few weeks of their existence in common he was too fiercely occupied in the task of keeping them both alive to consider or define his relationship to the creature who whimpered and stumbled at his heels and took scraps of food from his hands. When, at last, he considered it, the relationship was established on both sides. She was his dependent, after the fashion of a child or an accustomed dog; and having learned to look to him for food, for guidance and protection, she could be cast off only by direct cruelty and the breaking of a daily habit.

===Chinese===
一个人——只要不对他构成暴力威胁——在他眼中与另一个人并无分别，也许他几乎没有注意到，当他起身前行时，她便跟在后面。从那个时刻起，她总是尾随着他——要么抱怨，要么因太过凄苦而无力抱怨。他会在跋涉时让她挽着他的胳膊，把自己找到的食物分给她吃——因为她跟着他，在那里；过了好一阵子，他才意识到自己已经扛起了一份无意从他背上卸下的责任。……当他意识到这个事实时，他已经默认接受了它；在他们共同生活的头几个星期里，他过于激烈地忙于让两人都活下去的艰巨任务，无暇思考或界定他与那个在他脚后呜咽、跌跌撞撞、从他手中接过食物残渣的生灵之间的关系。当最终他终于思考这件事时，这种关系已在双方确立。她是他的依附者，如同一个孩子或一条习惯了主人的狗；既然她已学会指望他提供食物、指引和保护，除非施以直接的残忍并打破日常习惯，否则她无法被抛弃。

===Original===
In the beginning that was all; she followed because she did not know what else to do; he led and they hungered together. For the most part they were silent with the speechlessness of misery, and it was days before he even asked her name, weeks before he knew more of her life in the past than was betrayed by a Cockney accent. So long as existence was a craving and a fear, where nothing mattered save hunger and the fending-off of present death, the fact that she was a woman meant no more to him than her dependence and his own responsibility; thus her companionship was no more than the bodily presence of a human being whose needs were his own, whose terrors and whose enemies were his.

===Chinese===
起初仅此而已；她跟着他，因为她不知道还能做什么；他领路，他们一起挨饿。大部分时间他们因苦难的失语而沉默，过了好几天他才问她的名字，过了好几周他才知道她过去的生活比一口伦敦东区口音所透露的更多。只要生存还是饥饿与恐惧的交织，除了饥饿和抵御眼前的死亡之外一切都不重要，那么她是一个女人这一事实对他而言与她的依附和他自己的责任并无不同；因此她的陪伴不过是一个身体的存在，一个与他有着相同需求、相同恐惧、相同敌人的同类。

===Original===
They prowled and starved together through the long bitterness of winter in a world stripped bare of its last year's harvest where all hungry mouths strove to keep other mouths at a distance; and time and again, when they grubbed for food or sought to take shelter, they were driven away with threats and with violence by those who already held possession of some tract of street or country. No claim to ownership could stand against the claim of a stronger, and one man, meeting them, would avoid them, slink out of their way﻿—because, being two, they could strip him if the mood should take them. And when they, in their turn, sighted three or four figures in the distance, they made haste to take another road.

===Chinese===
他们在严酷而漫长的冬天里一起游荡、一起挨饿，这个世界已被剥去去年收成的最后一层皮，所有饥饿的嘴都在竭力将其他嘴拒之于外；一次又一次，当他们翻找食物或寻找庇护所时，被那些已经占据某片街区或乡野的人用威胁和暴力赶走。任何所有权的主张都无法对抗更强者的主张，一个人遇见他们时会避开他们，偷偷绕道——因为他们是两个人，如果他们起了念头，可以把他剥个精光。而当他们自己在远处瞥见三四个身影时，便急忙另择他路。

===Original===
Once, when a solitary wayfarer shrank from them and scuttled to the cover of a ragged patch of firwood, there came back to Theodore, like a rushing mighty wind, the memory of his last days in London, the thought of his journey down to York. The strange, glad fellowship of the outbreak of war, the eagerness to serve and be sacrificed; the friendliness of strangers, the dear love of England, the brotherhood!﻿ ﻿… The creature who scuttled at his very sight would have been his brother in those first days of splendid sacrifice!

===Chinese===
有一次，一个孤独的旅人畏缩着从他们身边逃开，匆匆钻入一片稀疏的冷杉林中寻求遮蔽，此时一股如同疾风骤雨般的记忆涌回西奥多的心头——他在伦敦最后的日子，他南下约克（York）的旅程。战争爆发时那种奇异而欢乐的同袍之情，那种渴望服役与牺牲的热忱；陌生人的友善，对英格兰深沉的爱，手足之情！……那个一见他便仓皇逃窜的家伙，在那些光荣牺牲的最初日子里本可以成为他的兄弟！

===Original===
"Lord God!" he said and laughed long and uncontrollably; while the girl, Ada, stared in open-mouthed bewilderment﻿—then pulled at his arm and began to cry, believing he was going off his head.

===Chinese===
"上帝啊！"他说着，笑了很久，无法自控；而那个女孩，艾达（Ada），张着嘴，一脸茫然地瞪着他——然后拉住他的胳膊哭了起来，以为他疯了。

===Original===
In their hunted and fugitive life their wanderings, of necessity, were planless; they drifted east or west, by this road or that, as fear, the weather or the cravings of their hunger prompted. They sought food, thought food only and, as far as possible, avoided the neighbourhood of those, their fellow-men, who might try to share their meagre findings. Houseroom, bare houseroom, stood ready for their taking in the country as well as in the town; but wherever there was more than houseroom﻿—food or the mere possibility of food﻿—the human wolf was at hand to dispute it with his rivals. There was a time when a road, followed blindly, led them down to the sea and the corpse of a pretentious little watering-place﻿—where stiff, blank terraces of ornate brick and plaster stared out at the unbroken sea-line; they found themselves shelter in a bow-windowed villa that still bore the legend "Ocean View: Apartments," trudged along the tidemark in search of sand-crabs and fished from an iron-legged pier. When a long winter gale swept the pier with breakers and put a stop to their fishing, they turned and tramped inland again.﻿ ﻿… And there was another time when they were the sole inhabitants of a stretch of Welsh mining-village﻿—they knew it for Welsh by the street-names﻿—where they hunted their rats and grubbed for roots in allotments already trampled over. For very starvation they moved on again; and later﻿—how much later they could not remember﻿—took shelter, because they could go no further, in a cottage on the outskirts of a moorland hamlet, where they were almost at extremity when a bitter spell of cold, at the end of winter, sent them food in the shape of frozen rooks and starlings. And, a day or two later, they were driven out again; Theodore, searching for dead birds in the snow, met others engaged in the same hungry quest﻿—other and earlier settlers in the neighbourhood who saw in him a poacher on their scanty hunting-grounds and, gathering together in a common hate and need, fell on the intruders and chased them out with stones and threats. Theodore and the girl were hunted from their homestead and out on to the bleakness of the moor; whence, looking back breathless and aching from their bruises, they saw half a dozen yelling starvelings who still threatened them with shouts and upraised fists.﻿ ﻿… They went on blindly because they dared not stay; and that, for many days, was the last they saw of mankind.

===Chinese===
在他们被追捕和逃亡的生活中，他们的游荡必然是无计划的；他们随恐惧、天气或饥饿的驱使，向东或向西，沿这条路或那条路漂泊。他们寻找食物，只想着食物，并尽可能避开那些可能试图分享他们微薄收获的同类——他们的同胞。容身之所，仅仅是容身之所，在乡间和城镇都唾手可得；但凡有容身之所之外的东西——食物或仅仅是食物的可能性——人狼就会出现与他的对手争夺。有一回，他们盲目地沿着一条路走到了海边，来到一座曾经浮夸的小海滨胜地的残骸前——一排排刻板而空洞的华丽砖石与灰泥露台面朝连绵不绝的海平线；他们在一栋凸窗别墅中找到了栖身之所，门上仍挂着"海景：出租"的字样，沿着潮汐线跋涉寻找沙蟹，从一座铁腿码头上钓鱼。当一场漫长的冬季风暴卷着碎浪席卷码头，迫使他们停止钓鱼时，他们便转身又向内陆跋涉而去。……还有一次，他们是威尔士一个采矿村仅有的居民——从街名他们认出那是威尔士——在那里他们捕猎老鼠，在已被踩踏过的分配地上挖掘根茎。实在饿得不行，他们再次上路；后来——多久以后他们已记不清——因为再也走不动了，便在一个荒原小村庄的外围一间小屋里栖身，就在他们几乎走投无路时，冬末的一阵酷寒送来了冻僵的秃鼻乌鸦和椋鸟作为食物。一两天后，他们又被赶了出去；西奥多在雪地里搜寻死鸟时，遇到了其他同样在进行饥饿搜寻的人——那些更早定居于此的邻人，将他视为侵入他们贫瘠猎场的偷猎者，出于共同的仇恨与需求聚集起来，袭击了这些入侵者，用石头和威胁将他们赶走。西奥多和女孩被赶出他们的家园，来到荒凉的荒原上；他们气喘吁吁、浑身伤痛地回望，看到五六个仍在叫嚣的饥民用喊叫和高举的拳头威胁着他们。……他们盲目地继续前行，因为不敢停留；而那是许多天以来，他们最后一次见到人类。

===Original===
It must have been towards the end of February or the beginning of March that they ended their long goings to and fro and found the refuge that, for many months, was to give them hiding and sustenance. Since they had been driven from their last shelter they had sighted no enemy in the shape of a living man, but the days that followed their flight had been almost foodless; and in the end they had come near to death from exposure on a stretch of hill and heath-covered country where they lost all sense of direction or even of desire. There, without doubt, they would have left their bones if there had not already been a promise of spring in the air; as it was, they could hardly drag themselves along when the moor dropped suddenly into a valley, a wide strip of land once pasture, now bleak and blackened from the passing of the poison-fire which had seared it from end to end. Here and there were charred mummies of men and of animals, lying thickest round a farmhouse, partly burned out; but beyond the burned farmhouse was a stream that might yield them fish; and with the warmth that was melting the snow on the hilltops little shafts of green life were piercing through the blackened soil. Before dark, in what once had been a garden, they scraped with their nails and their knives and found food﻿—worm-eaten roots that would once have seemed unfit for cattle, that they thrust into their mouths unwashed. They sheltered for the night within the skeleton walls of the farm; and when, with morning, they crawled into the sun, the last patch of snow had vanished from the hills and the tiny shafts of green were more radiant against the blackened soil.﻿ ﻿… The long curse and barrenness of winter was over and Nature was beginning anew her task of supporting her children.

===Chinese===
那大约是在二月底或三月初，他们终于结束了漫长的来回游荡，找到了一处将在往后数月里为他们提供遮蔽与生存的庇护所。自从被赶出上一处庇护所后，他们再未见过活着的人形敌人，但逃亡之后的日子几乎颗粒无收；最终他们几乎因暴露在一片丘陵与石楠覆盖的荒野中而死亡，在那里他们完全丧失了方向感，甚至丧失了求生的意志。毫无疑问，若非空气中已有春天的预兆，他们必会葬身于此；事实上，当荒原突然坠入一个山谷时，他们几乎已无力拖动自己的脚步——那是一片曾为牧场的宽阔地带，如今因那烧遍其每一寸土地的毒火的经过而荒凉焦黑。四处散落着人与动物的焦黑尸骸，在一座部分烧毁的农舍周围堆积最密；但在烧毁的农舍之外有一条小溪，或许能为他们提供鱼获；随着山顶积雪融化的暖意，一簇簇绿色的生命正刺破焦黑的土壤。天黑之前，在一处曾经是花园的地方，他们用指甲和刀子挖掘，找到了食物——那些曾经连牲口都不屑一顾的虫蛀根茎，他们也不洗就塞进了嘴里。他们在农舍骨架般的墙垣中过夜；当清晨他们爬到阳光下时，最后一片积雪已从山丘上消融，那一簇簇绿色的生命在焦黑的土壤映衬下更加耀眼。……漫长而荒芜的严冬已经结束，大自然重新开始了她哺育子女的使命。

===Original===
From that day forward they lived isolated, without sight or sound of men. Chance had led them to a loneliness which was safety, coupled with a bare possibility of supporting life﻿—by rooting in fields left derelict, by fishing and the snaring of birds; but for all their isolation it was long before they ceased to peer for men on the horizon, to take careful precautions against the coming of their own kind. With the memory of savagery and violence behind them, they looked round sharply at an unaccustomed sound, kept preferably to woods and shadow and moved furtively in open country; and Theodore's ultimate choice of a dwelling-place was dictated chiefly by fear of discovery and desire to remain unseen. What he sought was not only a shelter, a rooftree, but a hiding-place which other men might pass without notice; hence he settled at last in a fold of the hills﻿—in a copse of tall wood, some four or five miles from their first halt, where oaks and larches, bursting into bud, denied the ruin that had come upon last year's world.﻿ ﻿… Theodore, setting foot in the wood for the first time﻿—seeking refuge, a hiding-place to cower in﻿—was suddenly in presence of the green life unchanging, that blessed and uplifted by its very indifference to the downfall and agony of man. The windflowers, thrusting through brown leaves, were as last year's windflowers﻿—a delicate endurance that persisted.﻿ ﻿… He had entered a world that had not altered since the days when he lived as a man.

===Chinese===
从那天起，他们过着与世隔绝的生活，不见人影，不闻人声。机缘将他们引向一处孤独而安全的所在，同时也仅提供了勉强维生的可能——在荒弃的田野中翻找根茎、捕鱼和设陷捕鸟；但尽管与世隔绝，过了很久他们才不再警觉地搜寻地平线上的人影，不再小心翼翼地防备同类的靠近。身后有野蛮与暴力的记忆，他们对任何不寻常的声音都反应敏锐，宁可待在树林和阴影中，在开阔地带也行动鬼祟；西奥多最终选择居住地，主要是出于对被发现的恐惧和保持不被看见的渴望。他所寻找的不仅仅是一个遮风避雨的屋顶，更是一个旁人路过也不会注意的藏身之处；因此他最终在一处山丘的褶皱中定居——在一片高大树木的小树林里，距他们最初歇脚处约四五英里，那里的橡树和落叶松正吐出新芽，拒绝承认去年那个世界所遭受的毁灭。……西奥多第一次踏入这片树林——寻找庇护、一个可以蜷缩其中的藏身之所——突然置身于那亘古不变的绿色生命面前，它因对人类的衰亡与苦难全然漠不关心而显得格外神圣与超然。林间银莲花从褐色的落叶中破土而出，与去年的银莲花别无二致——一种纤细而持久的韧性。……他走进了一个自他作为人生活以来未曾改变的世界。

===Original===
He explored his little wood with precaution, creeping through it from end to end; and, finding no more recent sign of human occupation than a stack of sawn logs, their bark grey with mould, he decided on the site of his camp and refuge﻿—a clearing near the stream that babbled down the valley, but well hidden by its thick belt of trees. The girl had followed him﻿—she dreaded being left alone of all things﻿—and assented with her customary listlessness when he explained to her that the bird-life and the stream would mean a food-supply and that the logs, ready cut, could be built into shelters from the weather; she was a town-dweller, mentally as well as by habit of body, whom the spring of the woods had no power to rouse from her apathy.

===Chinese===
他小心翼翼地探索着他的小树林，从一端匍匐到另一端；除了那堆树皮已因霉变而发灰的锯好的原木之外，没有发现更近期的人类活动痕迹，他便确定了营地和庇护所的选址——溪流旁的一块空地，溪水在山谷中潺潺流淌，却被茂密的树丛很好地遮蔽。女孩跟着他——她最害怕独自一人——当他向她解释鸟群和溪流意味着食物来源，那些锯好的原木可以搭建防风避雨的棚屋时，她一如既往地懒洋洋地应允了；她是一个城镇居民，无论是精神上还是身体习惯上都是，树林的春天无力将她从麻木中唤醒。

===Original===
There were empty cottages for the taking lower down the valley and it was the fear of the marauder alone that sent them to camp in the wilderness, that kept them lurking in their fold of the hills, not daring to seek for greater comfort. Within a day or two after they had discovered it, they were hidden away in the solitary copse, their camp, to begin with, no more than a couple of small lean-to's﻿—logs propped against the face of a projecting rock and their interstices stuffed with green moss. In the first few weeks of their lonely life they were often near starvation; but with the passing of time food was more abundant, not only because Theodore grew more skilled in his fishing and snaring﻿—learned the haunts of birds and the likely pools for fish﻿—but because, as spring ripened, they inherited in the waste land around them a legacy of past cultivation, fruits of the earth that had sown themselves and were growing untended amidst weeds.

===Chinese===
山谷下方有现成的空置小屋可供入住，但正是对掠夺者的恐惧才将他们送入荒野中扎营，将他们困在山丘的褶皱里，不敢去寻求更大的安适。发现这处所在后一两天内，他们便藏身于这片孤寂的小树林中，他们的营地起初不过是两个简陋的斜棚——原木倚靠在一块凸出的岩石面上，缝隙中塞满了绿色的苔藓。在他们孤独生活的最初几周里，常常濒临饿死；但随着时间推移，食物变得充裕起来，不仅因为西奥多的捕鱼和设陷技术日益娴熟——摸清了鸟群的栖息地和可能有鱼的水潭——更因为随着春天成熟，他们在周围的荒地上继承了一份过去耕作的遗产，那是大地自行播种、在杂草中无人照管地生长的果实。

===Original===
With time, with experiment and returning strength, Theodore made their refuge more habitable; tools, left lying in other men's houses, fields and gardens, were to be had for the searching, and, when he had brought home a spade discovered in a weed-patch and an axe found rusting on a cottage floor, he built a clay oven that their fire might not quench in the rain and hewed wood for the bettering of their shelters. Ada﻿—when he told her where to look for it﻿—gathered moss and heather for their bed-places and spread it to dry in the sun; and from one of his more distant expeditions he returned with pots which served for cooking and the carrying of water from the stream.﻿ ﻿… Spring lengthened into summer and no man came near them; they lived only to themselves in a primitive existence which concerned itself solely with food and bodily security.

===Chinese===
随着时光推移、实验积累和体力恢复，西奥多让他们的庇护所变得更加宜居；遗留在别人房屋、田野和花园中的工具，搜寻便可找到，当他从一处杂草丛生的荒地中带回一把铁锹、从一间小屋地板上找到一把生锈的斧头后，他砌了一个泥灶，以免雨水浇灭他们的火，又劈了木材来改善他们的棚屋。艾达——当他告诉她去哪里寻找时——采集了苔藓和石楠为他们铺床，并摊开来在阳光下晾晒；从一次更远的远征中，他带回了几口锅，用来做饭和从溪流中取水。……春天渐渐延展为夏天，没有人靠近他们；他们只为彼此而活，过着一种原始的存在，唯一关心的是食物和身体的安全。

===Original===
As the days grew longer and the means of subsistence were easier to come by, Theodore would go further afield﻿—still moving cautiously over open country, but no longer expectant of onslaught. In the immediate neighbourhood of his daily haunts and hunting-grounds was no sign of human life and work save a green cart-track that ended on the outskirts of his copse; but lower down the valley were ploughed fields lapsing into weed-beds, here and there an orchard or a garden-patch and hedges that straggled as they would. Lower down again was another wide belt of burned land which, so far, he had not entered﻿—trees on either side the stream, stood gaunt and withered to the farthest limit of his sight. The district, even when alive and flourishing, had seemingly been sparsely populated; its lonely dwellings were few and far apart﻿—a farmhouse here, a clump of small cottages there, all bearing traces of the customary invasion by the hungry. Sheep-farming had been one of the local industries, and hillsides and fields were dotted with the skeletons of sheep﻿—left lying where vagabond hunger had slaughtered them and ripped the flesh from their bones.

===Chinese===
随着白昼渐长，生计的获取变得容易，西奥多会走得更远——仍然在开阔地带谨慎移动，但不再预期会遭到袭击。在他日常活动和狩猎范围的近处，除了那条终止于他小树林外围的绿色车道之外，看不到人类生活和劳作的迹象；但山谷更远处是逐渐荒废为杂草丛的犁地，间或有一处果园或菜圃，以及任意蔓延的树篱。再往下又是另一片宽阔的焦土带，迄今为止他尚未进入——溪流两岸的树木，在他目力所及的最远处，都枯瘦凋零。这片地区即便在兴旺繁荣之时，似乎也人口稀少；零星的住所稀疏而遥远——这里一座农舍，那里一簇小屋，都留有饥民惯常入侵的痕迹。牧羊曾是当地的产业之一，山坡和田野上散落着羊的骨架——被流浪的饥饿者宰杀后剥去肉，便弃置在原地。

===Original===
As the year rolled over him, Theodore came to know the earth as primitive man and the savage know it﻿—as the source of life, the storehouse of uncertain food, the teacher of cunning and an infinite and dogged patience. When the weather made wandering or fishing impossible he would sit under shelter, with his hands on his knees, passive, unimpatient, hardly moving through long hours, while he waited for the rain to cease. It was months before there stirred in him a desire for more than safety and his daily bread, before he thought of the humanity he had fled from except with fear and a shrinking curiosity as to what might be happening in the world beyond his silent hills. In his body, exhausted by starvation, was a mind exhausted and benumbed; to which only very gradually﻿—as the quiet and healing of Nature worked on him﻿—the power of speculation and outside interest returned. In the beginnings of his solitary life he still spoke little and thought little save of what was personal and physical; cut off mentally from the future as well as from the past, he was content to be relieved of the pressure of hunger and hidden from the enemy, man.

===Chinese===
随着岁月在他身上流转，西奥多开始像原始人和野蛮人那样认识大地——它是生命之源，是不确定的食物储藏室，是教会人狡黠和无限坚韧耐心的导师。当天气使游荡或捕鱼变得不可能时，他会坐在遮蔽处，双手放在膝上，被动而无焦躁，一连几个小时几乎纹丝不动，等待雨停。过了好几个月，他心中才萌生出超越安全和每日面包的渴望，才开始思考他所逃离的人类世界，但只怀着恐惧和对那沉默山丘之外世界正在发生之事的退缩式好奇。在他因饥饿而疲惫的身体里，是一个同样疲惫而麻木的心灵；只有非常缓慢地——随着大自然的宁静与疗愈在他身上发挥作用——思辨能力和对外界的兴趣才逐渐回归。在他孤独生活的最初阶段，他仍然少言寡语，除了个人的、肉体的事物之外几乎不思考；精神上与未来和过去都已隔绝，他满足于从饥饿的压迫中解脱出来，躲藏在人类——敌人——的视线之外。

## XI

===Original===
Of the woman whom chance and her own helplessness had thrown upon his hands he knew, in those first months, curiously little. She remained to him what she had been from the moment she clutched at his arm and fled with him﻿—an encumbrance for which he was responsible﻿—and as the numbness passed from his brain and he began once more to live mentally, she entered less and less into his thoughts. She was Ada Cartwright﻿—as pronounced by its owner he took the name at first for Ida﻿—ex-factory hand and dweller in the northeast of London; once vulgarly harmless in the company of like-minded gigglers, now stupefied by months of fear and hunger, bewildered and incapable in a life uncivilized that demanded of all things resource. As she ate more plentifully and lost her starved hollows, she was not without comeliness of the vacant, bouncing type; a comeliness hidden from Theodore by her tousled hair, her tattered garments and the heavy wretchedness that sulked in her eyes and turned down the corners of her mouth. She was helpless in her new surroundings, with the dazed helplessness of those who have never lived alone or bereft of the minor appliances of civilization; to Theodore, at times, she seemed half-witted, and he treated her perforce as a backward child, to be supervised constantly lest it fail in the simplest of tasks.

===Chinese===
对于那个因命运和自身无助而落到他手中的女人，在最初几个月里，他奇怪地知之甚少。她对他而言仍是那个她抓住他手臂、随他逃走时的模样——一个他负责的累赘——随着麻木从他脑中消退，他重新开始精神生活，她在他的思绪中出现得越来越少。她是艾达·卡特赖特（Ada Cartwright）——按其主人的发音，他起初把名字听成了艾达——前工厂女工，居住在伦敦东北部；曾经与同样爱笑闹的同伴一起，平凡而无害，如今被数月的恐惧和饥饿弄得麻木，在野蛮生活中困惑而无能，这种生活要求一切都要有谋略。随着她吃得更饱，饥饿造成的凹陷消失，她并非没有那种空洞、活泼型的美；但这种美被她蓬乱的头发、破烂的衣物以及眼中郁结的沉重愁苦和嘴角下垂的神情所掩盖，西奥多看不到。她在新环境中无助，带着从未独自生活或被剥夺文明小工具的人的茫然无助；对西奥多来说，有时她似乎智力低下，他不得不把她当作一个落后的孩子对待，不断监督以防她在最简单的任务上出错。

===Original===
It was his well-meant efforts to renew her scanty and disreputable wardrobe that first revealed to him something of the mind that worked behind her outward sullen apathy. In the beginning of disaster clothing had been less of a difficulty than the other necessities of life; long after food was a treasure beyond price it could often be had for the taking and, when other means of obtaining it failed, those who needed a garment would strip it from the dead, who had no more need of it. In their hidden solitude it was another matter, and they were soon hard put to it to replace the rags that hung about them; thus Theodore accounted himself greatly fortunate when, ransacking the rooms of an empty cottage, he came on a cupboard with three or four blankets which he proceeded to convert into clothing by the simple process of cutting a hole in the middle. He returned to the camp elated by his acquisition; but when he presented Ada with her improvised cloak, the girl astonished him by turning her head and bursting into noisy tears.

===Chinese===
正是他善意地努力更新她稀少而破旧的衣橱，首先向他揭示了她那在阴郁麻木外表下运作的头脑。在灾难初期，衣物不如生活的其他必需品那么困难；食物早已成为无价之宝很久之后，衣物往往唾手可得，当其他获取方式失败时，需要衣物的人会从死者身上剥下来，死者已不再需要它。在他们隐秘的孤独中，情况就不同了，他们很快难以替换挂在身上的破布；因此西奥多认为自己非常幸运，当他搜查一间空屋时，发现了一个橱柜，里面有三四条毯子，他通过在中间简单地剪一个洞的过程将它们改造成衣物。他带着收获的喜悦回到营地；但当他把临时制作的斗篷送给艾达时，女孩惊讶地转过头，突然放声大哭。

===Original===
“What’s the matter?” he asked her, bewildered. “Don’t you like it?”

===Chinese===
“怎么了？”他困惑地问她。“你不喜欢吗？”

===Original===
She made no answer but noisier tears, and when he insisted that it would keep her nice and warm her sobs rose to positive howls; he stared at her uncertainly as she sat and rocked, then knelt down beside her and began to pat and soothe, as he might have tried to soothe a child. In the end the howls diminished in volume and he obtained an explanation of the outburst﻿—an explanation given jerkily, through sniffs, and accompanied by much rubbing of eyes.

===Chinese===
她没有回答，只是哭声更大了，当他坚持说这会让她又好又暖和时，她的啜泣变成了嚎啕大哭；他不确定地看着她坐在那里摇晃，然后跪在她旁边，开始轻拍安慰，就像他试图安慰一个孩子一样。最终嚎哭声减弱了，他得到了这次爆发的解释——一个断断续续、带着抽泣、并伴随大量揉眼动作的解释。

===Original===
No, it wasn’t that she didn’t want it﻿—she did want it﻿—but it reminded her.﻿ ﻿… It was so ’ard never to ’ave anything nice to wear. Wasn’t she ever going to ’ave anything nice to wear again﻿—not ever, as long as she lived?﻿ ﻿… She supposed she’d always got to be like this! No ’airpins﻿—and straw tied round her feet instead of shoes!﻿ ﻿… Made you look as if you’d got feet like elephants﻿—and she’d always been reckoned to ’ave a small foot.﻿ ﻿… Made you wish you was dead and buried!﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
不，不是她不想要——她确实想要——但它让她想起了。……从来没有漂亮衣服穿真是太难了。她这辈子再也不会有漂亮衣服穿了吗——永远没有，只要她还活着？……她想她得一直这样下去！没有发夹——草绑在脚上代替鞋子！……让你看起来好像有大象一样的脚——而她一直被公认为有一双小脚。……让你希望自己死了埋了算了！……

===Original===
He tried two differing lines of consolation, neither particularly successful; suggesting, in the first place, that there was no one but himself to see what she looked like, and, in the second, that a blanket could be made quite becoming as a garment.

===Chinese===
他尝试了两条不同的安慰路线，都不太成功；首先建议除了他没人会看到她的样子，其次建议毯子可以做成很得体的衣服。

===Original===
“That’s a lie,” Ada told him sulkily. “You know it ain’t becoming﻿—’ow could it be? A blanket with an ’ole for the ’ead!﻿ ﻿… Might just as well ’ave no figure. Might just as well be a sack of pertaters.﻿ ﻿… I wonder what anyone would ’ave said at ’ome if I’d told ’em I should ever be dressed in a blanket with an ’ole for the ’ead!﻿ ﻿… And I always ’ad taiste in my clothes﻿—everyone said I ’ad taiste.”

===Chinese===
“那是谎话，”艾达闷闷不乐地告诉他。“你知道它不得体——它怎么会得体？一条中间有个洞的毯子！……简直就像没有身材。简直就像一袋土豆。……我想知道如果我告诉家里人我会穿一条中间有个洞的毯子，他们会说什么！……而且我一直对衣服有品味——大家都说我有品味。”

===Original===
And﻿—stirred to the soul by the memory of departed chiffon, by the hideous contrast between present squalor and former Sunday best﻿—her howls once more increased in volume and she blubbered with her head on her knee.

===Chinese===
而且——被逝去的雪纺记忆、当下褴褛与过去周日盛装的可怕对比触动灵魂——她的嚎哭声再次增大，她把头埋在膝盖上痛哭。

===Original===
Theodore gave up the attempt at consolation as useless, leaving her to weep herself out over vanished finery while he busied himself with the cooking of their evening meal; and in due time she came to the end of her stock of emotion, ceased to snuffle, ate her supper and took possession of the blanket with the ’ole for the ’ead﻿—which she wore without further complaint. The incident was over and closed; but it was not without its significance in their common life. To Theodore the tragicomic outburst was a reminder that his dependent, for all her childish helplessness, was a woman, not only a creature to be fed; while the stirrings of Ada’s personal vanity were a sign and token that she, also, was emerging from the cowed stupor of body and mind produced by long terror and starvation, that her thoughts, like her companion’s, were turning again to the human surroundings they had fled from.﻿ ﻿… Man had ceased to be only an enemy, and the first sheer relief at security attained was mingling, in both of them, with the desire to know what had come to a world that still gave no sign of its existence. Order, the beginnings of a social system (so Theodore insisted to himself) must by now have risen from the dust; but meanwhile﻿—because order restored gave no sign and the memory of humanity debased was still vivid﻿—he showed himself with caution against the skyline and went stealthily when he broke new ground. There were days when he lay on a hilltop and scanned the clear horizon, for an hour at a time, in the hope that a man would come in sight; just as there were nights, many, when he lived his past agonies over again and started from his sleep, alert and trembling, lest the footstep he had dreamed might be real. Meanwhile he made no move towards the world he had fled from﻿—waiting till it gave him a sign.

===Chinese===
西奥多放弃了无用的安慰尝试，让她为消失的华服哭个够，而他则忙着准备晚餐；适时地，她的情感库存耗尽了，不再抽泣，吃了晚餐，并拥有了那条中间有个洞的毯子——她穿上了它，没有再抱怨。事件结束了，收场了；但在他们的共同生活中并非没有意义。对西奥多来说，这悲喜交加的爆发提醒他，他的被保护者，尽管孩子气的无助，是个女人，不仅仅是需要喂养的生物；而艾达个人虚荣心的萌动是一个迹象和象征，她也正在从长期恐惧和饥饿造成的身心麻木中苏醒，她的思想，像她的同伴一样，再次转向他们逃离的人类环境。……人类不再仅仅是敌人，在获得安全后最初的纯粹解脱感，在他们两人身上，正与想了解那个仍无存在迹象的世界发生了什么的愿望混合。秩序，一个社会体系的开端（西奥多如此对自己坚持）此刻一定已从尘土中升起；但与此同时——因为恢复的秩序没有迹象，而人类堕落的记忆仍然鲜活——他谨慎地在天际线上现身，在开拓新地时偷偷摸摸。有些日子，他躺在山顶，一小时一小时地扫描清晰的地平线，希望能看到一个人出现；就像有许多个夜晚，他重温过去的痛苦，从睡梦中惊醒，警觉而颤抖，唯恐他梦见的脚步声是真实的。与此同时，他没有向他逃离的世界采取行动——等待它给他一个信号。

===Original===
If he had been alone in his wilderness, unburdened by the responsibility of Ada and her livelihood, it is probable that, before the days shortened, he would have embarked upon a journey of cautious exploration; but there was hazard in taking her, hazard in leaving her, and their safety was still too new and precious to be lightly risked for the sake of a curious adventure﻿—which might lead, with ill-luck, to discovery of their secret place and the enforced sharing of their hidden treasure of food. Further, as summer drew on towards autumn, though his haunting fear of mankind grew less, his work in his own small corner of the earth was incessant and, in preparation for the coming of winter, he put thought of distant expedition behind him and busied himself in making their huts more weatherproof, as well as roomier, in the storing of firewood under shelter from the damp, and in the gathering together of a stock of food that would not rot. He made frequent journeys﻿—sometimes alone, sometimes with Ada trudging behind him﻿—to a derelict orchard in the lower valley which supplied them plentifully with apples; he had provided himself with a wet-weather occupation in the twisting of osiers into clumsy baskets﻿—which were filled in the orchard and carried to their camping-place where they spread out the apples on dried moss.﻿ ﻿… With summer and autumn they fared well enough on the harvest of other men’s planting; and if Theodore’s crude and ignorant experiments in the storage of fruit and vegetables were failures more often than not, there remained sufficient of the bounty of harvest to help them through the scarcity of winter.

===Chinese===
如果他在荒野中独自一人，没有艾达及其生计的责任，很可能在白天变短之前，他会开始一段谨慎的探索之旅；但带她去有风险，留下她也有风险，他们的安全来之不易、弥足珍贵，不能为了一次好奇的冒险而轻易冒险——运气不好的话，可能会导致他们秘密地点被发现，被迫分享他们隐藏的食物储备。此外，随着夏天接近秋天，尽管他对人类的萦绕恐惧减少了，他在地球这个小角落的工作是不断的，为即将到来的冬天做准备，他把远行探险的念头抛在脑后，忙于使他们的棚屋更防风雨、也更宽敞，将木材存放在避潮处，并收集不会腐烂的食物储备。他频繁出行——有时独自一人，有时艾达跟在他后面跋涉——去山谷下方一个废弃的果园，那里为他们提供充足的苹果；他为自己提供了雨天的工作，将柳条编成粗糙的篮子——在果园里装满，带回他们的营地，在那里把苹果摊在干苔藓上。……在夏天和秋天，他们靠别人种植的收成过得相当好；如果西奥多在储存水果和蔬菜方面的粗陋无知实验经常失败，丰收的馈赠仍足够帮助他们度过冬天的匮乏。

===Original===
---
It was with the breaking of the next spring that there came a change into the life that he lived with Ada.

===Chinese===
随着下一个春天的到来，他与艾达的生活发生了变化。

===Original===
They had dragged through the winter in a squalid hardship that, but for the memory of a hardship more dreadful, would have seemed at times beyond bearing; often short of food, with no means of light but their fire, with damp and snow dripping through their ill-built shelters﻿—where they learned, like animals, to sleep through the long dark hours. Through all the winter months their solitude was still unbroken, and if any marauders prowled in the neighbourhood, they passed without knowledge of the hidden camp in the hills.

===Chinese===
他们在肮脏的艰难中熬过了冬天，若不是记得更可怕的艰难，有时似乎难以忍受；经常缺少食物，除了火没有其他光源，湿气和雪水从他们简陋搭建的棚屋中滴落——在那里，他们像动物一样学会在漫长的黑暗时光中入睡。整个冬天，他们的孤独仍未被打破，如果有掠夺者在附近游荡，他们并未察觉山上隐藏的营地。

===Original===
It was﻿—so far as he could guess﻿—on one of the first sunny days of March that Theodore, the spring lust of movement stirring in his blood, went further from the camp than he had as yet explored; following the stream down its valley into the wide belt of burned land, now rank with coarse grass and yellow dandelions. For an hour or so there was nothing save coarse grass, yellow dandelion and gaunt, dead trees; then a bend of the stream showed him roofs﻿—a cluster of them﻿—and instinctively he halted and crouched behind a tree before making his stealthy approach.

===Chinese===
据他猜测，是在三月最初几个晴天之一，西奥多血液中涌动着春天的移动渴望，比以往探索得更远，离开营地；顺着小溪下到山谷，进入宽阔的烧焦地带，现在长满了茂盛的粗草和黄色的蒲公英。大约一个小时，除了粗草、黄蒲公英和枯死的光秃树木外什么也没有；然后小溪的一个转弯处让他看到了屋顶——一群屋顶——他本能地停下，蹲在一棵树后，然后才偷偷靠近。

===Original===
His stealth and precaution were needless. The village from a distance might have passed for uninjured﻿—the flames that had blackened its fields had swept by it, and the houses, for the most part, stood whole; but there was no living man in the long, straggling street, no movement, save of birds and the pattering little scuffle of rats. The indifferent life of beast and bird had taken possession of the dwellings of those who once tyrannized over them; and not only of their dwellings but their bodies. At the entrance of the village half-a-dozen skeletons lay sprawled on the grass-grown road, and a robin sang jauntily from his perch on the breastbone of a man.﻿ ﻿… From one end of the street to the other the bones of men lay scattered; in the road, in gardens, on the thresholds of houses﻿—some with tattered rags still fluttering to the wind, some bare bones only, whence the flesh had festered and been gnawed. By a cottage doorstep lay two skeletons touching each other﻿—whereof one was the framework of a child; the little bones that had once been arms reached out to the death’s-head that once had bore the likeness of a woman.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
他的潜行和谨慎是多余的。从远处看，村庄可能显得完好无损——烧焦田野的火焰绕过了它，大部分房屋仍屹立不倒；但在漫长、蜿蜒的街道上没有活着的人，没有动静，只有鸟儿和老鼠的轻微脚步声。野兽和鸟类冷漠的生命占据了那些曾经统治它们的人的住所；不仅是住所，还有他们的身体。在村庄入口处，半打骨架散落在长满草的路上，一只知更鸟欢快地站在男人的胸骨上歌唱。……从街道的一端到另一端，人的骨头散落着；在路上，在花园里，在房屋门槛上——有些还挂着破布随风飘动，有些只剩光秃的骨头，肉已腐烂并被啃食。在一个农舍门阶旁，躺着两个相互接触的骨架——其中一个是一个孩子的骨架；曾经是手臂的小骨头伸向那个曾经有着女人面貌的骷髅。……

===Original===
There was a time when Theodore would have turned from the sight and fled hastily; even now, familiar though he was with the ugliness of death, his flesh stirred and crept in the presence of the grotesque litter of bones.﻿ ﻿… These people had died suddenly, in strange contorted attitudes﻿—here crouching, there outstretched with clawing fingers. Gas, he supposed﻿—a cloud of gas rolling down the street before the wind﻿—and perhaps not a soul left alive!﻿ ﻿… From an upper window hung a long, fleshless arm: someone had thrust up the casement for air and fallen half across the sill.

===Chinese===
曾几何时，西奥多会从这景象转身匆忙逃离；即使现在，尽管他熟悉死亡的丑陋，但在这一堆怪诞骨头面前，他的肉身仍在颤抖。……这些人突然死去，姿势怪异扭曲——这里蹲伏，那里伸着抓挠的手指。他想是毒气——一团毒气顺风滚过街道——也许没有一个灵魂存活下来！……从一扇上层窗户伸出一只长长的、无肉的手臂：有人推开窗户透气，半个身子倒在窗台上。

===Original===
It was the indifferent, busy chirping of the nesting birds that helped him to the courage to explore the silent street to its end. It wound, through the village and out of it, to a bridge across a river﻿—into which flowed the smaller stream he had followed since he left his refuge in the hills. From the bridge the road turned with the river and ran down the valley in a south to southeasterly direction; a road grass-grown and empty and bearing no recent trace of the life of man﻿—nothing more recent than the remains of a cart, blackened wood and rusted metal, with the bones of a horse between its shafts.

===Chinese===
正是筑巢鸟儿冷漠而忙碌的啁啾帮助他鼓起勇气探索寂静的街道直到尽头。它蜿蜒穿过村庄，通向一座横跨河流的桥——他离开山上避难所后一直跟随的小溪就汇入其中。从桥上，道路随着河流转向，在山谷中向南至东南方向延伸；一条长满草、空无一人的道路，没有人类生活的近期痕迹——没有比一辆手推车残骸更近的东西，烧黑的木头和生锈的金属，车辕之间是马的骨头。

===Original===
Below the dead village the valley opened out, the hills receded and were lower; but between them, so far as his eye could discern, the trees were still blackened and lifeless. Down either side the stream the fire-blast had swept without mercy; and, from the completeness with which the country had been seared, Theodore judged that it had been largely cornland, waving with ripe stalks at the moment of disaster and fired after days of dry weather.﻿ ﻿… All life, save the life of man, teemed in the hot March sun; the herbage thrust bravely to obliterate his handiwork, larks shrilled invisibly and lithe, dark fish were darting through the arches of the bridge.

===Chinese===
在死去的村庄下方，山谷开阔，群山退去且较低；但在它们之间，目之所及，树木仍然焦黑无生命。沿着小溪两侧，火焰无情地横扫；从这片土地被灼烧的彻底程度判断，西奥多认为这曾主要是玉米地，在灾难发生时长满成熟的茎秆，并在数天干燥天气后着火。……在三月炎热的阳光下，除了人的生命外，所有生命都繁盛；草木勇敢地挺立，要抹去他的杰作，云雀看不见地尖声鸣叫，灵活、深色的鱼在桥拱下游弋。

===Original===
He went only a yard or two beyond the end of the bridge﻿—having, as the sun warned him, reached the limit of distance he could well accomplish if he was to return to the camp by nightfall. On his way back through the village he fought with his repugnance to the grinning company of the dead and turned into one of the silent houses that stood open for any man to enter. Though the dead still dwelt there﻿—stricken down, on the day of disaster before they could reach the open air﻿—there were the usual abundant traces that living men had been there before him; the door had been forced and rooms littered and fouled in the frequent search for clothing and food. All the same, in the hugger-mugger on a kitchen floor he found treasure of string and stuffed the blanket-bag slung over his back with odds and ends of rusting hardware; finally mounting to the floor above the kitchen where, at the head of the staircase, an open door faced him and beyond it a chest of drawers. The drawers had been pulled out and emptied on the floor; what remained of their contents was a dirty litter, sodden by rain when it drove through the window and browned with the dust of many months, and it was not until Theodore had picked up a handful of the litter that he saw it was composed of women’s trifles of underwear. What he held was a flimsy bodice made of soiled and faded lawn with a narrow little edging of lace.

===Chinese===
他只过了桥端一两码远——太阳警告他，如果他要在夜幕降临前返回营地，他已经达到了能达到的距离极限。在返回穿过村庄的路上，他强忍着对死者咧嘴笑伴的厌恶，走进了一间敞开供任何人进入的寂静房屋。虽然死者仍住在那里——在灾难当天被击倒，未能到达露天——但通常有大量痕迹表明在他之前有活人来过；门被撬开，房间因频繁搜寻衣物和食物而凌乱污秽。尽管如此，在厨房地板的杂乱中，他发现了绳子的宝藏，并往背上的毯袋里塞满了各种生锈的小五金；最后，他爬上厨房上面的楼层，在楼梯口，一扇敞开的门面对着他，里面是一个抽屉柜。抽屉被拉出来，空着放在地板上；里面剩下的东西是一堆肮脏的杂物，被雨水浸湿（雨水从窗户打进来），并因数月灰尘而变褐，直到西奥多捡起一把杂物，他才看到它是由女性内衣小物件组成的。他拿着的是一件轻薄的胸衣，用脏污褪色的平纹细布制成，边缘有狭窄的小蕾丝。

===Original===
He dropped it, only to pick it up again﻿—remembering suddenly the blanket episode and Ada’s lamentable howls for the garments a wilderness denied her. Perhaps an assortment of dingy finery would do something to allay her craving﻿—and, amused at the thought, he went down on a knee and proceeded to collect an armful. Appropriately the shifting of a heap of yellowed rags revealed a broken hand-glass, lying face downwards on the floor; as he raised it, wondering what Ada would say to a mirror as a gift, its cracked surface showed him a bedstead behind him﻿—not empty!﻿ ﻿… What was left of the owner of the scraps of lawn and lace was reflected from the oval of the glass.

===Chinese===
他扔下了它，然后又捡起来——突然想起毯子事件和艾达对荒野拒绝给她的衣物的悲惨嚎叫。也许一套昏暗的华服能缓解她的渴望——想到这里，他感到有趣，单膝跪下，开始收集一大抱。恰当地，一堆发黄破布的移动露出了一面破碎的手镜，面朝下躺在地板上；当他拿起它，想知道艾达会怎么看待作为礼物的镜子时，它破裂的表面映出他身后的一个床架——不是空的！……那堆平纹细布和蕾丝碎片的主人还剩下什么，从椭圆形的镜子里反射出来。

===Original===
He snatched up his bag and clattered down the stairs into the open.

===Chinese===
他抓起袋子，哐啷哐啷地下楼，跑到外面。

## XII / 第十二章

===Original===
It was well past dusk when he trudged up the path that led to the camp and found Ada on the watch at the outskirts of the copse, uneasy at the thought of dark alone.

"You 'ave been a time," she reproached him sulkily. "The 'ole blessed day—since breakfus. I was beginnin' to think you'd gone and got lost and I've 'ad the fair 'ump sittin' 'ere by myself and listenin' to them owls. I 'ate their beastly screechin'; it gives me the creeps."

"Never mind," he consoled her, "come along to the fire. I've brought you something—a present."

"Pertaters?" Ada conjectured, still sulky.

"Not potatoes this time," he told her. "Better than vegetables—something to wear."

"Something to wear," she repeated, with no show of enthusiasm. "I suppose that's another old blanket!"

"Wrong again," he rejoined, amused by the contempt in her voice. She was still contemptuous when he opened his bag and tossed her a dingy bundle; but as she disentangled it, saw lace and embroidery, she brightened suddenly and knelt down to examine in the firelight; while the sight of the cracked hand-glass brought an instant "Oh!" followed by intent contemplation and much patting and twisting of hair.

===Chinese===
天色早已暗下来，他沿着通往营地的小路跋涉而上，发现艾达（Ada）在矮树林边上守望着，独自面对黑暗令她不安。

"你可够久的，"她闷闷不乐地埋怨道，"一整天——打吃过早饭起。我都开始想你是不是迷路了，我一个人坐在这儿听那些猫头鹰叫，气得不行。我讨厌它们那恶心的尖叫；浑身起鸡皮疙瘩。"

"别在意，"他安慰她，"到火堆这边来。我给你带了样东西——一份礼物。"

"土豆？"艾达猜道，仍然不高兴。

"这次不是土豆，"他告诉她，"比蔬菜好——穿的东西。"

"穿的东西，"她毫无热情地重复了一遍，"我猜又是条旧毯子吧！"

"又猜错了，"他答道，被她语气中的轻蔑逗乐了。他打开袋子，扔给她一个灰扑扑的包裹时，她仍是一脸不屑；但当她解开包裹，看到蕾丝和刺绣时，顿时来了精神，跪下来在火光下细看；而那面有裂纹的手镜更让她发出一声"噢！"，紧接着便是专心致志的端详，以及不断地抚弄和扭绞头发。

===Original===
Theodore dished supper while she sat and pondered her reflection; and even while she ate hungrily she had eyes and thoughts for nothing but her new possessions. Some were what he had taken them to be—underclothes, for the most part of an ordinary pattern; but mingled with the plainer linen articles were one or two more decorative, lace collars and the like, and it was on these, dingy as they were, that she fell with delight that was open and audible. He watched her curiously when, for the first time since he had known her, he saw her mouth widen in a smile. She was no longer inert, the sullen, lumpish Ada, she was critical, interested, alive; she fingered her treasures, she smoothed them and made guesses at their price when new; she held them up, now this way, now that, for his admiration and her own. Finally, while Theodore stretched his tired length by the camp fire, she ran off to her shelter for a broken scrap of comb; and when he looked up, a few minutes later, she was posing self-consciously before the hand-glass, with hair newly twisted and a dirty scrap of lace round her neck.… She was another woman as she sat with her rags arranged to show her new frippery; tilting the hand-mirror this way and that and twitching now at the collar and now at her straying ends of hair.

===Chinese===
西奥多盛着晚饭，她则坐着端详镜中的自己；即便在狼吞虎咽地吃饭时，她的眼睛和心思也全在那些新宝贝上。有些确实如他所料——内衣，大多是普通样式；但在那些朴素的亚麻衣物之间，有一两件更讲究的，蕾丝衣领之类的东西，尽管灰扑扑的，她却毫不掩饰地流露出欢欣。他好奇地看着她——自从认识她以来，第一次看到她咧嘴笑了。她不再是那个迟钝、木然的艾达了，她变得挑剔、好奇、鲜活；她抚弄着她的宝贝，将它们抚平，猜测它们全新时的价格；她举起来，左看右看，给他看也给自己看。最后，当西奥多在营火旁伸展疲惫的身体时，她跑回自己的棚屋去找一把断齿的梳子；几分钟后他抬起头来，发现她正在手镜前忸怩作态，头发重新编过，脖子上围了一条脏兮兮的蕾丝碎片。……她变了个人似的坐着，把破衣烂衫整理了一番以展示她的新饰物；把手镜左歪右斜地照着，一会儿扯扯衣领，一会儿拨弄散落的发梢。

===Original===
Lying stretched on an arm by the fire, he watched her little feminine antics, amused and taken out of himself; realizing how seldom, till that moment, he had thought of her as a woman, how nearly she had seemed to him an animal only, a creature to be guided and fed; and parrying her eager and insistent demand to be taken to the house where the treasure had been found, that she might see if it contained any more. He had no desire to spoil her pleasure in her finery by the gruesome tale of the manner of its finding; hence, in spite of a curiosity made manifest in coaxing, he held to his refusal stubbornly.… The house was a long way off, he told her—much further than she would care to tramp; then, as she still persisted, maintaining her readiness even for a lengthy expedition, he went on to fiction and explained that the house was in a dangerous condition—knocked about, ruinous, might fall at any moment—and he was not going to say where it was, for her own sake, lest she should be tempted to the peril of an entry.

She pouted "You might tell me," glancing at him from under her lashes; then, as he still persisted in refusal, slapped him on the shoulder for an obstinate boy, turned her back and pretended to sulk. He returned the slap—she expected it and giggled; the next move in the game was his catching of her wrist as she raised her hand for a rejoinder—and for a moment they wrestled inanely, after the fashion of Hampstead Heath.… As he let her go, it dawned on him that this was flirtation as she knew it.

===Chinese===
他一条胳膊撑着躺在火边，看着她那些女性的小把戏，觉得有趣，也由此暂时忘却了自己；他意识到在此之前自己几乎从未把她当作一个女人来想，在他眼里她几乎只是一头牲畜、一个需要引导和喂养的生灵；而她一个劲儿地缠着他，要他带她去找到那些宝贝的那所房子，看看里面还有没有更多。他不想用那些东西骇人的来历来扫她兴；所以，尽管她撒娇央求得明明白白，他还是固执地拒绝了。……那房子很远，他告诉她——远得她不想走那么远的路；然而她依然坚持，甚至表示长途跋涉也在所不辞，他便编起了谎，解释说那房子状况危险——摇摇欲坠、破败不堪、随时可能倒塌——为了她好，他不会说它在哪里，免得她经不住诱惑去冒闯入的险。

她撅着嘴说"你就告诉我嘛"，从睫毛底下瞟了他一眼；见他仍然拒绝，便拍了他肩膀一下，骂他是个倔小子，转过身去假装生气。他也回拍了她一下——她料到了，咯咯地笑了；游戏的下一步是他抓住了她举起来要还击的手腕——两人有那么一会儿像在汉普斯特德荒野（Hampstead Heath）上那样无聊地扭打起来。……他松开她时，恍然大悟——这就是她所知道的调情。

---

===Original===
It did not take long for him to realize that they stood to each other, from that night on, in a new and more difficult relation; from foundling and guardian, the leader and led, they had developed into woman and man. For a time fear and hunger had suppressed in Ada the consciousness of sex—which a yard or two of lace and the possession of a hand-glass had revived. Once revived, it coloured her every action, gave meaning to her every word and glance; so that, day by day and hour by hour, the man who dwelt beside her was reminded of bodily desire.

One night when she had left him he lay staring at the fire, faced the situation and wondered if she saw where she was drifting? Possibly—possibly not; she was acting instinctively, from habit. To her (he was sure) a man was a creature to flirt with; an unsubtle attempt to arouse his desire was the only way she knew of carrying on a conversation.… Now that she was woman again—not merely bewildered misery and empty stomach—she had slipped back inevitably to the little giggling allurements of her factory days, to the habits bred in her bone.… With the result?… He put the thought from him, turned over, dog-weary, and slept.

So soon as the next night he saw the result as inevitable; the outcome of life reduced to mere animal living, of nearness, isolation and the daily consciousness of sex. If they stayed together—and how should they not stay together?—it was only a question of time, of weeks at the furthest, of days or it might be hours.… He raised himself to peer through the night at the log-hut that hid and sheltered Ada, wondering if she also were awake. If so, of a certainty, her thoughts were of him; and perhaps she knew likewise that it was only a question of time. Perhaps—and perhaps she just drifted, following her instincts.… He found himself wondering what she would say if she opened her eyes to find him standing at the entrance to her hut, to see him bending over her … now?

He put the thought from him and once more turned over and slept.

===Chinese===
没过多久他就意识到，从那一夜起，他们之间的关系已步入了一个新的、更艰难的阶段；从弃儿与守护者、引领者与追随者，他们已演变为女人与男人。有一段时间，恐惧和饥饿在艾达心中压抑着性意识——而一两码蕾丝和一面手镜又将它唤醒了。一旦苏醒，它便浸染了她的每一个举动，赋予她的每一句话和每一个眼神以含义；日复一日、时复一时，住在她身旁的这个男人都被提醒着肉体的欲望。

一天夜里她离开后，他躺着凝视火光，直面这个局面，不知她是否看清了自己正在滑向何方？也许看清了——也许没有；她只是凭本能行事，出于习惯。在他看来（他确信），男人就是用来调情的对象；一种笨拙的挑逗欲望的企图是她唯一知道的交谈方式。……如今她重新成了一个女人——不再只是茫然的痛苦和空瘪的胃——她不可避免地滑回了工厂时代那些咯咯笑着的诱惑手段，滑回了那些已深入骨髓的习惯。……结果会怎样？……他把这个念头推开，翻了个身，疲惫至极地睡去了。

到了第二天晚上，他就已经看到了那不可避免的结果；那是生活沦为纯粹的动物生存、朝夕相对、与世隔绝、日日被性意识所占据的必然结局。如果他们继续在一起——他们怎么可能不继续在一起？——那只是时间问题，至多数周，或许数日甚至数小时。……他撑起身，在夜色中望向那间遮蔽和庇护着艾达的原木棚屋，不知她是否也醒着。如果醒着，她想的肯定是他；也许她同样知道这只是时间问题。也许——也许她只是顺其自然，跟随本能。……他发现自己在想：如果她睁开眼睛，发现他站在她棚屋的入口处，发现他正俯身向她……现在？

他把这个念头推开，再次翻身睡去。

===Original===
With the morning it seemed further off, less inevitable; the sun was hidden behind raw grey mist, and when Ada, shivering and stupid, turned out into the chilly discomfort of the weather she was too much depressed for the exercise of feminine coquetry. The day's work—hard necessary wood-chopping and equally necessary fishing for the larder—sent his thoughts into other channels, and it was not till he sat at their evening fire—warmed, fed and rested, with no duties to distract him—that he became conscious again, and even more strongly, of the change in their attitude and intercourse. Something new, of expectation, had crept into it; something of excitement and constraint. When their hands touched by chance they noticed it, were instantly awkward; when a silence fell Ada was embarrassed, uncomfortable and made palpable efforts to break it with her pointless giggle. When their eyes met, hers dropped and looked away.… When she rose at last and said good night he was sure that she also knew. And since they both knew and the end was inevitable, certain.…

"You're not going yet," he said—and caught at her wrist, laughing oddly.

"It's late—and I'm sleepy," she objected with a foolish little giggle; but made no effort to withdraw her wrist from his hold.

"Nonsense," he told her, "it's early yet—and you're better by the fire. Sit down and keep me company for a bit longer."
