"That same wicked man﻿—who was also very wise﻿—told me something else that is as true for you as it was for me; he said that we never know anything except through our own experience. I might tell you that the sun is warm or the water is cold, but if you had never felt the heat of the sun or the cold of the water you would not know what I meant. And it was like that with us; there were always some few who understood that knowledge was a flame that, in the end, would burn us﻿—but the rest of us couldn’t even try to save ourselves until after we were burned."
He stroked the grubby face as he released it.
"That’s the Law, son; and all that matters you’ll learn that way. That way and no other﻿—just as we did."

===Chinese===
他在儿子眼中看到了几乎是轻蔑的神情，把那张脏兮兮的脸捧在手里。
"那个坏人——也是个非常聪明的人——告诉了我一些对你我同样真实的话；他说我们除了通过自己的经历，永远无法真正了解任何事情。我可以告诉你太阳是温暖的，水是冰冷的，但如果你从未感受过太阳的温暖或水的冰冷，你就不会明白我的意思。我们的情况就是这样；总是有少数人明白，知识是一团火焰，最终会灼伤我们——但我们其余人，直到被灼伤之后，才能尝试拯救自己。"
他抚摸着那张脏兮兮的脸，然后松开了手。
"这就是法则，儿子；所有重要的事情，你都会通过这种方式学会。只有这种方式，别无其他——就像我们曾经那样。"

===Original===
---
In time he found himself recalling, with strange interest, the fairytales of his childhood; he spent long hours re-weaving and piecing them together, searching his memory for half-remembered fragments of what had once seemed fantasy or nonsense invented for the nursery. The hobgoblins and heroes of his nursery days were transformed and made suddenly possible; looking through the mind of a new generation, he saw that they might have been as human and prosaic as himself. More﻿—he came to know that he and his commonplace, civilized contemporaries would be the heroes and hobgoblins of the future.

===Chinese===
---
渐渐地，他发现自己以一种奇特的兴趣回想起童年的童话故事；他花了很长时间重新编织和拼凑它们，在记忆中搜寻那些曾经看起来像是为育儿室编造的幻想或废话的、半遗忘的碎片。他童年时代的妖精和英雄被转变了，突然变得可能；透过新一代的心智，他看到他们可能和他一样平凡而普通。更进一步——他开始明白，他和他的那些平凡、文明的同代人，将成为未来的英雄和妖精。

===Original===
The process, the odd transformation, would be simple as it was inevitable. It was forbidden, by the spirit and letter of the Vow, to awaken youthful curiosity concerning the past﻿—youthful curiosity whose end might be youthful experiment; but women, in spite of all vows and prohibitions, would gossip to each other of their memories. While they talked their children would listen, open-eyed and puzzled; and when a youngster demanded the meaning of an unfamiliar term or impossible happening, the explanation, as a matter of course, took the form of analogy, of comparison with the known and familiar. The aeroplane was a bird extinct and monstrous﻿—larger, many times larger, than the flapping heron or the owl; the bomb was more dreadful than a lightning stroke; the tram, train or motor a gigantic wheelbarrow that ran without man or beast to drag it.﻿ ﻿… The ignorance of science of those who told, the yet greater ignorance of those who heard, resulted, inevitably, before many years had passed, in myth and religious legend﻿—an outwardly fantastic statement of actual fact and truth. The children, piecing together their fragments of incomprehensible information, made their own image of the past﻿—to be handed on later to their sons; an image of a world fantastic, enchanted and amazing, destroyed, as a judgment for sin against God, by strange, fire-breathing beasts and bolts from heaven. A world of gigantic fauna and bewitched chariots; likewise of sorcerers, their masters﻿—whom God and the righteous had exterminated.﻿ ﻿… So Theodore realized﻿—as his children grew and he heard them talk﻿—must a race that knew nothing of science explain the dead wonders of science; from the message that flashes round the world in seconds to the petrol-engine and the magic slumber of chloroform. That which is outside the power and beyond the understanding of man has always been denounced as magic; and steam, electricity, chemical action, were outside the power and beyond the understanding of men born after the Ruin. In default of understanding they must needs fall back on a wizardry known to their fathers; thus he and his contemporaries to their children’s children would be semi-supernatural beings, fit comrades of Sindbad, of Perseus, or the Quatre Fils Aymon: giants with great voices that called to each other across continents and vasty deeps; possessors of seven-league boots, magic steeds and flying carpets﻿—of all the stock-in-trade of the fairytale.﻿ ﻿… Belief in the demigod was a natural growth and product of the world wherein his son grew to manhood.

===Chinese===
这个过程，这种奇特的转变，既简单又不可避免。誓言的精神和字面意义都禁止唤起对过去的好奇心——那种可能导致年轻人尝试的好奇心；但是，尽管有各种誓言和禁令，女人们还是会互相闲聊她们的记忆。当她们交谈时，孩子们会睁大眼睛听着，充满困惑；当一个孩子要求解释一个陌生的术语或不可能发生的事件时，解释自然会采取类比的形式，与已知和熟悉的事物进行比较。飞机是一种已经灭绝的、怪异的鸟——比拍打翅膀的苍鹭或猫头鹰大很多倍；炸弹比闪电更可怕；电车、火车或汽车是一种巨大的独轮车，无需人力或畜力就能行驶。……讲述者的科学无知，加上倾听者更大的无知，不可避免地，在多年后，导致了神话和宗教传说——对实际事实和真相的表面上奇幻的陈述。孩子们将他们那些无法理解的信息碎片拼凑在一起，形成了自己对过去的想象——后来传给他们的儿子；一个奇幻、迷人、惊人的世界的想象，这个世界因为违背上帝的罪孽而被毁灭，被奇怪的、喷火的野兽和从天而降的雷霆摧毁。一个有着巨大动物群和被施了魔法的战车的世界；同样也有巫师，他们的主人——被上帝和正义之人消灭了。……西奥多意识到——随着他的孩子们长大，听他们谈论——一个对科学一无所知的种族必然会这样解释科学的已死奇迹；从能在几秒钟内传遍世界的信息，到汽油发动机和氯仿的神奇沉睡。超出人类力量和理解力的东西，总是被谴责为魔法；而蒸汽、电力、化学反应，在毁灭后出生的人们看来，都超出了他们的力量和理解。由于无法理解，他们必须回归到他们父亲所知的巫术中；因此，他和他的同代人对他们的子子孙孙来说，将是半超自然的存在，是辛巴达、珀尔修斯或艾蒙四子的合适同伴：有着能在大陆和浩瀚深海中相互呼唤的巨大嗓音的巨人；拥有七里靴、魔法坐骑和飞毯——所有童话故事的标配。……对半神的信仰，在他儿子成长的世界里，是一种自然生长和产物。

===Original===
Given time and black ignorance of mechanics and science, and the engineer would be promoted to a giant or demigod; who, by virtue of a strength that was more than human, dammed rivers, drained bogs and pierced mountains. "As it was in the beginning, is now and ever shall be"﻿—and always in the past there had been giants. Titans﻿—and Hercules, removing mighty obstacles and cleansing the stables of Augeas. He came to understand that all wonders were facts misinterpreted and that (given time and ignorance) a post-office underling, tapping out his Morse code, might be seen as a genie or an Oberon﻿—the absolute master of obedient sprites who could lay their girdles round the earth; and he pictured a college-bred, sober-suited Hercules planning his Labours in the office of a limited company﻿—jotting down figures, estimating costs and scanning the reports of geologists. Figures and reports, like his tunnels and dams, would pass into the limbo of science forgotten and forbidden, but the memory of his labours, his defiance of brute nature, would live on as the story of a demigod; and the childhood that was barbarism would explain his achievements by a giant strength that could tear down trees and move mountains.

===Chinese===
只要有时间，加上对机械和科学的完全无知，工程师就会被提升为巨人或半神；凭借超越人类的力量，他们堵塞河流、排干沼泽、穿透山脉。"太初如何，今亦如何，永世不渝"——而在过去，总有巨人存在。泰坦——还有赫拉克勒斯，移除巨大的障碍，清洗奥吉亚斯的牛圈。他逐渐明白，所有奇迹都是被误解的事实，而（只要有时间和无知）一个邮局的小职员，敲击着他的摩尔斯电码，可能会被视为精灵或奥伯龙——服从的精灵们的绝对主宰，他们能将腰带绕地球一周；他想象一个受过大学教育、穿着朴素西装的赫拉克勒斯在一家有限公司的办公室里策划他的功业——记下数字，估算成本，阅读地质学家的报告。数字和报告，就像他的隧道和水坝一样，将被遗忘和禁止，进入科学的虚无领域，但他功业的记忆，他对自然暴力的反抗，将以半神的故事流传下去；而那个处于野蛮时代的童年，将用能拔起树木、移动山岳的巨大力量来解释他的成就。

===Original===
The idea took fast root and grew in him﻿—the idea of a world that, time and again, had returned to the helplessness of childhood. He saw science as the burden that, time and again, the race found intolerable; as Dead-Sea fruit that turned to ashes in the mouth, as riches that humanity strove for, attained and renounced﻿—renounced because it dared not keep them. In his hours of dreaming he made fairies and demigods out of dapper little sedentary persons, the senders of forgotten telegrams, with forgotten engines﻿—motorcars and aeroplanes﻿—at their insignificant command; and once, in the night, when Ada snored beside him, he asked himself if Lucifer, Son of the Morning﻿—Lucifer who strove with his God and was worsted﻿—were more, in his beginnings, than a scientist intent on his work? A chemist, a spectacled professor, resplendent only in degrees and learning? An Archfiend of Knowledge who had sinned against God in the secret places of a laboratory and not upon the shining plains of Heaven? And whom ignorance and time had glorified into the Tempter, the Evil One﻿—setting him magnificently in the flaming Hell which he and his like, by their skill and patience, had created and let loose upon man?﻿ ﻿… This, at least, was certain; that in years to come and under other names, his children’s children would retell the story of Lucifer, Son of the Morning; the Enemy of Man who was flung out of Heaven because, in his overweening vanity, he encroached on the power of a God.

===Chinese===
这个想法在他心中迅速扎根并成长——一个一次又一次回归到童年无助状态的世界的想法。他将科学视为种族一次又一次发现难以承受的负担；视为口中化为灰烬的死海果实；视为人类努力争取、获得又放弃的财富——因为不敢保留而放弃。在梦想的时刻，他将那些穿着时髦、坐在办公室里的人，那些发送着被遗忘的电报、操控着被遗忘的引擎——汽车和飞机——的微不足道的人，变成了仙女和半神；有一次，在夜里，当艾达在旁边打鼾时，他问自己，晨星之子路西法——那个与上帝争斗并败下阵来的路西法——在最初，难道不就是一个专注于自己工作的科学家吗？一个化学家，一个戴着眼镜、只凭学位和学识显得光彩夺目的教授？一个在实验室的秘密场所，而非在闪耀的天堂平原上，得罪了上帝的知识大魔王？而无知和时间将他美化成了诱惑者、邪恶者——将他壮丽地置于他和他的同类凭借技艺和耐心创造出来、并释放到人间的地狱烈火中？……至少有一点是确定的：在未来的岁月里，以其他的名字，他的子子孙孙会重新讲述晨星之子路西法的故事；那个因狂妄自大、侵犯上帝权力而被逐出天堂的人类之敌。

===Original===
It was the new world that taught him that man invents nothing, is incapable of pure invention; that what seem his wildest, most fantastic imaginings are no more than ineffective, distorted attempts to set down a half-forgotten experience. What had once appeared prophecies he saw to be memories; the Day of Judgment, when the heavens should flame and men call upon the rocks to cover them, belonged to the past before it belonged to the future. The forecast of its terrors was possible only to a people that had known them as realities; a people troubled by a dim race-memory of the conquest of the air and catastrophe hurled from the skies.﻿ ﻿…
So, at least, his children taught him to believe.

===Chinese===
正是这个新世界教会了他，人类不会发明任何东西，不具备纯粹的创造力；那些看起来最狂野、最奇特的想象，不过是无效而扭曲的尝试，试图记录下一段半遗忘的经历。那些曾经看似预言的东西，他现在看是记忆；世界末日，当天堂燃烧，人们呼唤岩石来遮盖自己时，在它属于未来之前，它首先属于过去。只有那些曾将这些恐怖视为现实的民族，才可能预见到它的恐怖；一个因对征服天空和从天而降的灾难的模糊种族记忆而困扰的民族。……
至少，他的孩子们是这样教他相信的。

## XXII / XXII

===Original===
With years and rough husbandry the resources of the tribe were augmented and it emerged from its first starved misery; more land was brought under cultivation and, as tillage improved and better crops were raised, the little community was less dependent on the haphazard luck of its fishing and snaring and lived further from the line of utter want. While, save in bad seasons, the inter-tribal raiding that was caused by sheer starvation was less frequent. Even so, strife was frequent enough﻿—small intermittent feud that flared now and again into savagery; the desire of a growing community to extend its hunting-grounds at the expense of a neighbour meant, almost inevitably, appeal to the right of the strongest. Other quarrels had their origin in the border inroads and reprisals of poachers or a barbaric setting of the eternal story that was old when Helen launched a thousand ships.

===Chinese===
年复一年，粗放的农耕让部落的资源逐渐丰裕，它从最初的饥馑苦难中挣脱出来；更多的土地被开垦，随着耕作技术的改善和收成的提高，这个小小的社群不再那么依赖捕鱼和设陷捕猎的偶然运气，离赤贫的边缘也远了些。除了歉收的年景，因纯粹饥饿而引发的部落间劫掠也不再那么频繁。即便如此，冲突依然不断——时断时续的小规模仇杀，不时升级为野蛮的暴力；一个不断壮大的社群以牺牲邻族为代价扩展其猎场的欲望，几乎不可避免地诉诸强者为王的法则。其他争端则源于盗猎者的跨境侵扰与报复，或是那古老故事的野蛮重演——自海伦（Helen）引发千帆之战以来，这故事便已亘古不变。

===Original===
With husbandry, even rough husbandry, came the small beginnings of commerce, the barter and exchange of one man’s superfluities for the produce of another man’s fields. Cold and nakedness stimulated ingenuity in the matter of clothing, even in a society whose original members had in large part been bred to depend in all things on the aid of the machine and to earn a livelihood by the performance of one action only﻿—the tending of one lathe, the accomplishment of one stereotyped mechanical process. Outcasts of civilization flung into the world of savagery, they had in the beginning none of the adaptability and none of the resources of the savage﻿—knew nothing of the properties of unfamiliar plants, knew neither what to weave nor how to weave it, and often from sheer lack of understanding, starved and shivered in the midst of plenty. It was not till they had suffered long and intolerably that they learned to clothe themselves from such material as their new world afforded, to cure skins of animals and stitch them together into garments. In the first years of ruins only ratskins were plentiful; but, as time went on, rabbits, cats and wild dogs multiplied and, spreading through the countryside, were trapped and hunted for their flesh and the warmth of their skins. The dogs, as they bred, reverted to a mongrel and wolf-like type which, in summer, preyed largely on vermin; in winter, when scarcity of food made them bold, they prowled in packs, were a danger to the solitary and a legendary terror to children.

===Chinese===
农耕——即便是粗放的农耕——带来了商业的萌芽，人们开始以物易物，用自家的余粮换取他人田地的出产。寒冷与赤裸激发了人们在衣着方面的创造力，尽管这个社会的大多数成员原本被培养成凡事依赖机器、仅靠单一动作谋生的人——操作一台车床，完成一道刻板的机械工序。这些被文明抛弃、抛入野蛮世界的人，起初既没有野蛮人的适应能力，也没有他们的生存智慧——对陌生植物的特性一无所知，既不知道该织什么，也不知道如何织造，常常因为纯粹的无知，在丰饶之中忍饥挨饿、瑟瑟发抖。直到他们长期且难以忍受地受苦之后，才学会用新世界提供的材料制作衣物，学会鞣制兽皮并将其缝合成衣服。毁灭之初的岁月里，只有鼠皮是充裕的；但随着时间的推移，兔子、猫和野狗繁衍增多，它们遍布乡间，被捕猎以获取肉食和皮毛的温暖。狗在繁殖中退化成一种杂交的、近似狼的类型，夏天主要以害虫为食；冬天，当食物匮乏使它们变得胆大时，便成群结队地游荡，对独行者构成威胁，对孩子们则是传说中的恐怖。

===Original===
In the beginning the village was a straggle of rude huts, the tribesmen building how and where they would; later it took shape within its first wall and was roughly circular, enclosed by a fence of stake and thornbush. The raising of the fence was a sign and result of the beginning of primitive competition in armament; it was the knowledge that one village had fortified itself that set others to the driving in of stakes. One November evening Theodore, trudging in with his catch, saw a group round the headman’s fire; the centre of interest, a youth who had returned from poaching on other men’s land and brought back news of their doings. His trespassing had taken him within sight of the neighbouring village﻿—which lately was a cluster of huts, like their own, and now was surrounded by a wall. A stockade, fully the height of a man, with only one gap for a gate.﻿ ﻿… The poacher’s news was discussed with uneasy interest. The fortified tribe, in point of numbers, was already stronger than its rival; if it added this new advantage to its numbers, what was there to prevent it from raiding and robbing as it would? Having raided and robbed, it could shelter behind its defences﻿—beat off attack, make sorties and master the countryside! Its security meant the insecurity of others, the dependence of others on its goodwill and neighbourly honesty; the issue was as plain to the handful of tribesmen as to old-time nations competing in battleships, aeroplanes and guns, and the suspicions muttered round the headman’s fire were the raw material of arguments once familiar in the councils of emperors.

===Chinese===
起初，村庄只是一片杂乱搭建的简陋棚屋，部落成员随心所欲地建造；后来，它在第一道围墙内初具规模，大致呈圆形，由木桩和荆棘丛构成的栅栏环绕。栅栏的竖立是原始军备竞赛开始的标志与结果；正是得知一个村庄已设防，才促使其他村庄也开始打桩筑墙。一个十一月的傍晚，西奥多（Theodore）拖着捕获物回来时，看到一群人围在酋长的火堆旁；兴趣的中心是一个从他人土地上盗猎归来的年轻人，他带回了关于邻人动向的消息。他的越界使他得以窥见邻村的景象——那里不久前还像他们自己一样是一簇棚屋，现在却被一道墙围住了。一道齐人高的栅栏，只留了一个缺口作为门。……盗猎者带来的消息引起了不安的关注。那个设防的部落在人数上本已强于对手；如果它再添上这新的优势，还有什么能阻止它随心所欲地劫掠抢劫？劫掠之后，它还可以躲在防御工事后面——击退进攻、出击并掌控乡间！它的安全意味着他人的不安全，意味着他人要仰赖它的善意和睦邻的诚信；这个问题对这少数部落成员来说，就如同对旧时代竞相建造战舰、飞机和火炮的国家一样明晰，而围绕在酋长火堆旁低声说出的猜疑，正是当年在帝王议会中熟悉的论据的原始材料。

===Original===
In the end, as the result of uneasy discussion, Theodore and another were dispatched to spy out the new menace, to get as near as they might to the wall, ascertain its strength and the method of its building; and with their return from a night expedition there was more consultation and a hurried planning of defences. Before winter was over the haphazard settlement was a compound, a walled town in embryo; within the narrow limits of a circle small enough for a handful of men to defend all huts were crowded, all provisions stored, all animals driven at sunset﻿—so that, in case of night attack, no man could be cut off and the strength of the tribe be at hand to resist the assailants. With waste, healthy miles stretching out on either side, the village itself was an evil-smelling huddle of cabins; since a short stretch of wall was easier to defend than a long, men and beasts were crowded together in a foulness that made for security. In times of feud﻿—and times of feud were seldom distant﻿—stones were heaped beside the barrier, in readiness to serve as missiles, watch and ward was kept turn and turn by the able-bodied and﻿—naturally, inevitably and almost unconsciously﻿—there was evolved a system of military discipline, of penalty for mutiny and cowardice.

===Chinese===
最终，在不安的商议之后，西奥多和另一人被派去侦察新的威胁，尽可能靠近那道墙，探明其强度和建造方法；当他们夜间行动归来后，又进行了更多商议，并匆忙规划防御工事。冬天尚未结束，这个随意的聚落已变成一个围场，一个雏形中的城墙小镇；在一个仅够少数人防守的狭小圆形区域内，所有的棚屋都挤在一起，所有的储粮都集中存放，所有的牲畜在日落时被赶回——这样，万一遭遇夜袭，没有人会被隔绝在外，部落的力量也能就近抵抗入侵者。在大片荒芜、但两侧延伸着健康林地的环境中，村庄本身是一片恶臭弥漫的棚屋群；既然短墙比长墙更容易防守，人和牲畜便挤在一起，这种污秽却带来了安全。在仇杀时期——仇杀时期很少遥远——石头被堆在栅栏旁，以备投掷之用，警戒由健壮者轮流担任，而且——自然而然、不可避免且几乎是无意识地——一套军事纪律体系演化出来，对哗变和怯懦的惩罚也随之确立。

===Original===
As in every social system from the beginning of time, the community was welded to a conscious whole not by the love its members bore to each other, but by hatred and fear of the outsider; it was the enemy, the urgent common need to be saved from him, that made of man a comrade and a citizen; the peril from outside was the natural antidote to everyday hatreds and the ceaseless bickerings of close neighbours. The instinctive politics of a squalid village were in miniature the policy of vanished nations, and untraditioned little headmen, like dead and gone kings, quelled internal feuds by diverting attention to the danger that threatened from abroad. The foundations of community life in the new world, like the foundations of community life in the old, were laid in the selfishness of fear; but for all its base origin the life of the community imposed upon its members the essential virtues of the soldier and citizen, a measure of discipline and sacrifice. From these, in time, would grow loyalty and pride in sacrifice; the enclosure of ramshackle huts and pens was breaking its savages to achievements undreamed of and virtues as yet beyond their ken; the blind, stubborn instincts that created Babylon﻿—created London and Rome and destroyed them﻿—were laying well and truly in a mud-walled compound the foundations of cities which should rise, flourish, perish in the stead of London and of Rome.

===Chinese===
如同自古以来的每一个社会体系，这个社群并非因其成员彼此相爱而凝聚成一个有意识的整体，而是因对外人的仇恨与恐惧；正是敌人，正是亟待共同拯救的迫切需求，使人成为同伴和公民；外部的危险是日常仇恨与邻里间无休止争吵的天然解药。这个肮脏村庄的本能政治，正是那些消逝民族政策的缩影，而那些没有传统的小酋长们，如同已故的君王们一样，通过将注意力引向来自国外的威胁来平息内部纷争。新世界社群生活的基石，如同旧世界社群生活的基石一样，建立在恐惧的自私之上；但尽管起源卑劣，社群的生活却为其成员赋予了士兵与公民的基本美德，一种纪律与牺牲的精神。假以时日，忠诚与对牺牲的自豪感将从中萌生；这些摇摇欲坠的棚屋与围栏的 enclosure，正将其野蛮人塑造成就未敢想象的功业与尚不可及的美德；那些盲目而固执的本能曾创造巴比伦——创造伦敦与罗马并摧毁它们——如今正实实在在地在泥墙围场内奠定未来城市的基石，这些城市将崛起、兴盛、衰亡，取代伦敦与罗马的位置。

===Original===
Outside the little fortress with its noisome huddle of sheds and shelters lay a belt of ploughed land, of patches scraped and sown, where the women worked by the side of their men and worked alone when their men were gone hunting or fishing. One or two members of the tribe who were countrymen born were its saviours in its first years of leanness, imparting their knowledge of soil and seed to their unskilled comrades bred in towns; and, by slow degrees, as the lesson was learned, the belt of tilled ground grew wider and more fertile, the little community more prosperous.

===Chinese===
在这座散发着恶臭、棚屋与庇护所杂乱聚集的小堡垒之外，是一片耕作过的土地，一块块开垦并播种过的田地，女人们在男人身边劳作，当男人外出打猎或捕鱼时，她们便独自干活。部落里一两个土生土长的乡下人是头几年饥馑时期的救星，他们将关于土壤和种子的知识传授给那些在城市里长大、毫无技能的同伴；随着经验的缓慢积累，耕作带变得更宽、更肥沃，这个小小的社群也日益繁荣。

===Original===
As families grew and the tribe settled down the makeshift shelters of wood and moss were succeeded by stronger and better built cabins; by the time that her second child was born Ada was established in a weatherproof hut﻿—a mud-walled building, roofed with dried grass and with a floor of earth beaten hard. In its early years it possessed a glazed window, a pane which Theodore had found whole in a crumbling house and set immovably in an aperture cut in his wall. But, as years went on, unbroken glass was hard to come by; and there came a day when the window-aperture, no longer glazed, was plastered up to keep out the weather.

===Chinese===
随着家庭的繁衍和部落的定居，那些用木头和苔藓搭建的临时棚屋被更坚固、更精良的小屋所取代；当艾达（Ada）的第二个孩子出生时，她已住进了一座防风防雨的棚屋——一座泥墙建筑，屋顶覆盖着干草，地面是夯实的泥土。早年间，它还拥有一扇玻璃窗，那是西奥多在一栋摇摇欲坠的房子里找到的一块完好的窗玻璃，他把它牢牢地嵌入在墙上凿出的洞口里。但年复一年，完整的玻璃越来越难找到；终于有一天，那个不再镶嵌玻璃的窗洞被泥灰封死，以抵御风雨。

===Original===
Long before he set about the building of his cabin Theodore had brought a strip of ground under cultivation, sown a patch of potatoes and straggling beans which, in time, expanded to a field. His life, henceforth, was largely the anxious life of the seasons; the sowing and tending and reaping of his crop, the struggle with the soil and the barrenness thereof, the ceaseless war against vermin.﻿ ﻿… He ended rich, as the men of his time counted riches; the possessor of goats, the owner of land which other men envied him, the father of sons who could till it. The new world gave him what it had to give; and gradually, with the passing of years, the hope of life civilized died in him and he ceased to strain his eyes at the distance.

===Chinese===
远在动手建造自己的棚屋之前，西奥多就已开垦了一小片土地，种下了一块土豆和零星的豆子，这些作物日后扩展成了一片田地。从此以后，他的生活便大体上是那种随季节起伏的焦虑生活；播种、照料、收割庄稼，与土壤及其贫瘠抗争，与害虫进行无休止的战争。……他最终变得富有了，以那个时代人们的财富标准衡量；他拥有山羊，拥有一片令他人羡慕的土地，还有能耕种它的儿子们。新世界给予了它所能给予的一切；随着岁月的流逝，他对文明生活的希望在他心中逐渐熄灭，他不再向远方凝望。

---
===Original===
She, before she was thirty, was a haggard slattern of a woman; pitiable by reason of her discontent, and looking far older than her years. Childbearing aged her and the fieldwork she hated﻿—the bent-backed drudgery she tried in vain to shirk and to which she brought no shred of understanding; even more she was aged by the weary desire that sulked in the corners of her mouth. Before she lost her comeliness she had more than once sought distraction from her dullness in clumsy flirtation; which perhaps was no more than silly ogling and nudging and perhaps led to actual unfaithfulness. Theodore﻿—not greatly interested in his wife’s doings﻿—ignored the danger to his household peace until it was forcibly thrust upon his notice by a jealous spitfire who cursed Ada for running after other women’s husbands, and proceeded to tear out her hair. Ada’s snuffling protestations when the spitfire was pulled off did not savour of injured innocence; he judged her guilty, at least in thought, cuffed her soundly and from that time kept his eye on her. He was not (as she liked to think) jealous﻿—salving her bruises with the comforting balm that two males were disputing the possession of her body; what stirred him to wrath fundamentally was his outraged sense of property in Ada, his woman, and the possibility that her lightness might entail on him the labour of supporting another man’s child. The intrigue﻿—if intrigue it were﻿—ended on the day of the cuffing and hairpulling; her Lothario, awed by his spitfire or unwilling to tackle an outraged husband, avoided her company from that day forth and Ada sank back to domesticity.

===Chinese===
她还不到三十岁，就成了一个憔悴邋遢的女人；因她的不满而显得可怜，看上去比实际年龄老得多。生育使她衰老，她所厌恶的田间劳作——那种弯腰驼背的苦差事，她徒劳地想逃避，却对之毫无理解；更使她衰老的是郁结在嘴角的倦怠欲望。在她失去美貌之前，她曾不止一次用笨拙的调情来排遣她的沉闷；那也许不过是愚蠢的挤眉弄眼和推推搡搡，也许导致了实际的不忠。西奥多——对妻子的行为并不十分在意——忽视了对他家庭安宁的威胁，直到一个善妒的泼妇当面怒斥艾达勾引其他女人的丈夫，并动手扯她的头发，这威胁才被强行推到他面前。当那泼妇被拉开时，艾达带着鼻音的辩解听起来并不像无辜受害；他判定她有罪，至少心里是这么想的，狠狠地扇了她耳光，并从此盯紧了她。他并非（如她喜欢想象的那样）在嫉妒——用两个男人争夺她的身体所有权这种安慰性的说辞来抚慰她的瘀伤；真正激起他愤怒的，是他作为艾达——他的女人——的所有权受到了侵犯，以及她的轻浮可能迫使他承担抚养另一个男人的孩子的劳力。那段暧昧——如果算是暧昧的话——在扇耳光和扯头发那天就结束了；她的情夫，要么被那个泼妇吓住了，要么不愿对付一个被激怒的丈夫，从那天起便避开了她，艾达也就此回归了家庭生活。

===Original===
She, too, in the end accepted the loss of the world that had made her what she was, ceased to search the horizon and strain her eyes for the deliverer; whereupon﻿—having nothing to aim at or hope for﻿—she lapsed into slovenly neglect of her home, alternating hours of clack and gossip with fits of sullen complaining at the daily misery of existence.

===Chinese===
她最终也接受了那个造就了她的世界的丧失，不再搜寻地平线、不再为救世主而极目远眺；于是——既然再无目标或希望可言——她陷入了对家庭的邋遢忽视，整日唠叨闲聊，间或阴郁地抱怨着生存的日常苦难。

===Original===
Had destiny realized the dreams of her youth and set her to live out her married life in a shoddy little villa with bamboo furniture, she might have made a tolerable mother; she would at least have taken pride in the looks of her children, have dressed them with interest, as she dressed herself, and tied up their hair with satin bows. Being what she was, she could take no pride in ragamuffins who ran half the year naked; she could see no beauty, even, in straight agile limbs which were meant to be encased in reach-me-down suits or cheap costumes of cotton velveteen. Thus her naked little ragamuffins﻿—those of them that lived﻿—were apt to be dirtier, less cared-for, than the run of the dirty village youngsters. Theodore, in whom the instinct of fatherhood was strong, was sometimes roused to wrath by her stupid mishandling of her children; but, on the whole he was patient with her﻿—knowing it useless to be otherwise. He beat her as seldom as possible and she was looked on by her neighbours as a woman kindly handled and unduly blessed in her husband. To the end she remained what she had always been; essentially a parasite, a minor product of civilization, machine-bred and crowd-developed﻿—bewildered by a life not lived in crowds and not subject to the laws of the Machine. To the end all nature was alien and hateful to her﻿—raw life that she turned from with disgust.﻿ ﻿… In her last illness her mind, when it wandered, strayed back into the world where she belonged; Theodore, an hour before she died, heard her muttering of “last Bank ’Oliday.”

===Chinese===
假如命运实现了她青春的梦想，让她在一间摆着竹制家具的破旧小别墅里度过婚姻生活，她或许能成为一个尚可的母亲；她至少会为孩子的相貌感到自豪，会有兴趣给他们穿衣打扮，就像她打扮自己一样，并用缎带系住他们的头发。以她的本性，她不可能为那些大半年赤身裸体的小淘气鬼感到自豪；她甚至看不出那本应套在现成衣服或廉价棉绒布服装里的敏捷修长的四肢有何美感。因此，她那些赤身裸体的小淘气鬼——那些活下来的孩子——往往比村里其他脏兮兮的孩子更脏、更无人照料。西奥多，这个父性本能强烈的男人，有时会被她愚蠢地对待孩子的方式激怒；但总的来说，他对她保持着耐心——知道发脾气也无济于事。他尽可能少地打她，在邻居们看来，她是个被丈夫善待、过分有福气的女人。直到最后，她始终是她一直以来的样子：本质上是一个寄生虫，一个文明的次要产物，机器培育、群体发展——对一种非群体生活、不受机器法则约束的生活感到困惑。直到最后，整个自然对她而言都是异己且可憎的——那种她厌恶地转身离开的原始生活。……在她最后的病中，当她神志不清时，她的思绪回到了她所属的那个世界；在她死前一小时，西奥多听到她喃喃自语着“上个银行假日（last Bank ’Oliday）”。

===Original===
She died at the end of a long hard winter during which she had failed and complained unceasingly, sat huddled to the fire and grown weaker; creeping, at last, to her straw in the corner and forgetting, in delirium, the meaningless life she had shared with her husband and children. Death smoothed out the lines in her sullen face; it was peaceful, almost comely, when Theodore looked his last on it﻿—and wondered, oddly, if among the “many mansions,” were some Cockney paradise of noise and jostle where his wife had found her heart’s desire?

===Chinese===
她在一个漫长而严酷的冬天的尽头死去，整个冬天她都在不断衰弱和抱怨，蜷缩在火堆旁，变得越来越虚弱；最后，她爬到角落里的草铺上，在谵妄中忘却了与丈夫和孩子共享的那段无意义的生活。死亡抚平了她阴沉脸庞上的皱纹；当西奥多最后一次凝视它时，那张脸是平静的，几乎是端庄的——他奇怪地想，在那“许多住处”中，是否有一个喧闹拥挤的伦敦东区天堂，他的妻子在那儿找到了她的心之所向？

===Original===
Of the four or five children she had brought into the world but two were living on the day of her death, her eldest-born and a youngster at the crawling stage; but the care of even two children was a burdensome matter for a man unaided, and it was esteemed natural and no insult to the dead, that Theodore should take another wife as speedily as might be﻿—in the course not of months but of weeks. He found a woman to suit his needs without going further than his own tribe; a woman left widowed a year or two before, who was glad enough to accept the offer of a better living than she could hope to make by her own scratching of a rod or two of earth and the uncertain charity of neighbours. The proposal of marriage, made in stolid fashion, was accepted as a matter of course﻿ ﻿… and, that night, Theodore stared through the fire into a room in Westminster where a girl in a yellow dress made music﻿ ﻿… and a young man listened from the corner of a sofa with a cigarette, unlit, between his fingers. He was dreaming at a table﻿—with silver and branching yellow roses﻿—when his son nudged him that supper was ready, and he dipped his hand into a greasy bowl for the meat.

===Chinese===
她带到世上的四五个孩子，在她死的那天只有两个还活着，她的长子和一个尚在爬行阶段的幼儿；但即使照顾两个孩子，对一个孤身男人来说也是沉重的负担，而且人们认为西奥多尽快再娶是自然的事，并非对死者的不敬——不是在几个月内，而是在几周内。他在自己的部落里就找到了一个适合他需求的女人；一个一两年前守寡的女人，她非常乐意接受一个比她自己靠耕种一两 rod 土地和邻居不确定的施舍所能维持的更好的生活。他以一种木讷的方式提出了求婚，对方理所当然地接受了……那天晚上，西奥多凝视着火焰，目光仿佛穿透火焰，看到了威斯敏斯特的一个房间，一个穿着黄色裙子的女孩在演奏音乐……一个年轻人坐在沙发角落里听着，手指间夹着一支未点燃的香烟。他正坐在桌旁做梦——桌上摆着银器和盛开的黄玫瑰——这时他的儿子推了推他，说晚饭准备好了，他便把手伸进一个油腻的碗里抓肉。

===Original===
The wedding followed swiftly on the heels of betrothal, and was celebrated in the manner already compulsory and established; by a public promise made solemnly before the headman, by a clasping of hands and a ceremony of religious blessing. This last was moulded, like all tribal ceremonies, on remembered formulae and ritual; and the tradition that a wedding should be accompanied by much eating and general merrymaking was also faithfully observed.

===Chinese===
婚礼紧随订婚之后迅速举行，按照已经强制实行并确立的方式进行；在酋长面前庄严地公开宣誓，紧握双手，并举行宗教祝福仪式。这最后一项仪式，如同所有部落仪式一样，是根据记忆中的公式和仪轨塑造的；婚礼应伴随大量饮食和普遍欢庆的传统也得到了忠实的遵循。

===Original===
The new wife, if not over comely or intelligent, was a sturdy young woman who had been broken to the duties required of her, and Theodore’s home, under its second mistress, was better tended and more comfortable than in the days of her sluttish predecessor. He had married her simply as a matter of business, that she might help in his fieldwork, cook his food, look after his children and satisfy his animal desire; and on the whole he had no reason to complain of the bargain he had made. She was a younger woman than Ada by some years﻿—had been only a slip of a girl at the time of the Ruin﻿—and, because of her youth, had adapted herself more readily than most of her elders to a world in the making and untraditioned methods of living. Her husband found life easier for the help of a pair of sturdy arms and pleasanter for lack of Ada’s grumbling.﻿ ﻿… She brought more than herself to Theodore’s household﻿—a child by her first husband; and, as time went on, she bore him other children of his own.

===Chinese===
新妻子虽然不算特别漂亮或聪明，却是个健壮的年轻女子，已习惯于被要求履行的职责，在她的主持下，西奥多的家比她那位邋遢的前任掌管时更整洁、更舒适。他娶她纯粹是出于实用考虑，好让她帮忙干农活、做饭、照顾孩子并满足他的生理需求；总的来说，他没有理由抱怨这笔交易。她比艾达年轻几岁——在毁灭发生时还只是个黄毛丫头——而且，由于年轻，她比大多数长辈更能适应这个正在形成中的世界和没有传统的生活方式。她的丈夫发现，有一双健壮的臂膀帮忙，生活轻松多了，没有了艾达的唠叨，日子也愉快多了。……她带给西奥多家的不只是她自己——还有她与前夫所生的一个孩子；而且，随着时间的推移，她为他生下了属于他自己的孩子。

===Original===

===Chinese===

## XXIII / 第二十三章

===Original===
As the years went by and his children grew to manhood in the world primitive which was the only world they knew, the life of Theodore Savage became definitely twofold; a life of the body in the present and a life of the mind in the past. There was his outward, rustic and daily self, the labourer, hunter and fisherman, who begat sons and daughters, who trudged home at nightfall to eat and sleep heavily, who occasionally cudgelled his wife: a sweating, muscular animal man whose existence was bounded by his bodily needs and the bodily needs of his children; who fondled his children and cuffed them by turns, as the beast cuffs and fondles its offspring. Whose world was the world of a food-patch enclosed in a valley, of a river where he fished, a wood where he snared and a hut that received him at evening.﻿ … In time it was of these things, and these things only, that he spoke to his kin and his neighbours; the weather, the luck of his hunting or fishing, the loves, births and deaths of his fellows. With the rise and growth of a generation that knew only the world primitive, the little community lived more in the present and less in the past; mention of the world that had vanished was even less frequent and even more furtive than before.

And even if that had not been the case, there was no man in the tribe, save Theodore, whose mind was the mind of a student; thus his other life, his life of the past, was lived to himself alone. It was a vivid memory-life in which he delved, turning over its vanished treasures—the intangible treasures of dead beauty, dead literature, learning and art; a life that at times receded to a dream of the impossible and at others was so real and overwhelming in its nearness that the everyday sweating and toiling and lusting grew vague and misty—was a veil drawn over reality.

Sometimes the two lives clashed suddenly and oddly—to the wonder of those who saw him. As on the day when his wife had burned the evening mess and, raising his hand to chastise her carelessness, there flashed before his eyes, without warning, a vision of Phillida bent delicately over her piano.﻿ … Not only Phillida, but the room, her surroundings; every detail clear to him and the loveliness of Chopin in his ears.﻿ … Furniture, hangings, a Louis Seize clock and a Hogarth print—and swiftly-seen objects whose very names he had forgotten, so long was it since he had made use of the household words that once described them. The dead world caught him back to itself and claimed him; in the face of its reality the present faded, the burned stew mattered not and his hand dropped slack to his side; while his wife's mouth, open for a wailing protest, hung open in gratified astonishment. He stared through the open door of the hut, not seeing the tufted trees beyond it or the curving skyline of the hills; then, taking mechanically his stout wooden spoon, he shovelled down his portion without tasting it. In his ears, like a song, was the varied speech of other days; of art, of daily mechanics, of books, of daily politics, of learning.﻿ … Phillida, her curved hands touching the keys, gave place to the eager, bespectacled face of a scholar who had tried to make clear to him the rhythm and beauty of French verse. He had forgotten the man's name—long forgotten it—but from some odd crevice in his brain a voice came echoing down the years, caressing the lines as it quoted them:—

> O Corse à cheveux plats, que la France était belle
 
  Au soleil de Messidor!

His own lips framed the words involuntarily, attempting the accent long unheard. "*Au soleil de Messidor, au soleil de Messidor*"﻿ … and his wife and children stared after him as, thrusting the half-eaten bowl aside, he rose and went out, muttering gibberish. They were not unused to these fits in the housefather, to the change in his eyes, the sudden forgetting of their presence; but never lost their fear of them as something uncanny and inexplicable.

===Chinese===
岁月流逝，他的子女们在那个原始世界中长大成人——那是他们所知的唯一世界——西奥多·萨维奇（Theodore Savage）的生命日渐分明地一分为二：肉体活在当下，精神活在过去。他有他外在的、乡野的、日复一日的自我——劳动者、猎人和渔夫，生儿育女，暮色中跋涉回家大口吃饭、沉沉入睡，偶尔还会殴打妻子：一个汗流浃背、筋肉虬结的动物般的人，其存在被他自身和儿女们的肉体需要所框定；他时而抚弄孩子，时而掌掴他们，如同野兽对待幼崽那般又打又疼。他的世界不过是山谷中一块被围起来的菜地，一条他捕鱼的河流，一片他下套的树林，以及傍晚迎接他归来的茅屋。……日久天长，他跟亲族和邻居交谈的就只有这些事——天气、打猎或捕鱼的运气、同伴们的恋爱、出生和死亡。随着一个只知原始世界的新世代的成长，这个小小的社群越来越多地活在当下，越来越少地提及过去；那个已消逝的世界甚至比从前更少被提起，提起时也更加鬼鬼祟祟。

即便不是这样，部落里除西奥多之外也没有哪个人拥有学者的头脑；因此他的另一种生活——他关于过去的生活——只能独自品味。那是一段鲜活的记忆生活，他在其中翻寻，反复咀嚼那些已然消逝的宝藏——死去的美、死去的文学、学问与艺术的无形珍宝；这种生活有时退隐为一场不可能之梦，有时又真切得令人窒息、压迫感十足，以致日常的劳作、流汗和欲望都变得模糊朦胧——仿佛一层薄纱遮蔽了现实。

两种生活有时会突然而怪异地碰撞——令旁观者惊讶不已。比如有一天，妻子把晚饭烧糊了，他抬起手正要惩罚她的粗心，眼前却猝不及防地闪过一个画面：菲莉达（Phillida）俯身在钢琴前，姿态优雅。……不单是菲莉达，还有那个房间、她周围的陈设；每一个细节都清晰如昨，肖邦的乐曲在耳畔流淌。……家具、帷幔、一座路易十六风格的座钟和一幅霍加斯的版画——还有那些一闪而过却连名字都想不起的物件，他已经太久没有用过那些曾经用来描述它们的日常词汇了。死去的世界将他一把拽了回去，据为己有；面对它的真实，当下褪色了，烧糊的炖菜无关紧要，他的手无力地垂到身侧；而他妻子张着嘴正要号哭抗议，却愣在那里，惊讶中带着几分满意。他透过敞开的茅屋门凝视远方，没有看见门外蓬松的树丛，也没有看见山丘蜿蜒的天际线；然后他机械地拿起那把结实的木勺，把属于自己的那份食物铲进嘴里，却毫无滋味。耳畔如同一首歌，回响着往日纷繁的声音——艺术、日常器物、书籍、日常政治、学问。……菲莉达弯起的双手触碰琴键的画面让位给了另一个急切的、戴眼镜的学者的面孔——那位曾试图向他阐释法语诗歌的韵律与之美的人。他已经忘了那个人的名字——早已忘了——但从大脑某个隐秘的角落里，一个声音穿越岁月回荡而来，用抚摩般的语调吟诵着那几行诗：

> O Corse à cheveux plats, que la France était belle
 
  Au soleil de Messidor!

他的嘴唇不由自主地翕动着，试图发出那个久已不闻的口音。"Au soleil de Messidor, au soleil de Messidor"（梅西多尔的阳光下，梅西多尔的阳光下）……他把吃了一半的碗推到一边，站起身走出门去，嘴里嘟囔着谁也听不懂的话，妻子和孩子们呆望着他的背影。他们对这一家之主的这类发作早已不陌生——他眼神的骤变，对他们在场的突然无视；但他们始终无法消除对此的恐惧，觉得那是某种不可名状、无法解释的怪事。

===Original===
With these masterful rushes of the past came often an infinite melancholy; which was not so much a regret for what had been as a sense of the pity of oblivion. So that he would lie outstretched with his face to the earth, rebellious at the thought that with him and a few of his own generation must pass all knowledge of human achievement, the very memory of that which had once been glorious.﻿ … Not only the memory of actual men whose fame had once been blown about the world; but the memory of sound, of music, and of marvels in stone, uplifted by the skill of generations; the memory of systems, customs, laws, wrought wisely by the hand of experience; and of fanciful people, more real than living men and women. With him and his like would pass not only Leonardo, Caesar and the sun of Messidor, but Rosalind, d'Artagnan and Faust; the heroes, the merrymen, the women loved and loving who, created of dreams, had shared the dead world with their fellows created of dust.﻿ … Once deemed immortal, they had been slain by science as surely as their fellows of dust.

At times he pondered vaguely whether he might not save the memory of some of them alive by teaching his children to love them; but in the end he realized that, as we grasp nothing save through ourselves and our own relation to it, the embodied desires and beauty of an inconceivable age would be meaningless to his young barbarians.

===Chinese===
伴随这股势不可挡的对往昔的奔涌追忆而来的，往往是一种无尽的忧郁；与其说那是对曾经存在过的事物的惋惜，不如说是对被遗忘本身的悲悯。于是他会趴在地上，脸贴着泥土，心中涌起反叛——想到他和少数同代人一旦逝去，人类成就的全部知识也将随之湮灭，那些曾经辉煌的一切的记忆都将不复存在。……不仅是那些曾经声名远播的真实人物的记忆；还有声音、音乐的记忆，世代工匠的技艺所筑起的石砌奇迹的记忆；由经验之手明智地锻造出的制度、习俗与法律的记忆；还有那些虚构的人物——比活着的男女更加真实。与他和他那一代人一同消逝的，不仅是达·芬奇、凯撒和梅西多尔的太阳，还有罗莎琳、达达尼昂和浮士德；那些英雄、那些欢乐之人、那些被爱和爱着的女性——他们由梦想创造，与那些由尘土创造的同伴共享着那个死去的世界。……他们曾被视为不朽，但正如那些尘土同伴一样，他们也被科学确定无疑地杀死了。

有时他会隐约思索，是否可以通过教孩子们去爱他们来挽救其中某些人的记忆；但他终究意识到，我们只能通过自身以及与事物的关系来理解一切，一个不可想象的时代所凝聚的欲望与美，对于他那些年轻的蛮族儿女来说将毫无意义。

===Original===
If he ceased to believe in the survival of life as he had known it and a civilization that would reach out and claim him, there were times when he believed, or almost believed, that somewhere in the vastness of the great round world a remnant must hold fast to its inheritance; when it was inconceivable that all men living could be sunk in brutishness or vowed to the creed of utter ignorance. Hunger and blind terror—he knew, for he had seen it—could reduce the highest to the level of the beast; but with the passing of terror and the satisfaction of the actual needs of the body, there awakens the hunger of the mind. Somewhere in the vastness of the great, round world must be those who, because they craved for more than full stomachs and daily security, still clung to the power which is knowledge. Little groups and companies that chance had brought together or good fortune saved from destruction; resourceful men who had striven with surrounding anarchy and worsted it, and, having worsted it, were building their civilization.﻿ … And in the very completeness of surrounding anarchy, the very depth of surrounding brutishness, would lie their opportunity and chance of supremacy, their power of enforcing their will.

If such groups, such future nations, existed, he asked himself how they would build? What manner of world they would strive for—knowing what they knew?﻿ … This, at least, was certain: it would not be the world of their fathers, of their own youth. They had seen their civilization laid waste by the agency of science combined with human passion; hence, if they rejected the alternative of ignorance and held to their perilous treasure of science, their problem was the mastery of passion.

He came to believe that the problem—like all others—had been faced in forgotten generations; that old centuries had learned the forgotten lesson that the Ruin was teaching anew. To a race that had realized the peril of knowledge there would be two alternatives only; renunciation—the creed of blind ignorance and savagery—or the guarding of science as a secret treasure, removed from all contact with the flame that is human emotion. There had been elder and long-past civilizations in which knowledge was a mystery, the possession and the privilege of a caste; tradition had come down to us of ancient wisdom which might only be revealed to the initiate.﻿ … A blind fear massacred its scientific men, a wiser fear exalted them and set them apart as initiates. When science and human emotion between them had wrought the extreme of destruction and agony, there passed the reckless and idealistic dream of a world where all might be enlightened; the aim and tradition of a social system arising out of ruin would be the setting of an iron barrier between science and human emotion. That, and not enlightenment of all and sundry—the admission of the foolish, the impulsive and the selfish to a share in the power of destruction. The same need and instinct of self-preservation which had inspired the taking of the Vow of Ignorance would work, in higher and saner minds, for the training of a caste—an Egyptian priesthood—exempt from blind passion and the common impulse of the herd; a caste trained in silence and rigid self-control, its way of attainment made hard to the student, the initiate. The deadly formulae of mechanics, electricity and chemistry would be entrusted only to those who had been purged of the daily common passions of the multitude; to those who, by trial after trial, had fettered their natural impulses and stripped themselves of instinct and desire.

So, in times past, had arisen—and might again arise—a scientific priesthood whose initiates, to the vulgar, were magicians; a caste that guarded science as a mystery and confined the knowledge which is power of destruction to those who had been trained not to use it. The old lost learning of dead and gone kingdoms was a science shielded by its devotees from defilement by human emotion; a pure, cold knowledge, set apart and worshipped for itself.﻿ … And somewhere in the vastness of the great round world the beginnings of a priesthood, a scientific caste, might be building unconsciously on the lines of ancient wisdom, and laying the foundations of yet another Egypt or Chaldea. A State whose growth would be rooted in the mystery of knowledge and fear of human passion; whose culture and civilization would be moulded by a living and terrible tradition of catastrophe through science uncontrolled.﻿ … And, so long as the tradition was living and terrible, the initiate would stand guard before his mysteries, that the world might be saved from itself; only when humanity had forgotten its downfall and ruin had ceased to be even a legend, would the barrier between science and emotion be withdrawn and knowledge be claimed as the right of the uncontrolled, the multitude.

===Chinese===
即便他不再相信他所知晓的那种生命的存续，不再相信会有某种文明伸出手来认领他，有些时候他还是相信——或几乎相信——在茫茫大圆世界的某个角落，必定有一群人紧守着他们的遗产；他无法想象所有活着的人都沉沦于兽性，或宣誓效忠于彻底无知的信条。饥饿和盲目的恐惧——他知道，因为他亲眼见过——能把最高贵的人贬低到野兽的水平；但随着恐惧的消退和身体实际需求的满足，心灵的饥渴便会苏醒。在广袤大圆世界的某个角落，必定有人因为渴望比饱腹和日常安全更多的东西，仍然紧握着那名为知识的力量。一些偶然聚集在一起的小团体，一些被好运从毁灭中拯救出来的人；足智多谋之士与周围的混乱搏斗并战胜了它，战胜之后便在建设自己的文明。……恰恰是在周围混乱的彻底之中，在周围野蛮的深渊之中，蕴藏着他们凌驾一切的机会和可能，以及他们强制推行自身意志的力量。

如果这样的群体、这样的未来国度确实存在，他问自己——它们会怎样建设？以它们所知的一切，它们会努力追求一个怎样的世界？……至少有一点是确定的：那不会是它们父辈的世界，不会是它们自己年轻时的世界。它们亲眼目睹自己的文明被科学与人类激情的合谋所摧毁；因此，如果它们拒绝无知这一替代方案，固守那危险的科学宝藏，那么它们的课题就是驾驭激情。

他逐渐相信，这个问题——如同所有其他问题一样——在被遗忘的世代中早已被面对过；古老的世纪已经学到了那场毁灭正在重新教授的被遗忘的教训。对于一个已经意识到知识之险的种族来说，只有两个选择：放弃——即盲目的无知与野蛮的信条——或将科学作为秘密宝藏来守护，使之远离人类情感的火焰。在久远消逝的古代文明中，知识曾是一种奥秘，是某一阶层的专属与特权；我们从传统中得知，某些古老的智慧只能向入门者揭示。……盲目的恐惧屠杀了它的科学家，更明智的恐惧则提升了他们，将他们区隔为入门者。当科学与人类情感联手造成了极致的毁灭与痛苦之后，那个不计后果的、关于人人皆可启蒙的理想主义梦想便烟消云散了；从废墟中崛起的社会制度，其目标和传统将是为科学与人类情感之间竖起一道铁壁。那才是真正的目标——而非让所有庸众启蒙——让愚蠢者、冲动者和自私者也分得一份破坏的力量。同样是激发了"无知誓言"的自我保存需求，在更高、更清醒的头脑中，将推动培养出一个阶层——一个埃及祭司般的阶层——免于盲目的激情和群体的共同冲动；一个在沉默和严格自制中被训练出来的阶层，其修习之路对求学者和入门者都充满艰辛。机械、电力和化学的致命公式，只会托付给那些已经从庸众的日常激情中被净化的人；那些通过一次又一次考验，束缚了自己的天然冲动、剥除了本能与欲望的人。

在过去的时代，曾兴起——并可能再次兴起——一个科学祭司阶层，其入门者在俗众眼中就是魔法师；一个将科学作为奥秘来守护的阶层，将那具有毁灭力量的知识限制在那些被训练不去使用它的人手中。那些逝去王国的古老失落学问，是一门被其信奉者保护、免遭人类情感玷污的科学；一门纯粹而冰冷的知识，被分离出来，因其自身而受到崇拜。……在广袤大圆世界的某个角落，一个祭司阶层、一个科学种姓的雏形，可能正沿着古代智慧的路线无意识地建造着，为又一个埃及或迦勒底奠定基础。一个其成长将扎根于知识的奥秘和对人类情感之恐惧的国家；一个其文化和文明将被一种关于失控科学所致灾难的活生生的、可怕的传统所塑造的国家。……只要这传统仍然鲜活而可怕，入门者就会守护在他的奥秘之前，使世界免于自我毁灭；只有当人类遗忘了自己的堕落，毁灭甚至不再是一段传说时，科学与情感之间的屏障才会被撤除，知识才会被宣布为无节制者——即大众——的权利。

===Original===
Till his brain began to fail him he watched, in dumb interest, the life and development of the tribe; learning from it more than he had ever known in the world of his youth of the eternal foundations on which life in community is built. The unending struggle between the desire for freedom, which makes of man a rebel, and the need for security, which makes of him a citizen, was played before his understanding eyes; he watched parties, castes and priesthoods in the making and, before he died, could forecast the beginning of an aristocracy, a slave class and a tribal hereditary monarchy. In all things man untraditioned held blindly to the ways he had forgotten; instinctively, not knowing whither they led, he trod the paths that his fathers had trodden before him.

Most of all he was stirred in his interest and pity by the life religious of the world around him; watching it adapt itself, steadily and naturally, to the needs of a race in its childhood. As a new generation grew up to its heritage of ignorance, the foundations of faith were shifted; as tribal life crystallized, gods multiplied inevitably and the Heaven ruled by a Supreme Being gave place to a crude Valhalla of minor deities. Man, who makes God in his own image, can only make that image in the likeness of his own highest type; which, in a world divided, insecure and predatory, is the type of the successful warrior; the Saviour, in a world divided and predatory, takes the form of a tribal deity who secures to his people the enjoyment of their fields by strengthening their hands against the assaults and the malice of their enemies. As always with those who live in constant fear and in hate of one another, the Lord was a Man of War; and when Theodore's first grandson was received into the tribe, the deity to whom vows were made in the name of the child was already a local Jehovah. Faith saw him as a tribal Lord of Hosts, the celestial captain of his worshippers; if his worshippers walked humbly and paid due honour to his name he would stand before them in the day of battle and protect them with his shield invisible—would draw the sword of the Lord and of Gideon, show himself mightier than the priests of Baal and overthrow the altars of the Philistines.

A god whose attributes are those of a warrior, of necessity is not omnipotent; since he fights, his authority is partial—assailed and disputed by those against whom he draws the sword. A race in its childhood evolved the deity it needed, a champion and upholder of his own people; to the tribal warrior the god to whom an enemy prayed for success was a rival of his own protector.﻿ … So the mind primitive argued, more or less directly and consciously, making God in its image, for its own needs and purposes; and even in Theodore's lifetime the deities worshipped by men from a distance were not those of his own country. The jurisdiction of the gods was limited and the stranger, of necessity, paid homage to an alien spirit who took pleasure in an unfamiliar ritual.

===Chinese===
在他大脑开始衰退之前，他以沉默的兴趣观察着部落的生活和发展；从中他了解到比年轻时所知的更多的东西——关于社群生活所赖以建立的永恒基础。自由的渴望使人成为叛逆者，安全的需求使人成为公民，两者之间的永恒斗争在他洞察一切的眼前上演；他看着党派、阶层和祭司制度的形成，在他去世之前，他已能预见一个贵族阶层、一个奴隶阶层和一个部落世袭君主制的开端。在一切事情上，没有传统的人都盲目地固守着他已经遗忘的方式；本能地，不知通向何方，他踏上了祖辈曾走过的路。

最令他感兴趣和动情的，是周围世界中的宗教生活；他看着它稳定而自然地适应一个处于童年阶段的种族的需要。随着新一代人在无知的遗产中成长，信仰的基础发生了转变；随着部落生活的结晶化，众神不可避免地增多，由至高存在统治的天堂让位给了一个粗糙的瓦尔哈拉（Valhalla），里面供奉着各路次等神灵。人按照自己的形象造神，只能按照自身最高类型的样貌来塑造那个形象；在一个分裂、不安全和弱肉强食的世界里，这最高类型就是成功的战士；在一个分裂和掠夺的世界里，救世主采取了部落神灵的形态，通过增强信徒的力量来抵御敌人的攻击和恶意，从而保护他的人民享有自己的土地。正如那些生活在持续恐惧和相互仇恨中的人一贯如此，主是一位战争之神；当西奥多的第一个孙子被接纳进部落时，以孩子之名宣誓效忠的神灵已经是一个地方性的耶和华了。信仰将他视为部落的万军之主，信徒的天军统帅；如果信徒们谦卑行走并给予他应得的尊荣，他就会在战斗的日子里站在他们面前，用看不见的盾牌保护他们——就会拔出耶和华和基甸的剑，向巴力的祭司显示自己更为强大，推翻非利士人的祭坛。

一个以战士为属性的神，必然不是全能的；既然他在战斗，他的权威就是有限的——被他拔剑指向的人所攻击和质疑。一个处于童年阶段的种族进化出了它所需要的神，一个为本族人而战的勇士和捍卫者；对于部落战士而言，敌人向之祈求胜利的神就是自己保护者的对手。……原始思维就是这样或多或少直接而自觉地进行论证的——按照自身的形象造神，为满足自身的需求和目的；甚至在西奥多的一生中，远方民族所崇拜的神灵也不同于他自己国家的神灵。神灵的管辖权是有限的，外邦人必然要向一个陌生的神灵致敬——这个神灵在陌生的仪式中取乐。

===Original===
In his lifetime the darkness of Heaven was unbroken and there emerged no god whose attribute was mercy and long-suffering; the Day of Judgment was still too recent, its memory too clear and overwhelming, to admit of the idea of a Divine Love or a Father who had pity on his children. Fear, and fear only, led his people to the feet of the Lord. The God of Vengeance of the first generation and the tribal superman who gradually ousted him from his pride of place were alike wrathful, jealous of their despotism and greedily expectant of mouth-honour. Hence, propitiation and ignorance were the whole religious duty of man, and the rites wherewith deity was duly worshipped were rites of crawling flattery and sacrifice.﻿ … The blood of sinners was acceptable in the sight of Heaven; the Lord Almighty had destroyed a world that he might slake his vengeance, and his lineal descendants, the celestial warriors, rejoiced in the slaughter of those who had borne arms against their worshippers—in the end, rejoiced in blood for itself and the savour of the burnt sacrifice. And a race cowed spiritually (lest worse befall it) evolved its rites of sacrificial cruelty, paying tribute to a god who took ceaseless pleasure in the humbling of his people and could only be appeased by their suffering.

There were seasons and regions where abasement produced its own reaction; when, for all the savour of sacrificial cruelty, the gods remained deaf to the prayers of their worshippers, delivered them into the hands of their enemies or chastened them with famine and pestilence. Hope of salvation beaten out of them, the worshippers, like rats driven into a corner, ceased to grovel and turned on the tyrants who had failed them; and the Lord Almighty Who made the heavens, shrunk to the dimensions of a local fetish, was upbraided and beaten in effigy.

Since it seemed that the new world must in all things follow in the ways of the old, the gentler deities who delighted not in blood would in due time reveal themselves to man grown capable of mercy. As the memory of judgment faded with the centuries—as the earth waxed fruitful and life was kindlier—humanity would dare to lift its head from the dust and the life religious would be more than blind cringing to a despot. The Heaven of the future would find room for gods who were gracious and friendly; for white Baldurs and Olympians who walk with men and instruct them; and there would arise prophets whose message was not vengeance, but a call to "rejoice in the Lord."﻿ … And in further time, it might be, the God who is a Spirit﻿﻿ … and a Christ.﻿﻿ … The rise, the long, slow upward struggle of the soul of man was as destined and inevitable as its fall; all human achievement, material or spiritual, was founded in the baseness of mire and clay—and rose towering above its foundations. As the State, which had its origin in no more than common fear and hatred, in the end would be honoured without thought of gain and its flag held sacred by its sons; so Deity, beginning as vengeance personified, would advance to a spiritual Law and a spiritual Love. When the power of loving returned to the race, it would cease to abase itself and lift up its eyes to a Father—endowing its Deity with that which was best in itself; when it achieved and took pleasure in its own thoughts and the works of its hands, it would see in the Highest not the Vengeance that destroys but the Spirit that heals and creates.

===Chinese===
在他的一生中，天堂的黑暗从未被打破，没有出现过以慈悲和忍耐为属性的神；审判日仍然记忆犹新，记忆过于清晰而令人窒息，无法容纳神圣之爱或怜悯众生的天父的概念。唯有恐惧——仅仅是恐惧——引领他的人民走向主的脚下。第一代人的复仇之神，以及逐渐从至高宝座上将他赶走的部落超人，都是愤怒的、嫉妒他们的专制、贪婪地渴求口头赞美的。因此，安抚和无知便是人全部的宗教义务，而用以恰当地敬拜神灵的仪式，就是卑躬屈膝的奉承和献祭的仪式。……罪人的血在天堂眼中是可接受的；全能的主摧毁了一个世界来平息他的复仇，而他的直系后裔——天上的战士们——则为那些向信徒举刀之人的杀戮而欢欣；最终，为血本身和燔祭的气味而欢欣。一个精神上被震慑的种族（唯恐更糟的事降临）发展出了残酷的献祭仪式，向一个以贬低其人民为乐、只能通过他们的痛苦才能安抚的神灵进贡。

有些季节和地区，卑躬屈膝会引发自身的反弹；当尽管有献祭的残酷，神灵仍然对信徒的祈祷充耳不闻，将他们交到敌人手中，或以饥荒和瘟疫来惩罚他们时，得救的希望被彻底击碎，信徒们就像被逼到角落里的老鼠，不再匍匐，转而反抗那些让他们失望的暴君；而那创造天穹的全能之主，缩成了一个地方神像的大小，在人像中被责骂和殴打。

既然新世界似乎必须在一切事物上追随旧世界的道路，那些不以血为乐的温和神灵，终将在适当的时候向有能力怜悯的人类显现自己。随着审判的记忆随世纪消逝——随着大地变得丰饶、生活变得更加仁慈——人类将敢于从尘土中抬起头来，而宗教生活将不再是对专制者的盲目匍匐。未来的天堂将为那些仁慈友好的神灵留出空间；为那些与人同行、教导人类的白皙巴尔德（Baldur）和奥林匹亚诸神留出空间；将会出现先知，他们的讯息不是复仇，而是呼唤"在主里欢喜"。……更久以后，也许会出现那位作为灵的上帝……和一位基督。……人类灵魂的上升——那漫长而缓慢的向上奋斗——如同其堕落一样是命中注定且不可避免的；所有人类的成就，无论是物质的还是精神的，都建立在泥沼和尘土的卑劣之上——并高耸于其基础之上。正如国家最初不过源于普通的恐惧和仇恨，最终却会受到无功利的尊崇，其旗帜被子孙奉为神圣；同样，神灵最初是复仇的人格化，最终将发展为一种精神法则和精神之爱。当爱的能力回归种族时，它将不再贬低自己，而是仰望天父——将其自身最美好的品质赋予它的神灵；当它实现并享受自身的思想和双手所创造的作品时，它将在至高者身上看到的不是毁灭的复仇，而是治愈和创造的精神。

===Original===
Meanwhile the foundation of the life religious was, and must be, the timorous virtue of ignorance, of humble avoidance of inquiry into the dreadful secrets of God. In Theodore's youth he had turned from the orthodox religions, which repelled by what seemed to him a fear of knowledge and inquiry; now he understood that man, being by nature destructive, can survive only when his powers of destruction are limited; and that the ignorance enjoined by priest and bigot had been—and would be again—an essential need of the race, an expression of the will to live.﻿ … The jealous God who guards his secrets is the god of the race that survives.

How many times—(he would wonder)—how many times since the world began to spin has man, in his eager search for truth, rushed blindly through knowledge to the ruin that means chaos and savagery? How many times, in his devout, instinctive longing to know his own nature and the workings of the Infinite Mind that created him has he wrought himself weapons that turned to his own destruction?﻿ … Ignorance of the powers and forces of nature is a condition of human existence; as necessary to the continued life of the race as the breathing of air or the taking of food into the body. Behind the bench of zealots who judged Galileo lay the dumb race-memory of ruin—ruin, perhaps, many times repeated. They stood, the zealots, for that ignorance which, being interpreted, is life; and Galileo for that knowledge which, being interpreted, is death.﻿ …

Many times, it might be, since the world began to spin, had men called upon the rocks to cover them from the devils their own hands had fashioned; many times, it might be, a remnant had put from it the knowledge it dared not trust itself to wield—that it might not fall upon its own weapons, but live, just live, like the beasts! Behind the injunction to devout ignorance, behind the ecclesiastical hatred of science and distrust of brain, lay more than prejudice and bigotry; the prejudice and bigotry were but superficial and outward workings of instinct and the first law of all, the Law of Self-Preservation.

With his eyes open to the workings of that law, folktale and myth had long become real to him—since he saw them daily in the making.﻿ … The dragon that wasted a country with its breath—how else should a race that knew naught of chemistry account for the devilry of gas? And he understood now, why the legend of Icarus was a legend of disaster, and Prometheus, who stole fire from Heaven, was chained to eternity for his daring; he knew, also, why the angel with a flaming sword barred the gate of Eden to those who had tasted of knowledge.﻿ … The story of the Garden, of the Fall of Man, was no more the legend of his youth; he read it now, with his opened eyes, as a livid and absolute fact. A fact told plainly as symbol could tell it by a race that had put from it all memory of the science whereby it was driven from its ancient paradise, its garden of civilization.﻿ … How many times since the world began to spin had man mastered the knowledge that should make him like unto God, and turned, in agony of mind and body, from a power synonymous with death?

And how many times more, he wondered—how many times more?

===Chinese===
与此同时，宗教生活的基础是，而且必然是，无知的怯懦美德，是对探究上帝可怕秘密的谦卑回避。在西奥多年轻时，他曾远离正统宗教，那些宗教因其对知识和探究的恐惧而令他反感；如今他明白了，人类天性具有破坏性，只有当其破坏力受到限制时才能生存；而祭司和偏执者所强制的无知，过去是——将来也必将再次是——种族的基本需要，是生存意志的表现。……那位守护秘密的嫉妒之神，正是幸存种族的神。

自世界开始旋转以来，有多少次——他常常思考——人类在热切追求真理的过程中，盲目地穿越知识走向那意味着混乱与野蛮的毁灭？有多少次，在虔诚地、本能地渴望认识自己的本性和创造他的无限心智的运作时，他为自己锻造了武器，却转而毁灭了自己？……对自然力量和能力的无知是人类生存的条件；就像呼吸空气和进食一样，对种族的延续生命不可或缺。审判伽利略的狂热者们身后，是沉默的种族记忆——关于毁灭的记忆，也许重复了许多次。那些狂热者代表着被诠释为生命的无知；而伽利略代表着被诠释为死亡的知识。……

自世界开始旋转以来，也许有许多次，人类呼唤岩石来覆盖他们，躲避他们亲手制造的恶魔；也许有许多次，一群残存者放弃了他们不敢信任自己掌握的知识——以免倒在自己的武器下，而是活着，就像野兽一样活着！在虔诚无知的告诫背后，在教会的科学仇恨和对理智的不信任背后，隐藏的不仅仅是偏见和偏执；那些偏见和偏执不过是本能——即一切法则之上的自保法则——的表层外在表现。

当他睁开眼睛看到这条法则的运作时，民间传说和神话对他来说早已变得真实——因为他每天都在目睹它们的诞生。……那用呼吸摧毁一个国家的龙——一个对化学一无所知的种族，还能如何解释毒气的恶魔般力量？他现在明白了，为什么伊卡洛斯的传说是灾难的传说，普罗米修斯因从天堂盗取火焰的胆大妄为而被锁在永恒中；他也明白了，为什么手持火焰之剑的天使禁止那些品尝过知识的人进入伊甸园。……花园的故事，人类堕落的故事，不再是青年时代的传说；他现在用睁开的双眼阅读它，将其视为一个鲜明而绝对的事实。一个已经完全遗忘了那将其从古老天堂、从文明花园中驱逐出去的科学的种族，通过象征所能讲述的赤裸事实。……自世界开始旋转以来，有多少次人类掌握了本应使他如神一般的知识，却在身心的痛苦中转身逃离那与死亡同义的力量？

还有多少次——他想知道——还有多少次？

===Original===
Theodore Savage lived to be a very old man; how old in years he could not have said, since, long before his memory failed him, he had lost his count of time. But for fully a decade before he died he went humped and rheumatic, leaning on a stick, was blear-eyed, toothless and wizened; he had outlived all those who had begun the new world with him, and a son of his grandson was of those who—when the time came—dug a trench for his bones and shovelled loose earth on his head.

He had no lack of care in his extreme old age—in part perhaps because the tribe grew to hold him in awe that increased with the years; the sole survivor of the legendary age that preceded the Ruin and Downfall of Man, he was feared in spite of his helplessness. He alone of his little community could remember the Ruin with any comprehension of its causes; he alone possessed in silence a share of that hidden and forbidden knowledge which had brought flaming judgment on the world. Here and there in the countryside were grey-headed men, his juniors by years, who could remember vaguely the horrors of a distant childhood—the sky afire, the crash of falling masonry, the panic, the lurking and the starving. These things they could remember like a nightmare past﻿﻿ … but only remember, not explain. Behind Theodore's bald forehead and dimmed, oozing eyes lay the understanding of why and wherefore denied to those who dwelt beside him.

For this reason Theodore Savage was treated with deference in the days of his senile helplessness. As he sat, half-blind, in the sun by the door of his hut, no one ever failed to greet him with respect in passing; while in most the greeting was more than a token of respect or kindliness—the sign and result of a nervous desire to propitiate. In the end he was credited with a knowledge of unholy arts, and the children of the tribe avoided and shrank from him, frightened by the gossip of their elders; so that village mothers found him useful as a bogey, arresting the tantrums of unruly brats by threats of calling in Old Bald-Head.

Even in his lifetime legends clustered thick about him, and sickness or accident to man or beast was ascribed to the glance of his purblind eye or the malice of his vacant brain; while there was once—though he never knew or suspected it—an agitated and furtive discussion as to whether, for the good of the community, he should not be knocked on the head. The furtive discussion ended in discussion only—not because the advocates of mercy were numerous, but because no man was willing to lay violent hands on a wizard, for fear of what might befall him; and, the interlude over, the tribe relapsed into its customary timid respect for its patriarch, its customary practice of ensuring his goodwill by politeness and small offerings of victuals. These added to the old man's comfort in his latter years—nor had he any suspicion of the motive that secured him both deference and dainties.

With his death the local legends increased and multiplied; the distorted, varied myths of the Ruin of Man and its causes showing an inevitable tendency to group themselves around one striking and mysterious figure, to make of that figure a cause and a personification of the Great Disaster. Theodore Savage, to those who came after, was Merlin, Frankenstein and Adam; the fool who tasted of forbidden fruit, the magician whose arts had brought ruin on a world, the devil-artisan whose unholy skill had created monsters that destroyed him. His grave was an awesome spot, apart from other graves, which the timorous avoided after dark; and, long after all trace of it had vanished, there clung to the neighbourhood a tradition of haunting and mystery.﻿﻿ … To his children's children his name was the symbol of a dead civilization; a civilization that had passed so completely from the ken of living man that its lost achievements, the manner of its ending, could only be expressed in symbol.

===Chinese===
西奥多·萨维奇活到了极高龄；具体多少岁他说不上来，因为早在记忆衰退之前，他就已经失去了计数时间的能力。但在去世前的整整十年里，他驼背而行，关节疼痛，拄着拐杖，老眼昏花，牙齿掉光，形容枯槁；他比所有那些与他一起开创新世界的人都活得更久，他孙子的一个儿子正是那个在——时候到来时——为他挖掘墓穴、将松土撒在他头上的人之一。

他在极老之年并不缺乏关怀——部分原因也许是部落对他日益增长的敬畏，与年俱增；作为那场大灾难和人类陨落之前传说时代唯一的幸存者，尽管他已经虚弱无力，仍然令人畏惧。在他那个小社群中，只有他能记忆大灾难并理解其原因；只有他默默地掌握着那部分曾给世界带来炽烈审判的隐秘而禁忌的知识。在乡间各地，有一些比他年轻的白发老人，能模糊地记起遥远童年的恐怖——天空燃烧、建筑倒塌的轰响、恐慌、潜伏和饥饿。这些事情他们能像一场过去的噩梦一样记起……但只是记起，无法解释。在西奥多秃顶的前额和浑浊的、流淌着液体的眼睛背后，埋藏着那些与他比邻而居的人所无法理解的因果。

正因为如此，西奥多·萨维奇在他衰老无助的日子里受到了恭敬的对待。当他半盲地坐在茅屋门口的阳光下时，路过的人无不恭敬地向他致意；而在大多数人那里，这种致意不仅仅是尊敬或善意的表示——而是一种紧张地想要安抚的欲望的标志和结果。最终，他被认为掌握着不洁的技艺，部落的孩子们躲避他、害怕他，被长辈的闲话所惊吓；于是村里的母亲们发现他很有用，可以用威胁叫"老秃头"来吓唬那些不听话的孩子，制止他们的暴躁脾气。

在他生前，传说就已密集地围绕着他，人畜的疾病或意外都被归咎于他那昏花老眼的一瞥或他那空虚头脑的恶意；甚至有一次——尽管他从未知道或怀疑过——有过一场焦虑而隐秘的讨论，是否为了社区的利益应该把他敲死。那场隐秘的讨论止于讨论而已——不是因为支持仁慈的人很多，而是因为没有人愿意对一个巫师施暴，唯恐灾祸降临到自己头上；而插曲过后，部落恢复了对族长惯常的怯懦尊敬，惯常地通过礼貌和少量食物供奉来确保他的善意。这些在老人的晚年增添了舒适——他也从未怀疑过给他敬意和美食背后的动机。

在他死后，地方传说有增无减；关于大灾难及其原因的各种扭曲、变异的神话，不可避免地倾向于围绕一个引人注目而神秘的人物，将那个形象塑造成大灾难的起因和化身。西奥多·萨维奇，对后来者而言，是梅林、弗兰肯斯坦和亚当；那个品尝禁果的愚人，那个用技艺给世界带来毁灭的魔法师，那个用不洁技艺创造了毁灭他的怪物的魔鬼工匠。他的坟墓是一个令人敬畏的地方，与其他坟墓隔开，胆怯者在天黑后都避开；而很久以后，当坟墓的所有痕迹都已消失时，附近仍萦绕着闹鬼和神秘的传统。……对他的子孙后代来说，他的名字是一个逝去文明的象征；一个已经完全从活人的视野中消失的文明，以至于它的失落成就、它的终结方式，只能通过象征来表达。
